# Rigveda Samhita — Volume 28 (Kannada → English translation)

### Printed p. 1

Om.

Śrī Gaṇādhipataye namaḥ.

वागीशाद्याः सुमनसः सर्वार्थानामुपक्रमे । यं नत्वा कृतकृत्याः सुस्तं नमामि गजाननं ॥ (*vāgīśādyāḥ sumanasaḥ sarvārthānāmupakrame | yaṃ natvā kṛtakṛtyāḥ sustaṃ namāmi gajānanaṃ ‖*)

यस्य निश्यसितं वेदा यो वेवेभ्योऽखिलं जगत् । निर्ममे तमहं वंदे विद्यातीर्थमहेश्वरं ॥ (*yasya niśyasitaṃ vedā yo vevebhyo 'khilaṃ jagat | nirmame tamahaṃ vaṃde vidyātīrthamaheśvaraṃ ‖*)

वेदः शिवः शिवो वेदो वेदाध्याया सदाशिवः । तस्मात्पर्वप्रयत्नेन वेदमेव सदा जपेत् ॥ (*vedaḥ śivaḥ śivo vedo vedādhyāyā sadāśivaḥ | tasmātparvaprayatnena vedameva sadā japet ‖*)

व्याख्याय निगमाभिज्ञः सप्तमं सप्तमाष्टके । अध्यायं सायणाचार्यः अष्टमं व्याकरोत्यथ ॥ (*vyākhyāya nigamābhijñaḥ saptamaṃ saptamāṣṭake | adhyāyaṃ sāyaṇācāryaḥ aṣṭamaṃ vyākarotyatha ‖*)

With Sāyaṇa's Bhāṣya

Ṛgveda Saṃhitā (with explanation in Kannada translation)

Part 28

The eighth adhyāya in the seventh aṣṭaka

The forty-first sūkta in the tenth maṇḍala

**Introduction (Jīḷike)**

In the twenty-seventh part of the Ṛgveda Saṃhitā the first forty sūktas of the tenth maṇḍala were completed. This twenty-eighth part begins with the forty-first sūkta; the first seven adhyāyas of the seventh aṣṭaka and twenty vargas of the eighth adhyāya are completed in it. The seer of the sūkta now beginning, the forty-first, which opens with समानमु त्यं (*samānamu tyaṃ*), is Suhastya, the son of the woman Ghoṣā. It is therefore necessary to describe briefly here the background of Ghoṣā, the mother of this seer, and of the seer Suhastya.

### Printed p. 2

It was explained earlier, with quotations from the Nirukta and other texts, what differing opinions exist about the principle the Aśvin deities represent. Many occasions that tell of the greatness of these deities were also set out. Many seers have praised them. Among them, the 39th and 40th sūktas of the tenth maṇḍala, composed by the woman seer Ghoṣā, and the 41st sūkta, composed by her son Suhastya, are well known. In these sūktas the seers praise the extraordinary great qualities of the Aśvins.

Although the sūktas composed by the seer named Ghoṣā are only two, the great help the Aśvins did her and her greatness were praised earlier in many mantras. Because she was an excellent devotee, these deities showed her great favour. Giving her personal background, the Bṛhaddevatā speaks of her thus:

आसीत्काक्षीवती घोषा पापरोगेण दुर्भगा । उवास षष्टिं वर्षाणि पितुरेव गृहे पुरु ॥ (*āsītkākṣīvatī ghoṣā pāparogeṇa durbhagā | uvāsa ṣaṣṭiṃ varṣāṇi pitureva gṛhe puru ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 7, 42)

A disfiguring and dreadful disease befell the woman Ghoṣā, daughter of Kakṣīvān. Her beauty was destroyed. For that very reason, without marriage, she had to live sixty years in her father's house.

आतस्थे महतीं चिंतां न पुत्रो न पतिर्मम । जरां प्राप्ता मुधा तस्मात् प्रपद्येऽहं शुभस्पती ॥ (*ātasthe mahatīṃ ciṃtāṃ na putro na patirmama | jarāṃ prāptā mudhā tasmāt prapadye 'haṃ śubhaspatī ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 7. 45)

"Unmarried, without husband and without son, I have grown old. My life is going to waste." Thus constant worry beset her. So she resolved: "I shall take refuge in the Aśvin deities, famed as Śubhaspatis (lords of radiance), praise them and obtain their grace"; and she prayed and cried out to the Aśvins.

यथैत् मामकस्तात आराध्यावाप यौवनं । आयुरारोग्यमैश्वर्यं सर्वभूतहने विषं ॥ (*yathait māmakastāta ārādhyāvāpa yauvanaṃ | āyurārogyamaiśvaryaṃ sarvabhūtahane viṣaṃ ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 7. 44)

"My father worshipped these same Aśvin deities and by their grace obtained youth, long life, health, strength, and a poison able to kill all beings. So I too shall worship them and obtain my wish."

### Printed p. 3

रूपवत्तां च सौभाग्यमहं तस्य सुता यदि । ममापि मंत्राः प्रादुः स्युरैस्तोष्टे मयाश्विन् ॥ (*rūpavattāṃ ca saubhāgyamahaṃ tasya sutā yadi | mamāpi maṃtrāḥ prāduḥ syuraistoṣṭe mayāśvin ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 7. 45)

"If I too come to know the way of praising and worshipping the Aśvin deities according to rule, I shall worship them and by their grace obtain beauty and well-being. As my father obtained a boon from them, I too shall obtain my wishes." Thus she reflected on the manner of praising the Aśvins.

चिंतयंतीति सूक्ते द्वे यो वां परि ददर्श सा। स्तुषौ तावश्विनौ देवौ प्रीत् तस्या भगांतरं ॥ (*ciṃtayaṃtīti sūkte dve yo vāṃ pari dadarśa sā| stuṣau tāvaśvinau devau prīt tasyā bhagāṃtaraṃ ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 7. 46)

While she was thus absorbed in meditation she received the vision of two sūktas, beginning with the mantras यो वां परि (*yo vāṃ pari*) and रथं यांतं (*rathaṃ yāṃtaṃ*) (10-39; 10-40), and praised the Aśvins with them. The Aśvin deities, pleased with this, entered her body.

प्रविश्य विजरारोगां सुभगां चक्रतुश्च ता । भर्तारं ददतुस्तस्यॆ सुहस्त्यं च सुतं मुनिं ॥ (*praviśya vijarārogāṃ subhagāṃ cakratuśca tā | bhartāraṃ dadatustasyè suhastyaṃ ca sutaṃ muniṃ ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 7. 47)

Then they removed her old age, freed her from disease and gave her a beautiful form. They also bestowed an excellent husband and an excellent son named Suhastya. Suhastya too is a renowned seer, like her.

ददतुस्तत्सुवर्णाभ्यां यन्नासत्येति कीर्त्यते । काक्षीवत्कै च घोषायै न तस्यामाजुरोऽनया ॥ (*dadatustatsuvarṇābhyāṃ yannāsatyeti kīrtyate | kākṣīvatkai ca ghoṣāyai na tasyāmājuro 'nayā ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 6. 48)

The manner in which the Aśvin deities came with their excellent suparṇas (horses having wings) and favoured Ghoṣā is described in laudatory sentences such as अमाजुरश्चिद्भवथः (*amājuraścidbhavathaḥ*) and न तस्य विद्म (*na tasya vidma*) (10-39-2; 10. 40. 11).

Ghoṣā is praised as belonging to the company of Brahmavādinīs, who have the power of supersensory vision and excellent skill in ritual action. These Brahmavādinīs are divided into three groups. Each of the three groups possesses a different kind of power and the ability to see the deities; the power of the woman seers of these groups is described thus:

### Printed p. 4

नवकः प्रथमस्ता सां वर्गस्तुष्टाव देवताः । यषिभिर्देवताभिश्च समूदे मध्यमो गणः ॥ (*navakaḥ prathamastā sāṃ vargastuṣṭāva devatāḥ | yaṣibhirdevatābhiśca samūde madhyamo gaṇaḥ ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 2, 85)

Of these, the first group has nine members. They praised the deities but did not speak directly with them. Those of the second group alone spoke directly both with the seers and with the deities.

आत्मनो भाववृत्तानि जगौ वर्गस्तथोत्तमः । उत्तमस्य तु वर्गस्य य ऋषिः सैव देवता ॥ (*ātmano bhāvavṛttāni jagau vargastathottamaḥ | uttamasya tu vargasya ya ṛṣiḥ saiva devatā ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 2. 86)

The seers who composed a sūkta praising their own bhāvavṛtta, that is, the special states of change of the self, are of the highest group. The seers of this group who composed the sūkta are themselves the deities named in that sūkta. Thus the Brahmavādinīs are of three kinds. Stating that Ghoṣā belongs to the first group, the text says:

घोषा, गोधा, विश्ववारा, अपालोपनिषन्निषत् । ब्रह्मजाया जुहूर्नाम अगस्त्यस्य स्वसादितिः ॥ (*ghoṣā, godhā, viśvavārā, apālopaniṣanniṣat | brahmajāyā juhūrnāma agastyasya svasāditiḥ ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 2, 82)

Ghoṣā (10-39, 40); Godhā; Viśvavārā (5. 28); Apālā (7. 91); Upaniṣat and Niṣat (composers of the khila sūkta beginning प्र धारयंत (*pra dhārayaṃta*)); Juhū Brahmajāyā (10. 109); the sister of Agastya (10. 60. 6); Aditi (some mantras in 4. 18). Thus it is indicated that Ghoṣā is one of the Brahmavādinīs of the first group. Therefore it becomes clear that Ghoṣā was a human woman with supersensory power able to realise divinity. Though a human woman, it is clear that by her divine power she worshipped the deities, saw them directly and obtained their grace.

It is also stated that although she had such divine power, because of her physical defect she could not obtain a proper husband, and that a life without husband and son grieved her mind. From such examples we learn how physical health and beauty serve as means to happiness in life. From this and many similar circumstances the nature of social life in the Vedic age is indicated: women lacking these two remained unmarried in their fathers' houses even when very old.

Even when life is sorrowful, Ghoṣā's life shows that by leading a pure life and by acts of worship of the deities this sorrow can be removed. Her excellent

### Printed p. 5

praise and worship pleased the Aśvins, and it is praised in many places that they brought her the highest happiness. This was explained earlier (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1. 112. 7 etc.).

It is also stated that a son named Suhastya was born to Ghoṣā by the grace of the Aśvins. This Suhastya too is an excellent composer of praises. He is known as the composer of the 41st sūkta of Ṛ. Saṃ. 10. The Ṛks of the present sūkta show his skill in composition. In them he praises the Aśvin deities. Ghoṣā and Ghauṣeya, both in their works, praised the divine powers of the Aśvins. These divine powers, and the differing opinions about the Aśvins, were stated earlier. Here only some points described in the present sūktas will be examined.

Sentences such as व्युष्टा उषसो हवामहे (*vyuṣṭā uṣaso havāmahe*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10. 41. 1) and प्रातर्युजं नासत्याधितिष्ठथः (*prātaryujaṃ nāsatyādhitiṣṭhathaḥ*) (10, 41. 2) show the natural relation between the Aśvin deities and Uṣas. It was also stated earlier that, according to one view cited by the Nirukta authors, the Aśvins are of the form of day and night (Ni. 12, 1). Many have approved this view. They are invoked at both day and night. This is clear in the very Ṛks composed by Ghoṣā. For example:

युवां मृगेव वारणा मृगण्यवो दोषा वस्तोर्हविषा नि ह्वयामहे। युवं होत्रामृतुथा जुह्वते नरेषं जनाय वहथः शुबस्पती ॥ (*yuvāṃ mṛgeva vāraṇā mṛgaṇyavo doṣā vastorhaviṣā ni hvayāmahe| yuvaṃ hotrāmṛtuthā juhvate nareṣaṃ janāya vahathaḥ śubaspatī ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10, 40, 4; the Kannada prints य (*ya*). सं (*saṃ*).)

"O Aśvin deities, at day and night we call and invite you with oblations." Thus their appearance at both these times is stated.

ताविद्दोषा ता उषसि शुभस्पती ता यामन्नुद्रवर्तनी (*tāviddoṣā tā uṣasi śubhaspatī tā yāmannudravartanī*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8. 22. 14)

Mantras composed by other seers, such as this, also make the matter clear. But here there is another refinement. According to some, "day and night" should be understood as the twilight at the time of the appearance of day and the twilight at the time of the appearance of night. These twilights consist of both light and dimness. But this refinement is not so fitting. Another view is that there is no simultaneous companionship in them. The answer to this objection is that, just as unity and companionship are spoken of in Mitra and Varuṇa, who represent day and night, so they must be spoken of here too. The view that the Aśvins appear at the time of Uṣas' appearance is stated in many places. But the time of Uṣas' appearance is not only the morning; the beginning of night too is designated Uṣas. For example:

### Printed p. 6

सुरुक्मे हि सुपेशसाधि श्रिया विराजतः । उषासावेह सीदशां ॥ (*surukme hi supeśasādhi śriyā virājataḥ | uṣāsāveha sīdaśāṃ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1. 18. 6)

Here the word Uṣas is used in the dual, उषासा (*uṣāsā*). For this word the meaning "day and night" is given. The Bhāṣya explains: उषसा एतत् रात्रेरप्युपलक्षणं उषःशब्दो दिवसस्कोपलक्षकः रात्रिरुषाश्च (*uṣasā etat rātrerapyupalakṣaṇaṃ uṣaḥśabdo divasaskopalakṣakaḥ rātriruṣāśca*). The Aśvins are described by नाना जात् (*nānā jāt*) (5-73.4); the purport of this sentence is that they are born in both day and night.

Along with this close relation of Uṣas and the Aśvins, a close relation of the goddess Sūryā and the Aśvins is also stated. Whether this allows an identity of Uṣas and Sūryā to be spoken of must be stated later, when Sūryā is explained (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10, 45). That is, since Sūryā is said to have mounted the three-wheeled chariot of the Aśvins, it is necessary here to examine the nature of the three wheels. In रथं त्रिचक्रं सवना गनिग्यतं (*rathaṃ tricakraṃ savanā ganigyataṃ*) (10. 41. 1), and likewise in other places (10. 85, 15), the three-wheeled chariot described can on the whole be said to be the three-wheeled chariot of Āditya.

This chariot is described in some places as one-wheeled, in others as two-wheeled, in still others as three-wheeled. Wherever one wheel is mentioned it is the wheel in the form of Āditya; where two wheels are mentioned they are the two wheels of Āditya's chariot touching heaven and earth. The same two wheels belong to the chariot of the Aśvins also. Stating this:

न्यंघ्नस्य मूर्धनि चक्रं रथस्य येमथुः । परि द्यामन्यदीयते ॥ (*nyaṃghnasya mūrdhani cakraṃ rathasya yemathuḥ | pari dyāmanyadīyate ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1. 20. 19; the Kannada prints य (*ya*). सं (*saṃ*).)

"O Aśvins, of the two wheels of your chariot you have set one on a firm mountain-top; the other you have made go around the whole heaven." These two wheels are visible to the world. The third is hidden.

द्वे ते चक्रे सूर्ये ब्रह्माण यतुथा विदुः । अथैकं चक्रं यद्दु हा तदद्धातय इद्दिदुः ॥ (*dve te cakre sūrye brahmāṇa yatuthā viduḥ | athaikaṃ cakraṃ yaddu hā tadaddhātaya iddiduḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10. 85. 16)

"O Sūryā, the skilled Brāhmaṇas can see two wheels of your chariot; but the other wheel alone is hidden." In explaining this, Sāyaṇa explains द्वे एव चंद्र सूर्यात्मके (*dve eva caṃdra sūryātmake*) as the two wheels in the form of moon and sun. And since the third wheel is hidden, he says that its nature is not accessible to ordinary awareness.

### Printed p. 7

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In the verse ज्योतिष्मंतं केतुमंतं त्रिचक्रं सुखं रथं सुषदं भूरिवारं । चित्रामघा यस्य योगेऽधिजज्ञे तं वां हुवे अति रिक्तं पिबध्यै ॥ (*jyotiṣmaṃtaṃ ketumaṃtaṃ tricakraṃ sukhaṃ rathaṃ suṣadaṃ bhūrivāraṃ | citrāmaghā yasya yoge 'dhijajñe taṃ vāṃ huve ati riktaṃ pibadhyai ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8. 58. 3), this chariot, which belongs to all the gods, is described. The word त्रिचक्रं (*tricakraṃ*) shows the special power of this chariot: it means that it is by nature one that travels through the earth-world, the atmosphere-world and the heaven-world. Here the hidden third wheel pervades all the worlds, yet keeps its secret nature concealed. Describing the same thing in another way, the verse इदं त एकं पर ऊत एकं तृतीयेन ज्योतिषा संविकस्व । (*idaṃ ta ekaṃ para ūta ekaṃ tṛtīyena jyotiṣā saṃvikasva |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10. 56. 1) and others point out three kinds of light. In sum, if the Aśvin deities are of the form of day and night, then the two wheels of their chariot are likewise of the form of the sun and the moon. The third is more secret. Ghoṣā and the son of Ghoṣā have praised most highly the Aśvin deities, who possess a chariot with wheels that are all-pervading and of the nature of light.

## Sūkta 41 (Maṇḍala 10)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> समानमिति तृचं द्वादशं सूक्तं घोषायाः पुत्रस्य सुहस्त्यस्यार्षं
> जागतमाश्विनं । अनुक्रांतं च । समानं तृचं सुहस्तो फौषेय इति ॥ प्रातरनुवाकाश्विनशस्त्रयोरुक्तो विनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The twelfth sūkta, beginning समानं (*samānaṃ*), a group of three verses, has as its seer Suhastya, son of Ghoṣā; its metre is Jagatī and its deity is the Aśvins. The Anukramaṇī says: समानं तृचं सुहस्तो फौषेय इति (*samānaṃ tṛcaṃ suhasto phauṣeya iti*). Its use (viniyoga) is in the Prātaranuvāka and the Aśvina-śastra.

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta, beginning *samānam*, is the twelfth sūkta in the third anuvāka of the tenth maṇḍala. It has three Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Suhastya, son of the woman Ghoṣā. The deities are the Aśvins; the metre is Jagatī. The Anukramaṇī says समानं तृचं सुहस्तो घौषेयः (*samānaṃ tṛcaṃ suhasto ghauṣeyaḥ*). This sūkta is used for the Prātaranuvāka and the Aśvina-śastra mantras.

##### Particulars

- Sūkta 72
- Maṇḍala 10, Anuvāka 3, Sūkta 41
- Aṣṭaka 7, Adhyāya 8, Varga 21
- Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 3
- Seer: Suhastya Ghauṣeya
- Deity: Aśvins
- Metre: Jagatī

### Printed p. 8

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स॒मा॒नमु॒ त्यं पु॑रुहू॒तमु॒क्थं१॒॑ १ रथं॑ त्रिच॒क्रं सव॑ना॒ गनि॑ग्यतं ।  
> परि॑ज्ञानं विद॒थ्यं॑ सुवृ॒क्तिभि॑र्व॒यं व्यु॑ष्टा उ॒षसो॑ हवामहे ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> समानं । ऊं इति । त्यं । पुरुहूतं । उक्ष्यं । रथं । त्रिचक्रं । सवना ।  
> गनिग्यतं ।  
> परिज्ञानं । विदथ्यं । सुवृक्तिभिः । वयं । विउष्टौ । उषसः । हवामहे ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अश्विनौ युवयोः समानमु साधारणमेव त्यं तं पुरुहूतं बहुभिराहूतमुक्थं प्रशस्यं त्रिचक्रं सवना सवनानि यज्ञान्गनिग्मतं प्रतिगच्छंतं
> परिज्ञानं परितो गंतारं विदथ्यं यज्ञहितं रथमुषसो व्युष्टौ सुवृक्तिभिः
> सुष्टु दोषवर्जिताभिः स्तुतिभिर्वयं हवामहे । आह्वयामः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Aśvins, we call, at the break of dawn (*uṣaso vyuṣṭau*), with well-made, faultless praises, that chariot of yours which is common to you both (समानमु (*samānamu*)), famed, invoked by many, praiseworthy (उक्ष्यं (*ukṣyaṃ*)), three-wheeled, going to the sacrifices, going all round (परिज्ञानं (*parijñānaṃ*): one who goes about on every side) and beneficial to the sacrifice (विदथ्यं (*vidathyaṃ*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Aśvin deities) समानमु (*samānamu*)—common to you both
- त्यं (*tyaṃ*)—famed
- पुरुहूतं (*puruhūtaṃ*)—worthy of the invocation of many
- उक्ष्यं (*ukṣyaṃ*)—praiseworthy
- त्रिचक्रं (*tricakraṃ*)—having three wheels
- सवना गनिग्मतं (*savanā ganigmataṃ*)—going to the sacrifices
- परिज्ञानं (*parijñānaṃ*)—moving all around
- विदथ्यं (*vidathyaṃ*)—useful for the sacrifice
- रथं (*rathaṃ*)—the chariot
- उषसः व्युष्टौ (*uṣasaḥ vyuṣṭau*)—at the time of the appearance of Uṣas
- सुवृक्तिभिः (*suvṛktibhiḥ*)—with excellent praises
- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we
- हवामहे (*havāmahe*)—we call, praying

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, that chariot which is common to you both, famed, worthy of the invocation of many, praiseworthy, having three wheels, going to the sacrifices, moving all around and useful for the sacrifice, we call, praying, with excellent praises at the time of the appearance of Uṣas.

### Printed p. 9

##### English translation
At break of dawn, (Aswins) we invoke with pious prayers your common chariot, praised of many, adorable, three-wheeled, frequenting religious rites, traversing all space, suitable at sacrifices.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्रा॒त्॒रयुर्जं॑ नास॒त्याधि॑ तिष्मथः प्रात्॒रर्यावा॑णं मधु॒वाह॑नं॒  ॑
> रथं ।  
> विशो॒ येन॒ गच्छ॑थो॒ यज्व॑रीर्नरा की॒रेश्चि॑द्य॒ज्ञं होतृ॑मंतमत्विना ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्रातःयुजं । नासत्त्या । अधि । तिष्कथः । प्रातःयावानं । मधुवाहनं । रथं ।  
> विशः । येन । गच्छथः । यज्वरीः । नरा । कीरेः । चित् । यज्ञं । होतृमंतं ।  
> अश्विना ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे नासत्या नासत्यभूतौ सत्यस्य प्रणेतारौ वा नरा नेतारावश्विनौ युवां
> प्रातर्युजं प्रातःकालेऽश्चैर्युज्यमानं प्रातर्यावाणं प्रातर्गंतारं मधुवाहनं मधुनोऽमृतस्य वाहकं तं रथमधि शिष्ठथः । आरोहतमित्यर्थः । यज्वरीर्यजनशीला विशः प्रजा येन रथेन गच्छथः । येन च कीरेश्चित्तोतुरपि होतृमंतमृषियुक्तं यज्ञं गच्छथः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Nāsatyas—either "not untrue" or "leaders of truth"—O leaders, Aśvins, mount that chariot which is yoked in the morning, goes in the morning, and carries honey (the nectar). It is the chariot by which you go to the sacrificing people (यज्वरीः (*yajvarīḥ*) = those given to sacrifice), and by which you go also to the sacrifice of the praiser (कीरेः (*kīreḥ*) = the reciter of praise), which is furnished with priests (होतृमंतं (*hotṛmaṃtaṃ*): accompanied by seers).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- नासत्या (*nāsatyā*)—O Aśvin deities
- नरा (*narā*)—you, who are leaders
- येन (*yena*)—by which chariot
- यज्वरीः (*yajvarīḥ*)—sacrificers
- विशः (*viśaḥ*)—to the people
- गच्छथः (*gacchathaḥ*)—you go
- येन (*yena*)—by which
- कीरेश्चित् (*kīreścit*)—of the praiser
- होतृमंतं (*hotṛmaṃtaṃ*)—accompanied by priests
- यज्ञं (*yajñaṃ*)—to the sacrifice
- (you go: such a chariot,) प्रातर्युजं (*prātaryujaṃ*)—to be yoked in the morning
- प्रातर्यावाणं (*prātaryāvāṇaṃ*)—to go to the sacrifice in the morning
- मधुवाहनं (*madhuvāhanaṃ*)—to carry honey
- रथं (*rathaṃ*)—the chariot
- अधि तिष्ठथः (*adhi tiṣṭhathaḥ*)—mount and sit

### Printed p. 10

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, you who are leaders, mount and sit on that chariot by which you go to the sacrificing people, and by which you go to the priest-furnished sacrifice of the praiser, which is to be yoked in the morning, goes to the sacrifice in the morning, and carries honey.

##### English translation
Ascend, Nasatyas, your nectar-bearing chariot, which is harnessed at dawn, and set in motion at dawn, with which, leaders (of rites), you go to the people who are engaged in worship, to the priest-administered sacrifice, Aswins, of (your) adorer.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒ध्व॒युर्वा॒ मधु॑पाणिं सु॒हस्त॑म॒ग्निधं॑ वा धृ॒तद॑क्षं॒  
> दमू॑नसं ।  
> विप्र॑स्य वा॒ यत्सव॑नानि॒ गच्छ॒थोऽत॒ आ या॑तं मधु॒पेय॑मत्विनागि

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अध्वयुं । वा । मधुपाणिं । सुहस्यं । अग्निधं । वा । धृतदक्षं । दमूनसं ।  
> विप्रस्य । वा । यत् । सवनानि । गच्छथः । अतः । आ । यातं । मधुपेयं । अश्विना॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अश्विनौ युवां मधुपाणिं सोमपाणिमध्वर्युं वा सुहस्यं सुहस्यनामानमृषिं मां धृतदक्षं धृतबलं दमूनसं दानमनसमग्निधं दर्श्विग्निशेषं
> समायातं । तदेव दर्शयति । यद्यद्यपि विप्रस्यान्यस्य मेधाविनः सवनानि यज्ञान्नच्छथः तथाप्यतस्तेभ्यो यज्ञेभ्यो मधुपेयमस्माकं सोमात्मकं पेयमा
> यातं । अन्वागच्छतं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Aśvins, come together to me, Suhastya by name, the seer, whether as the adhvaryu with soma in his hand, or as the Āgnīdhra, the remainder of the fire-priests—me who hold strength (धृतदक्षं (*dhṛtadakṣaṃ*)) and am generous-minded (दमूनसं (*damūnasaṃ*)). He then shows the point: even if you go to the sacrifices (सवनानि (*savanāni*)) of another wise man, nevertheless from those sacrifices come after to our soma-drink (मधुपेयं (*madhupeyaṃ*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Aśvin deities) मधुपाणिं (*madhupāṇiṃ*)—one holding soma in his hand
- अध्वर्युं दा— (*adhvaryuṃ dā—*)—either in the form of the adhvaryu
- धृतदक्षं (*dhṛtadakṣaṃ*)—possessing greater strength
- दमूनसं (*damūnasaṃ*)—possessing strength of giving

### Printed p. 11

- अग्निधं वा (*agnidhaṃ vā*)—or in the form of the Āgnīdhra
- सुहस्त्यं (*suhastyaṃ*)—to me, named Suhastya
- आ यातं (*ā yātaṃ*)—come
- यत् (*yat*)—if perchance
- विप्रस्य (*viprasya*)—of the wise man
- सवनानि (*savanāni*)—to the sacrifice
- गच्छथः (*gacchathaḥ*)—you have gone
- अतः (*ataḥ*)—from there
- मधुपेयं (*madhupeyaṃ*)—come to our sacrifice, which has a drink in the form of soma

**NOTE**

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, come to me, named Suhastya, either in the form of the adhvaryu holding soma in his hand, or in the form of the Āgnīdhra, who is of greater strength and generous-minded. Even if you have gone to the sacrifice of another sacrificer, come from there to our sacrifice, which offers soma.

##### English translation
Come, Aswins, to Suhasya, whether as the ministrant priest bearing the Soma in his hand, or as the competent lowly-minded feeder of the sacred fire; although you go to the sacrifice (of some other) sage, (yet) come, Aswins, from them to the beverage of the Soma (which I offer).

## Sūkta 42 (Maṇḍala 10)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अस्तेवेत्येकादशर्चं त्रयोदशं सूक्तमांगिरसस्य कृष्णाख्यस्यार्षं
> त्रैष्टुभमैंद्रं । अनुक्रांतं च । अस्तेवैकादश कृष्ण इति ॥ अभिप्लविकेषूक्ष्येषु
> स्तोमवृद्धाविदं सूक्तं ब्राह्मणाच्छंसिन आवापार्थं । सूत्रितं च । यज्ञे दिव इति
> सूक्ते अस्तेव सु प्रतरं । अ-७-९ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The thirteenth sūkta, beginning अस्तेव (*asteva*), of eleven verses, has as seer Kṛṣṇa, the Āṅgirasa; its metre is Triṣṭubh and its deity Indra. The Anukramaṇī says अस्तेवैकादश कृष्ण इति (*astevaikādaśa kṛṣṇa iti*). In the Abhiplavika ukthya rites this sūkta is used for the increase of the stoma, for the Brāhmaṇācchaṃsin's āvāpa (insertion). It is prescribed by the sūtra यज्ञे दिव इति सूक्ते अस्तेव सु प्रतरं । अ-७-९ । इति (*yajñe diva iti sūkte asteva su prataraṃ | a-7-9 | iti*).

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta, beginning *asteva*, is the thirteenth sūkta in the third anuvāka. It has eleven Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Kṛṣṇa, son of Aṅgiras; the deity is Indra; the metre is Triṣṭubh. The Anukramaṇī says अस्तेवैकादश कृष्णः (*astevaikādaśa kṛṣṇaḥ*). The use of this sūkta is for the sūkta which the priest called Brāhmaṇācchaṃsin must recite as an āvāpa for the increase of soma in the ukthya sacrifices connected with the Abhiplava, as set out in the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra by the sūtra यज्ञे दिव इति सूक्ते अस्तेव सु प्रतरं (*yajñe diva iti sūkte asteva su prataraṃ*) (Ā. 7-9).

### Printed p. 12

##### Particulars

- Maṇḍala 10, Anuvāka 3, Sūkta 42
- Aṣṭaka 7, Adhyāya 8, Varga 22, 23
- Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 11
- Seer: Kṛṣṇa
- Deity: Indra
- Metre: Triṣṭubh

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अस्ते॑व॒ सु प्र॑त॒रं लाय॒मस्य॒न्यूष॑न्निव॒ प्र भ॑रा॒ स्तोम॑मस्मै ।  
> वा॒चा वि॑प्रास्तरत॒ वाच॑म॒र्यो नि रा॑मय जरितः॒ सोम॒  ॑
> इंद्रं ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अस्ता इव । सु । प्रतरं । लायं । अस्यन् । भूषन् इव । प्र । भर । सोमं । अस्मि ।  
> वाचा । विप्राः । तरत । वाचं । अर्यः । नि । रमय । जरितरिति । सोमे । इंद्रं ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> प्रतरं प्रवृद्धतरं लायं संश्लेषणं हृदयवेधिनं शरं सु सुष्पस्यन्
> क्षपन्नस्तेव यथा क्षेप्ता धानुष्टो भूषन्निव यथा जालंकारमलंकुर्वन्नलंकर्ता
> तद्वत्सोममस्मा इंद्राय प्र भर । प्रापय । हे अंतरात्मन् त्वमिति शेषः । किंच
> हे विप्रा मेधाविनः यूयं वाचा स्तुत्र्यार्योऽरेः शत्रोर्वाचं तरत । निशरां
> तरत । निराकुरुतेत्यर्थः । हे जरितः स्तोतः त्वं सोमे सोमयाग इंद्रं वि
> रमय । क्रीडय ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

As an archer (अस्ता (*astā*)) shoots very well a swifter (प्रतरं (*prataraṃ*)) arrow (लायं (*lāyaṃ*): one that joins, piercing the heart), and as an adorner (भूषन्निव (*bhūṣanniva*)) adorns an ornament—so, O inner self (you being understood), bring forward soma to this Indra. Further, O wise ones (विप्रा (*viprā*)), with speech, that is, with praise, overcome the speech of the enemy (*areḥ*)—that is, put it down. O praiser (जरितः (*jaritaḥ*)), delight (रमय (*ramaya*): make sport) Indra at the soma sacrifice.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- प्रतरिं (*pratariṃ*)—swift-going
- लायं (*lāyaṃ*)—the arrow that reaches the target
- सु (*su*)—exceedingly
- अस्यन् (*asyan*)—throwing
- अस्तेव (*asteva*)—like a bow-bearer
- भूषन्निव (*bhūṣanniva*)—like a painter adorning pictures
- स्तोमं (*stomaṃ*)—this hymn
- अस्मै (*asmai*)—addressed to Indra
- प्र धर (*pra dhara*)—offer
- विप्राः (*viprāḥ*)—O wise praisers
- वाचा (*vācā*)—with your praise

### Printed p. 13
- of the enemy
- वाचं (*vācaṃ*)—the praise-word
- तरत (*tarata*)—surpass
- जरितः (*jaritaḥ*)—O worshipper
- सोमे (*some*)—in the Soma sacrifice
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra
- नि रमय (*ni ramaya*)—make (him) sport

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Offer this hymn to Indra as an archer aims and looses a swift arrow that reaches its mark, and as a painter, wholly absorbed in his pictures, applies colours to them. O wise worshippers, surpass by your praise the praise-words of the enemy. O worshipper, make Indra sport at the Soma sacrifice.

##### English translation
Direct your laudation to him, (Indra), like an archer aiming a larger arrow (to its mark), like a decorator (applying his ornaments) : surpass by your praises, sages, the praises of your adversaries; delight Indra, worshipper, by the (offering of the) Soma.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> दोहे॑न॒ गामुप॑ शिक्षा॒ सखा॑यं॒ प्र बो॑धय जरितर्जा॒रमिंद्रं॑ ।  
> कोशं॒ न पू॒र्णं वसु॑ना॒ न्यृ॑ष्ट॒मा च्या॑वय मघ॒देया॑य॒  ॑
> शूरं ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> दोहेन । गां । उप । शिक्ष । सखायं । प्र । बोधय । जरितः । जारं । इंद्रं ।  
> कोकं । न । पूर्णं । वसुना । नियष्टं । आ । च्यदय । मघदेयाय । शूरं ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे जरितः त्वं दोहेन कामानां दोहनार्थं गां गोरूपं सखायं
> प्रियमिंद्रमुप शिक्ष । वशं नय । किंच जारं भूतानां जरयितारमिंद्रं प्र
> बोधय । स्तुतिभिः प्रबुद्धं कुरु । किंच पूर्णमुदकेन पूरितं कोशं न पात्र-

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O worshipper, for the milking out of your wishes win over Indra, your dear friend, who is here as it were in the form of a cow (गां (*gāṃ*)): bring him under your control. Further, awaken Indra, the one who makes beings grow old (जारं (*jāraṃ*)), by your praises, make him fully awake. And further, like a vessel (कोशं न (*kośaṃ na*)) filled full of water, filled with wealth, gold and the like, bowed down, the hero Indra, make him come toward us for the giving of wealth."

### Printed p. 14

> मिव वसुना हिरण्यादिना धनेन नृष्टं नीचैर्गतं शूरं वीरमिंद्रं मघदेयाय
> धनदानाया च्यवय । अभिमुखं कुर्वित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The end of the sentence: the hero Indra, bowed down with the burden of wealth, is to be brought to face us, so that he may give wealth.

**Pratipadartha**
- जरितः (*jaritaḥ*)—O worshipper
- दोहेन (*dohena*)—for the milking of milk
- गां (*gāṃ*)—as one stops a cow and makes it stand
- सखायां (*sakhāyāṃ*)—the friend
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra
- उप शिक्ष (*upa śikṣa*)—stop and keep (him) with yourself
- जारं (*jāraṃ*)—Indra, who brings old age on beings
- प्रबोधय (*prabodhaya*)—awaken
- पूर्णं (*pūrṇaṃ*)—full of water
- कोशं न (*kośaṃ na*)—like a vessel
- वसुना (*vasunā*)—by the burden of wealth
- नृष्टं (*nṛṣṭaṃ*)—bowed down
- शूरं (*śūraṃ*)—the hero, Indra
- मघदेयाय (*maghadeyāya*)—for the distribution of wealth
- आ च्यवय (*ā cyavaya*)—make (him) turn toward us

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O worshipper, stop and keep your friend Indra with you, as one stops a cow to milk her. Awaken Indra, who brings old age on beings. Make Indra, the hero, bowed down with the burden of wealth like a vessel full of water, turn toward us for the distribution of wealth.

##### English translation
Control, worshipper, your friend(Indra), like a cow for your milking: awake, Indra, the author of decay; bring down for the distribution of wealth the hero who is pressed down with wealth like a vessel full (of water)

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> किमं॒ग त्वा॑ मघवन्नो॒जमा॑हुः शिती॒हि मा॑ त्रिश॒यं त्वा॑ शृ  
> होमि ।  
> अप्न॑स्वती॒ मम॒ धीर॑स्तु शक्र वस्तु॒विदं॒ भग॑मिं॒द्रा भ॑रा नः ॥३॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कं । अंगत्वा । मघवन् । भोजं । आहुः । शिशिहि । मा । शिशयं । त्वा । शृणोमि ।  
> अप्पसुती । मम । धीः । अस्तु । शक्त । वस्तुविदं । भगं । इंद्र । आ । भर । सः ॥ ३ ॥

### Printed p. 15

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अंगेति सामान्याह्वानं । अंग हे मघवन्थनवञ्चक्रेंद्र त्वा त्वां किं किमर्थं
> भोजं स्तोतृणां भोजकमभीष्टमाहुः सर्वे विद्वांसः । यदि न ददासीत्यध्याहारः । तस्माद्ददास्केव स्तोतृभ्यो धनानीत्यर्थः । तथा सति मा स्तोतारं शिशीहि ।
> धनदानेन तीक्ष्णीकुरु । त्वा त्वां शिशयं स्तोतृणां संस्कर्तारं शृणोमि । किंच
> मम धीर्बुद्धिरप्पस्वती कर्मवत्यस्तु । भवतु । किंच नोऽस्माकं वसुविदं धनस
> लंबकं भगं भाग्यमा भर । आहर ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

अंग (*aṃga*) is a general form of address. "O Maghavan, O wealthy Indra, why do all the learned call you भोजं (*bhojaṃ*), the one who feeds the singers, the giver of what they desire?" One must supply: "if you do not give". Therefore you should give wealth to the singers. That being so, "sharpen me, the singer": make me keen by the gift of wealth. "I hear you as शिशयं (*śiśayaṃ*), the one who refines (संस्कर्तारं (*saṃskartāraṃ*)) the singers." And "let my understanding be अप्पस्वती (*appasvatī*), endowed with works." And "bring us the fortune (भगं (*bhagaṃ*)) that finds wealth."

**Pratipadartha**
- अंग (*aṃga*)—O revered one
- मघवन् (*maghavan*)—O wealthy one
- शक्र (*śakra*)—O Indra
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- किं (*kiṃ*)—for what reason
- भोजं (*bhojaṃ*)—as the giver of enjoyment to the worshippers
- आहुः (*āhuḥ*)—they have said
- (therefore) मा (*mā*)—me
- शिशीहि (*śiśīhi*)—invigorate by the gift of wealth
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- शिशयं (*śiśayaṃ*)—as the one who refines
- कृणोमि (*kṛṇomi*)—I have heard
- मम (*mama*)—my
- धीः (*dhīḥ*)—understanding
- अप्पस्वती (*appasvatī*)—devoted to rites
- अस्तु (*astu*)—let it be
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- वसुविदं (*vasuvidaṃ*)—that brings wealth
- भगं (*bhagaṃ*)—fortune
- आ भर (*ā bhara*)—provide

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O revered and wealthy Indra, for what reason have they called you the giver of enjoyment to the worshippers? Therefore invigorate me by the gift of wealth. I have heard of you as such a refiner. Make my understanding devoted to rites. Provide us the fortune that brings wealth.

##### English translation
Why, Maghavat, do (the wise) call you the nourisher(of worshippers)? Quicken me, (Indra), with wealth, (for) I hear that you are the quickener (of the worshippers); may my understanding, Shakra, be productive of works; grant us, Indra, a destiny that brings wealth.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> त्वां जना॑ ममस॒त्येष्विं॑द्र संतस्था॒ना वि ह्व॑यंते समी॒के ।  
> अत्ता॒ युजं॑ कृणुते॒ यो ह॒विष्मा॒न्नासु॑न्वता स॒ख्यं व॑ष्टि॒ शूरः॑॥

### Printed p. 16

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> त्वां । जनाः । ममसत्येषु । इंद्र । संतस्तानाः । दि । ह्ययंते । संईके ।  
> अत्र । युजं । कृणुते । यः । हविष्मान् । न । अगुन्नता । सख्यं । दष्टि । तदः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र त्वां जना ममसत्येषु संग्रामेषु वि द्वयंते ! विविधमात्रयंति
> साहाय्यार्थं । एतदेव दर्शयति । समीके युद्धे संतस्थाः सह शिष्यतेन जना
> वि द्वयंत इति । अश्रास्मिन्नाह्वाने स शूरो वीर इंद्रस्तं युजं सहायं कृणुंते
> कुरुते यो हविष्मान्भवति ! असुन्वता सोमाभिसवमकुर्वता पुरुषेण सह सख्यं
> सखित्वं न वष्टि । न कामयते ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, people call upon you in battles (ममसत्येषु (*mamasatyeṣu*), i.e. conflicts) in manifold ways, for help." This is what he shows: standing together in the fight (समीके (*samīke*)) with joint effort, people call upon you. At this call the hero Indra makes his ally (युजं (*yujaṃ*)) the one who is a bearer of oblations (हविष्मान् (*haviṣmān*)). With a man who does not press Soma (असुन्वता (*asunvatā*)) he does not desire (न वष्टि (*na vaṣṭi*)) friendship.

**Pratipadartha**
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- त्वां (*tvāṃ*)—you
- the people (printed जनाति (*janāti*))
- ममसत्येषु (*mamasatyeṣu*)—in the battles
- विह्वयंते (*vihvayaṃte*)—call in many ways
- समीके (*samīke*)—in battle
- युजं (*yujaṃ*)—as a friend
- कृणुते (*kṛṇute*)—they make
- अत्र (*atra*)—at these times
- यः (*yaḥ*)—whichever battle **NOTE**
- हविष्मात् (*haviṣmāt*)—the one who has the oblation
- शूरः (*śūraḥ*)—the hero Indra
- युजं (*yujaṃ*)—as a friend
- कृणुते (*kṛṇute*)—I make for myself **NOTE**
- असुन्वता (*asunvatā*)—with one who does not press the leaking things **NOTE**
- सख्यं (*sakhyaṃ*)—friendship
- न वष्टि (*na vaṣṭi*)—does not desire

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, people call you in battles in manifold ways. In battle they make you their friend. At these times, whichever sacrificer provides the oblation, the hero Indra makes him his friend. Indra does not desire friendship without drying up the Soma. **NOTE**

##### English translation
The people earnestly call upon you in battles, Indra, (they call upon you) when standing together in the conflict : at this(call) Indra makes him his friend who offers oblations; the hero desires not friendship with him who presents no libation.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> धन्वं॒ न स्यं॒द्रं ब॑हु॒लं यो अ॑स्मै ती॒व्रान्तोस्मा॑ आस्तु॒नोति॒  
> प्रय॑स्वान् ।

### Printed p. 17

> तस्मै शत्रून्तुसतुकान्प्रातरह्नो नि सृष्टान्त्युवति हंति वृत्रं ॥५॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> धनं । न । स्कंद्रं । बहुलं । यः । अस्मै । तीव्रान् । सोमान् । आऽनुनोति ।  
> प्रयॆस्वान् ।  
> तस्मै । शत्रुन् । स्तुतुकान् । प्रातः । अह्नः । नि । सुअष्टान् । युवति । हंति ।  
> वृत्रं ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> प्रयस्मान् हविष्मान्यो यजमानः स्यंद्रं स्यंदनशीलं गवाश्वादिकं
> बहुलं प्रभूतं धनं न यथा धनं दरिद्राय पात्रभूताय दातुं संस्करोति
> तद्वदस्मा इंद्राय तीव्रांस्त्रीव्ररसान्नोमानासुनोति अभिषुगोति । अभिषवाख्येन
> संस्कारेण संस्करोतीत्यर्थः। तस्यॆ यजमानाय प्रातरह्मः पूर्वाह्ने सुतुकान्सुप्रेरणाज्योभनपुत्रसहितान्वा स्पष्टाज्योभनायुधाण्यत्रून्नि युवति । स इंद्रः
> पृथक्करोति । किंच वृत्रं तदीयमुपद्रवं हंति हिनस्ति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sacrificer who has oblations (हविष्मान् (*haviṣmān*)) is ready, as one is ready to give abundant moving wealth (स्यंद्रं (*syaṃdraṃ*)), cattle, horses and the like, to a poor man who is a fit recipient. In the same way he presses (आसुनोति (*āsunoti*)) for this Indra strong-flavoured Soma juices; that is, he refines him with the rite called pressing. For that sacrificer, in the forenoon (प्रातरह्नः (*prātarahnaḥ*)), Indra separates off (नि युवति (*ni yuvati*)) the enemies, who are सुतुकान् (*sutukān*), "of good impulse", or "with fine sons", and *spaṣṭān*, "with fine weapons". And he destroys Vṛtra, their trouble.

**Pratipadartha**
- स्यंद्रं (*syaṃdraṃ*)—moving
- बहुलं (*bahulaṃ*)—abundant
- धनं न (*dhanaṃ na*)—as wealth is given to a poor man
- प्रयस्मान् (*prayasmān*)—endowed with oblation
- यः (*yaḥ*)—the sacrificer who
- अस्मै (*asmai*)—for Indra
- तीव्रान् (*tīvrān*)—intoxicating
- सोमान् (*somān*)—Soma juices
- आसुनोति (*āsunoti*)—presses and offers
- तस्मै (*tasmai*)—for that sacrificer
- प्रातरह्नः (*prātarahnaḥ*)—in the forenoon itself
- सुतुकान् (*sutukān*)—those with good helpers **NOTE**
- स्वष्टान् (*svaṣṭān*)—those with good weapons
- शत्रून् (*śatrūn*)—enemies
- नि युवति (*ni yuvati*)—drives far away
- वृत्रं (*vṛtraṃ*)—their violence
- हंति (*haṃti*)—destroys

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

As one gives abundant, moving wealth in the form of cattle and the like to a poor man, the sacrificer endowed with oblation who presses and offers intoxicating Soma juices to Indra: for that sacrificer, in the forenoon itself, Indra drives far away the enemies who have good helpers and good weapons. He destroys their violence.

##### English translation
The bearer of the oblation who offers to Indra the sharp Soma-juices, like much moveable wealth (given to the poor)—for him in the fore

### Printed p. 18
part of the day Indra scatters his eager and well-armed (enemies), and defeats his foe.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यस्मि॑न्व॒यं द॑धि॒मा शंस॒मिंद्रॆ॒ यः शि॒श्राय॑ म॒घवा॒ काम॑म॒स्मे ।  
> आ॒राच्चि॒त्सन्व॑यतामस्य॒ शत्रु॒र्न्य॑स्मै द्यु॒म्ना जन्मा॑ नमंतां ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यस्मिन् । वयं । दधिम । शंसं । इंद्रे । यः । शिश्राय । मघवा । कामं । अस्मे  
> इति ।  
> आरात् । चित् । सन् । भयतां । अस्य । शत्रुः । नि । अस्मै । द्युम्ना । जन्मा । नमंतां ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यस्मिन्निंद्रे वयं घौषेयाः सुहस्ताः शंसं स्तुतिं दधिम विदध्य । यश्च
> मघवा धनवानिंद्रोऽस्मे अस्मासु काममभिलाषं शिप्राय श्राययति । अस्येंद्रस्य
> शत्रुराराद्दूर एव संक्षित् सन्नपि भयतां । बिभेतु । किंबास्मा इंद्रिय जन्मा
> जन्यानि शत्रुजनपदभवानि द्युम्ना द्युम्नास्यन्नानि नि समंतां । प्रह्वीफपंतु ।
> भोजनीयानि भवंतित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"In which Indra we, the घौषेयाः सुहस्ताः (*ghauṣeyāḥ suhastāḥ*), have set (दधिम (*dadhima*)) praise (शंसं (*śaṃsaṃ*)), and who, as the wealthy one (मघवा (*maghavā*)), fulfils (शिश्राय (*śiśrāya*)) our desire: the enemy of this Indra, even though far away, let him fear. And let the enemy-country-born foods (द्युम्ना (*dyumnā*)) bow down to him, let them submit; that is, let them become fit for his enjoyment."

**Pratipadartha**
- यस्मिन् इंद्रे (*yasmin iṃdre*)—in which Indra
- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we
- शंसं (*śaṃsaṃ*)—praise
- दधिम (*dadhima*)—we offer
- यः (*yaḥ*)—who
- मघवा (*maghavā*)—the wealthy one, such Indra
- अस्यॆ (*asyè*)—in us
- कामं (*kāmaṃ*)—the desired object
- शिश्राय (*śiśrāya*)—fulfils
- अस्य (*asya*)—of this Indra
- शत्रुः (*śatruḥ*)—the enemy
- आरात् सन् चित् (*ārāt san cit*)—even while being far away
- भयतां (*bhayatāṃ*)—let him fear
- अस्यॆ (*asyè*)—to this Indra
- जन्या (*janyā*)—produced in the country of the enemy people
- द्युम्ना (*dyumnā*)—all wealth of food
- नि नमंतां (*ni namaṃtāṃ*)—let them come under his sway

The Indra in whom we have set praise, the wealthy one, fulfils our desired objects. The enemy of this Indra fears even while he is far away. May all the food-wealth produced in the country of the enemy people come under the sway of this Indra.

### Printed p. 19

##### English translation
May the enemy of that Indra on whom we have laid our praise, the lord of affluence, who has granted us our desires, be in fear of him even when far off : may the food belonging to the country of the foe bow down before him (to enjoy)

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ॒राच्छत्रु॒मप॑ बाधस्व दू॒रमु॒गो यः शंबः॑ पुरुहूत॒  ॑
> तेन ।  
> अ॒स्मे धे॑हि॒ यव॑म॒ुद्योम॑दिंद्र कृ॒धी धियं॑ जरि॒त्रे वाज॑रत्नां

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आरात् । शत्रुं । अप । बाधस्व । दूरं । उग्रः । यः । शंबः । पुरुहूत । तेन ।  
> अस्मे इति । धेहि । यवमत् । गोमत् । इंद्र । कृधि । धियं । जरित्रे । वाजरत्नां ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे पुरुहूत बहुभिराहूतेंद्र तव य उग्र उद्धूणः शंबो वज्रः ।
> तथा च यास्कः । शंब इति वज्रनाम शमयतेर्वा शातयतेर्वा । नि ५-२४ । इति ।
> तेन वज्रेण शत्रुमस्मदरिमारादस्मत्समीपाद्दूरमप बाधस्व । त्वमपगमयॆ । किंचास्मे अस्मासु यवमद्यवयुक्तं गोमद्गोयुक्तं च धनं धेहि । निधेहि । किंच
> जरित्रे स्तोत्रे मह्यं राजरत्नां रमणीयान्नं धियं कर्म कृधि । कुरु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Purūhūta, Indra, invoked by many, your fierce, terrible Śamba is the thunderbolt." Yāska says: शंब इति वज्रनाम शमयतेर्वा शातयतेर्वा । (*śaṃba iti vajranāma śamayatervā śātayatervā |*) (Ni. 5-24). With that thunderbolt drive the enemy far from us, away from our neighbourhood; make him go away. Further, place in us wealth that is rich in barley and rich in cattle. And for me, the worshipper who praises, accomplish the rite (धियं (*dhiyaṃ*)) together with delightful food (राजरत्नां (*rājaratnāṃ*) is so glossed).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पुरुहूत (*puruhūta*)—worthy of being invoked by many
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- यः (*yaḥ*)—which
- उग्रः (*ugraḥ*)—of terrible power
- शंबः (*śaṃbaḥ*)—thunderbolt-weapon there is
- केन (*kena*)—with that **NOTE**
- शत्रुं (*śatruṃ*)—the enemy
- आरात् दूरं (*ārāt dūraṃ*)—far from us
- अप बाधस्व (*apa bādhasva*)—strike and drive away
- अस्मे (*asme*)—in us
- यवमत् (*yavamat*)—endowed with barley
- गोमत् (*gomat*)—endowed with cattle, the wealth
- धेहि (*dhehi*)—establish
- जरित्रे (*jaritre*)—to me, the praiser
- वाजरत्नां (*vājaratnāṃ*)—delightful
- धियं (*dhiyaṃ*)—the rite
- कृधि (*kṛdhi*)—construct and give

### Printed p. 20

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, worthy of being invoked by many, with the thunderbolt of terrible power that you have, drive the enemy far away from us. Establish in us wealth that is rich in barley-grain and rich in cattle. To me, the praiser, construct and give delightful food and the rite.

##### English translation
Invoked of many, (Indra), drive far away the enemy with that fierce thunderbolt of yours : grant to us, Indra, wealth of barley and cattle : render to the worshipper his sacrifice productive of pleasant food

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र यमं॒तर्वृ॑षस॒वासो॒ अग्॑न्ती॒व्राः सोमा॑ बहु॒लांता॑स॒  ॑
> इंद्रं ।  
> नाह॑ दा॒मानं॑ म॒घवा॒ नि यं॑स॒न्नि सु॑न्व॒ते व॑हति॒ भूरि॑ वा॒मं

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । यं । अंतः । वृषसवासः । अग्मन् । तीव्राः । सोमाः । बहुलअंतासः ।  
> इंद्रं ।  
> न । अह । दामानं । मघवा । नि । यंसत् । नि । सुन्वते । वहति । भूरि । पामं ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अंतॆर्जठरे यमिंद्रं वृषसवासो वृषसवा अग्नौ सोमस्य वर्षित्प्रभर्धर्युभिरभिषुतास्तीव्रास्तीव्ररसा बहुलांतासो बहुलमन्नादिकमंते येभ्यत्ते
> बहुलांताः सोमाः प्राग्मन् प्रगच्छंति स मघवा धनवानिंद्रो दामानं दाकारं
> यजमानं नाह नैव नि यंसत् । नियच्छति । किंतु सुन्वते सोमाभिषवं कुर्वते
> यजमानाय भूर्यधिकं वामं वननीयं धनं नि वहति । निकरां प्रासयति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The Indra into whose belly (अंतः (*aṃtaḥ*)) the Soma-juices have entered—pressed in the fire by the Adhvaryus, the showerers of Soma, sharp (strong in juice), and having abundant food at their end—that wealthy Indra does not restrain (नि यंसत् (*ni yaṃsat*)) the giver, the sacrificer. On the contrary, to the sacrificer who presses Soma he brings abundant, desirable wealth.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वृषसवासः (*vṛṣasavāsaḥ*)—pressed by the Adhvaryus
- तीक्ष्णाः (*tīkṣṇāḥ*)—intoxicating
- बहुलांतासः (*bahulāṃtāsaḥ*)—having abundant food-juices
- सोमाः (*somāḥ*)—the Soma-juices
- यं इंद्रं (*yaṃ iṃdraṃ*)—which Indra

### Printed p. 21

- अंतः (*aṃtaḥ*)—inside the belly
- प्र अग्मन् (*pra agman*)—have entered
- मघवा (*maghavā*)—that same wealthy Indra
- दामानं (*dāmānaṃ*)—the sacrificer
- नाह नि यंसत् (*nāha ni yaṃsat*)—does not abandon
- सुस्वते (*susvate*)—to the sacrificer
- भूरि (*bhūri*)—abundant
- वामं (*vāmaṃ*)—attractive wealth
- नि वहति (*ni vahati*)—brings and provides

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The Soma-juices—pressed by the Adhvaryus, intoxicating, having abundant food-juices—enter the belly of Indra; that same wealthy Indra does not abandon the sacrificer. He brings and provides to the sacrificer abundant and attractive wealth.

##### English translation
The Indra into whom the sharp aboundānt Soma-juices cſfused by (the priests), the showerers (of the Soma), have entered : he, Maghavat, deserts not the donor of the sacrifice, but bestows ample wealth upon him who presents libations.

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒त प्र॒हाम॑त्ति॒दीव्या॑ जयाति कृ॒तं यच्छ्व॒घ्नी वि॑चि॒नोति॑ का॒ले  
> यो दे॒वका॑मो॒ न धना॑ रुणद्धि॒ समित्तं रा॒या सृ॑जति स्व॒धावा॑न् ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उत । प्रहां । अत्तिऽदीव्य । जयाति । कृतं । यत् । श्वघ्नी । विचिनोति । काले ।  
> यः । देवकामः । न । धना । रुणद्धि । सं । इत् । तं । राया । सृजति । स्वधावान् ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> उपापि चेंद्रः प्रहां प्रहंतारमतिदीव्य । अतिरभिपूजितार्थे वर्तते ।
> दीवृतिर्गत्यर्थे । अभिपूजितं गमयित्वा जयाति । जयति । किंच यद्यथा । अत्र
> प्रकारवचनप्रत्ययस्य लोपो द्रष्टव्यः । श्वघ्नी कितवः कृतं कृतसमयं प्रतिकितवं
> किरवानां मध्यॆ विचिनोति परीक्ष्य गृह्णाति तद्वदिंद्रः काले युद्धकाले बलवंतं
> शत्रुं परीक्ष्य वधार्थं गृह्णातीत्यर्थः । किंच । यो मनुष्यो देवकामो देवान्यष्टुं स्तोतुं वाभिलाषवान्भवति तस्यार्थाय धना धनानि न रुणद्धि । नावृगोति ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Indra also, having driven away (*atidīvya*) the assailant (प्रहां (*prahāṃ*), the striker), conquers. अति (*ati*) is used in the sense of honoured; the root दीव् (*dīv*) is in the sense of going; having made the honoured one go, he conquers. Further, "as": here the affix of manner is to be seen as dropped. As a gambler (श्वघ्नी (*śvaghnī*)) selects from among the players the opponent with whom the कृत (*kṛta*) throw is agreed, examining and taking him, so Indra at the time of battle examines the strong enemy and takes him up for slaying. Further: the man who longs to worship or praise the gods does not obstruct (रुणद्धि (*ruṇaddhi*): does not cover) his riches for that purpose. On the contrary, powerful (स्वधावान् (*svadhāvān*)) Indra unites that god-loving man with wealth.

### Printed p. 22

> नाच्छादयतीत्यर्थः । किंतु स्वधावान्बलवानिंद्रस्तं देवकामं राया धनेन सं
> सृजति । संयोजयत्येव ॥

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- उत (*uta*)—and
- प्रहां (*prahāṃ*)—the enemy who falls upon (him)
- अतिदीप्त्य (*atidīptya*)—having driven far away
- जयाति (*jayāti*)—he conquers
- यत् (*yat*)—in what manner
- कृष्णी (*kṛṣṇī*)—the gambler (hunter) **NOTE**
- कृतं (*kṛtaṃ*)—watching for the time
- विचिनोति (*vicinoti*)—selects his opponent (animals etc.), as he does
- काले (*kāle*)—at the time of battle (Indra too) selects and defeats the enemy
- यः (*yaḥ*)—which sacrificer
- देवकामः (*devakāmaḥ*)—being fond of the worship of the gods
- धना (*dhanā*)—riches
- न रुणद्रि (*na ruṇadri*)—does not cover up **NOTE**
- तं (*taṃ*)—that devotee of the gods
- स्वधावान् (*svadhāvān*)—Indra, endowed with power
- राया (*rāyā*)—with wealth
- सं सृजति (*saṃ sṛjati*)—unites

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra drives away the enemy who falls upon him and conquers. As a hunter lies in ambush and selects and strikes animals and the like, and as a gambler examines the time and selects his opponent, so Indra too at the time of battle selects the enemy and defeats him. The sacrificer who, being fond of the worship of the gods, does not cover up his riches—that devotee of the gods the powerful Indra unites with wealth.

##### English translation
Having driven away the assailant, he triumphs: at the time (of battle) he selects his antagonist as does a gambler, the man who, desirous of gratifying the gods, withholds not his riches, him the powerful Indra associates with wealth.

#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> गोभि॑ष्टरे॒माम॑तिं दु॒रेवां॒ यवे॑न॒ क्षुधं॑ पुरुहूत॒ विश्वां॑।  
> व॒यं राज॑भिः प्रथ॒मा धना॑न्य॒स्माके॑न वृ॒जने॑ना जयेम ॥१०॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> गोभिः । तरेम । अमतिं । दुःएवां । यवेन । क्षुधं । पुरुहूत । विश्वां ।  
> वयं । राजभिः । प्रथमाः । धनानि । अस्माकेन । वृजनेन । जयेम ॥ १० ॥

### Printed p. 23

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे पुरुहूत बहुभिराहूतेंद्र तव प्रसादाद्दुरेवां दुष्टागमनां
> दारिद्रादागताममतिं दुर्बुद्धि वयं घौषेयाः सुहस्त्या गोभिः पशुभिस्तरेम ।
> निस्तरेम । किंच यवेन विश्वां व्याप्तां क्षुधं तरेम । किंच प्रथमा मुख्यानि
> धनानि राजभिर्धनानामीश्वरैः । लभेमहीति शेषः । यद्वा । प्रथमा वयमिति
> संबंधः । किंचास्माकेनास्मदीयेन वृजनेन बलेन जयेम । शत्रुनिति शेषः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Purūhūta, Indra, invoked by many, by your favour may we cross over the evil (दुरेवां (*durevāṃ*), of evil approach, come from poverty) ill-mindedness (अमतिं (*amatiṃ*)) with cattle." Further, may we cross over all-pervading hunger with barley. Further, may we obtain the first, chief riches through the kings, the lords of riches—"may we obtain" is to be supplied. Or else the connection is "we the first". Further, may we conquer by our own strength (वृजनेन (*vṛjanena*), strength); "the enemies" is to be supplied. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पुरुहूत (*puruhūta*)—O Indra, called by many
- दुरेवां (*durevāṃ*)—come from poverty
- अमतिं (*amatiṃ*)—ill-mindedness
- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we
- गोभिः (*gobhiḥ*)—with the help of our wealth of cattle
- तरेम (*tarema*)—may we cross over
- यवेन (*yavena*)—with the help of barley-grain
- विश्वां (*viśvāṃ*)—which surrounds us
- क्षुधं (*kṣudhaṃ*)—may we cross over hunger
- प्रथमाः (*prathamāḥ*)—very chief
- धनानि (*dhanāni*)—riches
- राजभिः (*rājabhiḥ*)—through the lords of wealth (may we obtain)
- अस्माकेन (*asmākena*)—our
- वृजनेन (*vṛjanena*)—by strength
- जयेम (*jayema*)—may we conquer the enemies

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, who are called by many, may we cross over, with our wealth of cattle, the ill-mindedness that has come from poverty. May we put an end, with the help of barley-grain, to the hunger that surrounds us. May we obtain the chief riches from the wealthy. May we conquer the enemies by our strength.

##### English translation
Indra, invoked of many, may we escape all ignorance caused by poverty by means of our cattle : (may we escape) all hunger by means of our barley : (may we acquire) the chief riches through the princes (of wealth) : may we conquer by our valour.

#### Ṛk 11

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> बृह॒स्पति॑र्नः॒ परि॑ पातु प॒श्चादु॒तोत्त॑रस्मा॒दध॑रादघा॒योः ।  
> इंद्रः॑ पु॒रस्ता॑दु॒त म॑ध्य॒तो नः॒ सखा॒ सखि॑भ्यो॒ वरि॑वः कृणोतु

### Printed p. 24

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> बृहस्पतिः । नः । परि । पातु । पश्चात् । उत । उत्तरस्मात् । अधरात् । अघयोः ॥  
> इंद्रः । पुरस्तात् । उत । मध्यतः । नः । सखा । सखिभ्यः । वरिवः । कृणोतु ॥ ११ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> पश्चात्पश्चिमतो योऽघायुरागच्छति तस्मादघायोः पापकाच्छत्रोर्नोs-
> sस्मान्स्पृहस्पतिः परि पातु । रक्षतु । उतापि चोत्तरस्मादुत्तरतोऽधरादधस्ताच्च
> योऽघायुरागच्छति तस्मादघायोर्नोऽस्मान्सरि पातु । उतापि चेंद्रः पुरस्तातूर्वतोमध्यतश्च योऽघायुरागच्छति तस्मादघायोर्नोऽस्मान्सरि पातु । किंच ।
> सखा सखीभूतेंद्रः सखिभ्यः प्रियेभ्योऽस्मभ्यं वरिवो धनं कृणोतु । करोतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

From the malicious foe (अघायोः (*aghāyoḥ*)) who comes from behind, from the west, may Bṛhaspati protect us all around. And from the malicious foe who comes from the north and from below, may he protect us. Also may Indra protect us from the malicious foe who comes from the front, from the east, and from the middle. Further, may Indra, the friend, bestow wealth (वरिवः (*varivaḥ*)) on us, his dear friends.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- बृहस्पतिः (*bṛhaspatiḥ*)—Bṛhaspati
- पश्चात् (*paścāt*)—from behind us
- अघायोः (*aghāyoḥ*)—from the enemy who wishes evil
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- परि पातु (*pari pātu*)—may he protect
- उत (*uta*)—and
- उत्तरस्मात् (*uttarasmāt*)—from above us
- अधरात् (*adharāt*)—from below; may he protect us from the enemies who fall upon us
- उत (*uta*)—and
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- पुरस्तात् (*purastāt*)—from the east
- मध्यतः (*madhyataḥ*)—from the middle
- नः (*naḥ*)—may he protect us
- सखा (*sakhā*)—Indra, the friend
- सखिभ्यः (*sakhibhyaḥ*)—to us, his friends
- वरिदः (*varidaḥ*)—wealth
- कृणोतु (*kṛṇotu*)—may he provide

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May Bṛhaspati protect us from the enemy who comes from behind us and wishes us evil. May he protect us also from the enemies who fall upon us from above and from below. May Indra protect us from the east and from the middle. May Indra, the friend, provide wealth to us, his friends.

##### English translation
May Brihaspati defend us from a malignant (foe) coming from be-
hind; from above or from below may Indra defend us from foes in front
and in the middle; may he, our friend bestow wealth upon (us) his friends

### Printed p. 25
## Sūkta 43 (Maṇḍala 10)

##### Anuvāda (introduction)

The fourth Anuvāka of the tenth Maṇḍala of the Ṛgveda-saṃhitā contains eighteen sūktas. Of these, the sūkta beginning अच्छा मे (*acchā me*) is the first. It has eleven ṛks. The seer and deity of this sūkta are the same as in the preceding sūkta. The tenth and eleventh ṛks are in the Triṣṭubh metre; the remaining ṛks are in the Jagatī metre. The Anukramaṇikā says: अच्चा मे द्वित्रिष्टुबंतमिति (*accā me dvitriṣṭubaṃtamiti*). In the Ukthya sacrifice this sūkta is employed for the mantras to be recited by the Brāhmaṇācchaṃsin priest at the third pressing. This is explained by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra, in the sūtra अच्चा म इंद्रं बृहस्पते युवमिंद्रश्च वस्व इति याज्ये (*accā ma iṃdraṃ bṛhaspate yuvamiṃdraśca vasva iti yājye*) (Ā. 6-1). It is also to be recited on the sixth day of the sacrifice; this is explained by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra, in the sūtra अच्चा म इंद्रमिति नित्यमैकाहिकं (*accā ma iṃdramiti nityamaikāhikaṃ*) (Ā. 8-3).

##### Particulars

Maṇḍala 10; Anuvāka 4; Sūkta 43; Aṣṭaka 7; Adhyāya 8; Varga 24, 25. Number of ṛks in the sūkta: 11. Ṛṣi: Kṛṣṇa. Devatā: Indra. Metre: 1–9 Jagatī; 10, 11 Triṣṭubh.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अच्छा॑ म॒ इंद्रं॑ म॒तयः॑ स्व॒र्विदः॑ स॒ध्रीची॒र्विश्वा॑ उश॒तीर॑नूषत ।  
> परि॑ ष्वजंते॒ जन॑यो॒ यथा॒ पतिं॒ मर्यं॒ न शुं॒ध्युं म॒घवा॑नमू॒तये॑ ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

### Printed p. 26

> अच्छ । मे । इंद्रं । मतयः । स्वःविदः । सध्रीचीः । विश्वाः । उततीः । अनूषत ।  
> परि । स्वजंते । जनयः । यथा । पतिं । मर्यं । न । शुंध्युं । मघवानं । ऊतये ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आंगिरसस्य कृष्णस्य मे मम स्वर्विदः सर्वस्य लंभयित्र्यः सध्रीजीः संगता
> विश्वा व्याप्ता उशतीः कामयमाना मतयः स्तुतय इंद्रमच्छानूषत । अभिष्टु
> वंति । किंच जनयो जाया यथा पतिं भर्तारं मर्यं न यथा च मनुष्यं
> शुंध्युं शुद्धं दोषरहितं मघवानं धनवंतमूतये रक्षणार्थं परि ष्टजंते
> अलिंगंति तद्वदिंद्रं मे स्तुतयः परि ष्टजंते ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: of me, Kṛṣṇa the Āṅgirasa, the praises (मतयः (*matayaḥ*)) — which win everything (स्वर्विदः (*svarvidaḥ*)), are united (सध्रीचीः (*sadhrīcīḥ*)), pervasive (विश्वाः (*viśvāḥ*)) and desirous (उशतीः (*uśatīḥ*)) — extol Indra. Further, as wives embrace their husband, their protector, and as women embrace a man who is pure, free from fault and wealthy (मघवानं (*maghavānaṃ*)), for the sake of protection (ऊतये (*ūtaye*)), so my praises embrace Indra on all sides.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मे (*me*)—my
- स्वर्विदः (*svarvidaḥ*)—acquiring everything
- सध्रीचीः (*sadhrīcīḥ*)—gathered together
- विश्वाः (*viśvāḥ*)—all-pervading
- उततीः (*utatīḥ*)—desiring the gods
- मतयः (*matayaḥ*)—praises
- इंद्रं अच्छ (*iṃdraṃ accha*)—addressing Indra
- अनूषत (*anūṣata*)—praise
- जनयिः (*janayiḥ*)—wives
- यथा (*yathā*)—in what manner
- मर्यं (*maryaṃ*)—a human
- पतिं न (*patiṃ na*)—as they embrace the husband
- शुंध्युं (*śuṃdhyuṃ*)—the purified
- मघवानं (*maghavānaṃ*)—Indra
- ऊतये (*ūtaye*)—for protection
- परि ष्टजंते (*pari ṣṭajaṃte*)—embrace

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

My praises, which win all desired things, are gathered together, pervade everywhere and desire the gods, extol Indra. As wives embrace their husband, a human being, so my praises embrace the pure Indra for the sake of protection (they go and join him).

##### English translation
My praises all-acquiring, concentrated and eager, glorify Indra ; they embrace Magavat as wives (embrace) a husband : as (women embrace) a man free from defect for the sake of protection.

### Printed p. 27
#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> न घा॑ त्व॒द्रिगप॑ वेति मे॒ मन॒स्त्वे इत्कामं॑ पुरुहूत तिश्रय ।  
> राजे॑व दस्य॒ नि ष॒दोऽधि॑ ब॒र्हिष्य॒स्मिन्नु सोमे॑व॒पान॑मस्तु  
> ते ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> न । घ । त्वद्रिक् । आप । वेति । मे । मनः । श्वे इति । इत् । कामं । पुरुऽहूत ।  
> शिश्रय ।  
> राजाऽइव । दस्य । नि । सदः । अधि । बर्हिषि । अस्मिन् । सु । सोमे । अव्यपानं ।  
> अस्तु । ते ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे पुरुहूत बहुभिराहूत दस्य दर्शनीयेंद्र त्वद्रिक् त्वदभिमुखं मे
> मदीयं मनो न घ न खल्वप वेति । त्वत्तोऽपगच्छति । किंच श्वे इत् त्वय्येव
> कामदुभिलाषं शिश्रय । स्थापयामि । किंच राजीवेश्वर इव बर्हिष्यधि नि षदः ।
> निषीद । किंच ते तवास्मिन्नोमेऽवपानमस्तु । भवतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O Puruhūta, invoked by many, O Dasma, Indra worthy to be seen: my mind, turned toward you, does not turn away from you. Further, I fix my desire (कामं (*kāmaṃ*)) in you alone (त्वे इत् (*tve it*)). Further, sit like a king (an overlord) upon the sacred grass. Further, may the drinking (अवपानं (*avapānaṃ*)) of this Soma be yours.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- दस्म (*dasma*)—possessing an attractive form
- पुरुहूत (*puruhūta*)—O Indra
- त्वद्रिक् (*tvadrik*)—turned toward you
- मे मनः (*me manaḥ*)—my mind
- न घ अप वेशि (*na gha apa veśi*)—surely does not turn back from there
- त्वे इत् (*tve it*)—in you alone
- कामं (*kāmaṃ*)—the desired object
- शिश्रय (*śiśraya*)—I place
- राजीव (*rājīva*)—like a lord
- अधि नि षदः (*adhi ni ṣadaḥ*)—sit down
- ते (*te*)—for you
- आस्मिन् सोमे (*āsmin some*)—in this Soma
- अवपानं अस्तु (*avapānaṃ astu*)—may your act of drinking be accomplished

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, of attractive form and invoked by many: my mind, turned toward you, surely never turns back from you. I fix my desired object in you alone. Sit here like a lord. In this sacrifice may this act of drinking Soma be accomplished for you.

##### English translation
Indra, of godly aspect, the invoked of many, my mind intent on you
wanders not from your presence : I fix my desire on you : take your seat like
a prince upon this sacred grass; and may your drinking be of this Soma.
