#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वि॒षू॒वृदिंद्रो॒ अम॑तेरु॒त क्षु॒धः स इद्रा॒यो म॒घवा॒ वस्य॑  
> ईशते ।  
> तस्येदि॒मे प्र॑व॒णे स॒प्त सिंध॑वो॒ वयो॑ वर्धंति वृष॒भस्य॑ शु॒ष्मिणः॑ ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> विषूवृत् । इंद्रः । अमतेः । उत । क्षुधः । सः । इत् । रायः । मघवा । वस्य ।  
> ईशते ।  
> तस्य । इत् । इमे । प्रवणे । सप्त । सिंधवः । वयः । वर्धंति । वृषभस्य । शुष्मिणः ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इंद्रोऽमतेरस्माकं दुर्बुद्धेः प्रज्ञापहारिण्याः पिसासाया ना विषूवृद्धिष्टगवर्तको भवतु । उतापि च क्षुधो बुभुक्षाया विषूवृद्धवतु । किंच स इत्
> स एव मघवा धनवानिंद्रो वसो वासयितू रायो धनस्येशते । ईष्टे । किंच
> वृषभस्य वर्षितुस्तस्येदिंद्रस्येवेमे प्रसिद्धाः सप्त स्तप्तसंख्याकाः सिंधवो नद्यः
> प्रवणे देशे वयोऽन्नं वर्धंति । वर्धयंति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: may Indra be the remover (विषूवृत् (*viṣūvṛt*)) of our evil-mindedness (अमतेः (*amateḥ*)), which robs the understanding, पिसासाया (*pisāsāyā*) **NOTE**, and also of hunger (क्षुधः (*kṣudhaḥ*), the desire to eat). Further, that same wealthy Indra is lord (ईशते (*īśate*)) of wealth (रायः (*rāyaḥ*)) that makes dwelling possible. Further, of that same showerer Indra, these seven well-known rivers cause food (वयः (*vayaḥ*)) to increase in the low-lying (प्रवणे (*pravaṇe*)) country.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- अमतेः (*amateḥ*)—of our thirst, the cause of bad thinking
- विषूवृत् (*viṣūvṛt*)—let him be the remover
- उत (*uta*)—and
- क्षुधः (*kṣudhaḥ*)—of hunger too, the remover
- स इत् मघवा (*sa it maghavā*)—that same wealthy Indra
- वस्वः (*vasvaḥ*)—causing habitation
- रायः (*rāyaḥ*)—of wealth
- ईशते (*īśate*)—is the lord
- वृषभस्य (*vṛṣabhasya*)—the giver of rain
- तस्य इत् (*tasya it*)—belonging to that same Indra
- सप्त (*sapta*)—seven in number
- इमे सिंधवः (*ime siṃdhavaḥ*)—these rivers
- प्रवणे (*pravaṇe*)—in the lower regions
- वयः (*vayaḥ*)—food
- वर्धंति (*vardhaṃti*)—grow

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May Indra be the remover of thirst and hunger, which are the causes of our evil-mindedness. That wealthy Indra is lord of wealth that makes dwelling possible. These seven rivers, belonging to that same rain-giving Indra, grow food in the low-lying regions.

### Printed p. 29

##### English translation
May Indra be the remover of thirst and hunger, for he, Maghavat,
is lord over precious riches: these seven rivers of the powerful showerer,
(Indra) flowing down a declivity, augment food.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वयो॒ न वृ॒क्षं सु॑पला॒शमास॑द॒न्नोमा॑स॒ इंद्रं॑ म॒ुंदिन॑श्चमू॒षदः॑ ।  
> प्रैषा॒मनी॑कं॒ शव॑सा॒ दवि॑द्युतद्वि॒दत्स्व१॒॑ १ र्मन॑वे॒ ज्योति॒रार्यं॑ ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वयः । न । वृक्षं । सुपलाशं । आ । असदन् । सोमासः । इंद्रं । मुंदिनः । चमूसदः ।  
> प्र । एषां । अनीकं । शवसा । दविद्युतत् । विदत् । स्वः । मनवे । ज्योतिः । आर्यं ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वयो न यथा दक्षिणः सुपलाशं शोभनपर्णं वृक्षमासीदंति तद्वन्मंदिनो
> मदकराश्च मूषदश्च मसपात्रेषु स्थातारः सोमासः सोमा इंद्रमासदन् । आसीदंति । अधितिष्ठंतीत्यर्थः । एषामिंद्रमधितिष्ठतां सोमानां शवसा वेगेन युक्तमनीकमग्रं प्र दविद्युतत् । प्रद्योतते । किंचायमिंद्रः स्वः स्वात्मनार्यं
> प्रेय्यं ज्योतिरादित्याख्यं तेजो मनवे मनुष्येभ्यो विदत् । प्रयच्छतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: as birds settle on a tree with good leaves (सुपलाशं (*supalāśaṃ*)), so the Soma juices — exhilarating (मुंदिनः (*muṃdinaḥ*)) and abiding in the ladles (चमूषदः (*camūṣadaḥ*)) — settle upon Indra, that is, take their place upon him. The front part (अनीकं (*anīkaṃ*)) of these Somas that rest upon Indra, endowed with speed (शवसा (*śavasā*)), shines forth (प्र दविद्युतत् (*pra davidyutat*)). Further, may this Indra of himself (स्वः (*svaḥ*)) give to Manu, to men (मनवे (*manave*)), the light (ज्योतिः (*jyotiḥ*)) called Āditya, the radiance to be impelled (आर्यं (*āryaṃ*), explained as प्रेय्यं (*preyyaṃ*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सुपलाशं (*supalāśaṃ*)—having excellent leaves
- वृक्षं (*vṛkṣaṃ*)—resorting to the tree
- वयः न (*vayaḥ na*)—as birds sit
- मुंदिनः (*muṃdinaḥ*)—causing delight
- चमूषदः (*camūṣadaḥ*)—being in the ladle
- सोमासः (*somāsaḥ*)—Soma juices
- इंद्रं आ असदन् (*iṃdraṃ ā asadan*)—resort to Indra and join him
- एषां (*eṣāṃ*)—of these Soma juices
- शवसा (*śavasā*)—with speed
- अनीकं (*anīkaṃ*)—the front part
- प्र दविद्युतत् (*pra davidyutat*)—shines
- स्वः (*svaḥ*)—by himself
- आर्यं (*āryaṃ*)—impelled
- ज्योतिः (*jyotiḥ*)—brilliance
- मनवे (*manave*)—for human beings
- विदत् (*vidat*)—let him give

### Printed p. 30

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

As birds settle on a tree with excellent leaves, so the Soma juices, which cause delight and abide in the ladle, resort to Indra and join him. The front part of these swift Soma juices shines forth. May Indra distribute to human beings the brilliance impelled by himself.

##### English translation
As birds perch upon the leafy tree, so the exhilarating Soma juices filling the ladles (repair) to (Indra), the summit of them shines through their speed, may Indra spontaneously bestow excellent light upon man.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> कृ॒तं न श्व॒घ्नी वि चि॑नोति॒ देव॑ने सं॒वर्गं॒ यन्म॒घवा॒ सूर्यं॒  ॑
> जयत् ।  
> न तत्ते॑ अ॒न्नो अनु॑ वी॒र्यं॑ शक॒न्न पु॑रा॒णो म॑घव॒न्नोत  ॑
> नूतनः ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कृतं । न । श्वघ्नी । वि । चिनोति । देवने । संवर्गं । यत् । मघवा । सूर्यं । जयत् ।  
> न । तत् । ते । अन्यः । अनु । वीर्यं । शकत् । न । पुराणः । मघवन् । न । उत ।  
> नूतनः ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> श्वघ्नी परस्वानां हंता कितवो देवने द्यूते कृतं न यथा कृतं वि
> चिनोति तद्वदिंद्रः सूर्यं मृगयते । कदेत्यत आह । यद्यदा मघवा धनवानिंद्रः संवर्गं समग्वृष्टीर्वर्जयितारं सूर्यमादित्यं जयत् तदीयरसापहरणद्वारेण जयति । अथ प्रत्यक्षस्तुतिः । हे मघवन्धनवन्निंद्र ते तव तत्सूर्यजयलक्षणं वीर्यं वीरत्वमन्यस्त्वत्तोऽन्नो भविष्यत्कालीनः पुमानन्तनुकर्तुं न

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: as the gambler (श्वघ्नी (*śvaghnī*), one who destroys the property of others) at the game (देवने (*devane*)) of dice picks out the winning throw (कृतं (*kṛtaṃ*)), so Indra seeks out the Sun. When? He says: when (यत् (*yat*)) the wealthy Indra wins (जयत् (*jayat*)) the Sun, the Āditya, the संवर्गं (*saṃvargaṃ*) **NOTE**, he conquers him by taking away his essence. Then follows a direct address: O wealthy Indra, that heroism of yours, the winning of the Sun, no other man (अन्यः (*anyaḥ*)) of the future can imitate …

### Printed p. 31

> शकत् । न शक्नोति । पुराणः पूर्वोऽपि त्वदीय्यमनुकर्तुं न शकत् । न शक्नोति ।
> उतापि च नूतनो वर्तमानकालीनोऽपि त्वदीयं वीर्यमनुकर्तुं न शक्नोति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa completes his gloss of the verse. No other man, whether ancient or modern, is able to imitate Indra's heroism.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- कृष्णी (*kṛṣṇī*)—the gambler
- देवने (*devane*)—in the game of dice
- कृतं (*kṛtaṃ*)—waiting for the moment
- विचिनोति न (*vicinoti na*)—as he picks out his opponent and defeats him
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- मघवा (*maghavā*)—Indra
- संवर्गं (*saṃvargaṃ*)—the one who pours down rain
- सूर्यं (*sūryaṃ*)—picks out the sun; then
- जयत् (*jayat*)—he conquers him by seizing away his essence
- मघवन् (*maghavan*)—O Indra
- ते (*te*)—your
- तत् (*tat*)—that well-known
- वीर्यं (*vīryaṃ*)—valour
- अन्यः (*anyaḥ*)—anyone else
- अनु (*anu*)—to imitate
- न शकत् (*na śakat*)—is not able
- पुराणः न (*purāṇaḥ na*)—nor one of former times is able
- उत (*uta*)—and
- नूतनः (*nūtanaḥ*)—nor one of the present is able

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

As a gambler at play waits for the moment, picks out his opponent and defeats him, so when Indra picks out the sun, the sender of rain, he conquers him by seizing away his essence. O Indra, no one, whether of former times or of the present, can imitate your well-known valour.

##### English translation
As a gambler at play selects his adversary, so does Maghavat when he overcomes the rain-bestowing sun : no one, Maghavat, ancient or modern is able to imitate this your vigour.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In passages that show valour or cruelty, the comparison with the श्वघ्नि (*śvaghni*), one who has valour or one who cheats, is shown in many contexts. Sāyaṇa gives this word different meanings in different contexts. It is made of two words, श्वान् (*śvān*) and घ्न (*ghna*), and घ्न (*ghna*) is formed from the root हन् (*han*). The word can be explained either as श्वानं हंति (*śvānaṃ haṃti*), "he kills a dog", or as शुना (सह) हंति (*śunā (saha) haṃti*), "he kills together with a dog". In the first three places of its use Sāyaṇa gives the word the meaning "hunter" or "one who pursues game".
पुनःपुनर्जायमाना पुराणी समानं वर्णमभि शुंभमाना । कृत्स्नव कृत्सुर्विज आमिनाना मर्तस्य देवी जरयंत्यायुः ॥ (*punaḥpunarjāyamānā purāṇī samānaṃ varṇamabhi śuṃbhamānā | kṛtsnava kṛtsurvija āminānā martasya devī jarayaṃtyāyuḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-92-10)

### Printed p. 32
This verse describes Ushas. Just as the श्वघ्नि (*śvaghni*) is cruel, so Ushas too, pitiless, brings all creatures under the sway of old age and harms them; this is the sense of the sentence. Sāyaṇa explains: शुना मृगान् हतवान् (*śunā mṛgān hatavān*), he who has killed game with a dog is श्वहा (*śvahā*), and his wife is श्वघ्नी (*śvaghnī*), the wife of a hunter who kills game by means of dogs. Likewise in शृघ्नद यो जिगिनास् (*śṛghnada yo jiginās*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-12-4), where the subject is Indra defeating and harming his enemies, Indra is compared to a cruel hunter. There too this same meaning is given. In other contexts, however, the same word is given another meaning. For example:
एवारे वृषभा सुशेऽसिन्वन्भूरादयः । श्श्विनेद विवता चरस् ॥ (*evāre vṛṣabhā suśe 'sinvanbhūrādayaḥ | śśvineda vivatā caras ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-43-44 **NOTE** 45-46 **NOTE**) **NOTE**
Here too Indra is praised. In this context Sāyaṇa, quoting श्वघ्नी कितवो भवति स्वं हंति (*śvaghnī kitavo bhavati svaṃ haṃti*) (Ni. 5.22), gives the word the meaning of one who cheats: one who seizes the property of others. Many English scholars too have expressed the opinion that the meaning of this word has been wrongly taken. Since a gambler is commonly one who loses his own property and takes the property of others, the word श्वघ्नी (*śvaghnī*) may be said to mean a gambler. But the original sense of the words श्वन् (*śvan*) (dog) and हन् (*han*) (kill) does not here give the general sense; this derivation is possible only if the meaning "hunter" is taken. Yet the meaning "hunter" is not possible in the places where this word occurs ... **NOTE**. The question naturally arises whether a gambler can have the epithet श्वघ्न (*śvaghna*). It is possible that Sāyaṇa gives this meaning on the strength of a Gṛhya-sūtra. There is a section called "श्वग्रहप्रायश्चित्त (*śvagrahaprāyaścitta*)" for destroying diseases such as epilepsy. As the name itself shows, young children suffer epilepsy and similar diseases through the affliction of an evil seizer (श्व ग्रह (*śva graha*)). In the Hiraṇyakeśi and Pāraskara Gṛhya-sūtras the expiation of this fault is prescribed as follows.
The father of the afflicted child performs the rite according to rule, takes the child to the gambling-place of the gamblers and performs certain prescribed acts there. Then he lays the child upon the dice-board and recites mantras meaning "O dog, go far away". In this rite the gambler is an assistant. Since the gambler helps in it, he is as it were the destroyer of the dog-seizer; hence he too became कृष्ण (*kṛṣṇa*) **NOTE**. On this basis it may be said that Sāyaṇa (also showing the authority of Yāska's sentence) explains the word त्वघ्निप् (*tvaghnip*) **NOTE** as gambler. (15-2-37)

### Printed p. 33
उत प्रहामतिदीव्या जयाति कृतं यच्छ्वघ्नी विचिनोति काले । यो देवकामो न धना रुणद्धि समित्तं राया सृजति स्वधावान् ॥ (*uta prahāmatidīvyā jayāti kṛtaṃ yacchvaghnī vicinoti kāle | yo devakāmo na dhanā ruṇaddhi samittaṃ rāyā sṛjati svadhāvān ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-42-9)
In this and similar passages too, Indra's doings are compared with the doings of a gambler, such as his search for his man. Here the standard and the thing compared are consistent.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> विशं॑विशं म॒घवा॒ पर्य॑शायत॒ जना॑नां॒ धेना॑ अव॒चाक॑श॒द्वृषा॑  
> यस्याह॑ श॒क्रः सव॑नेषु॒ रण्य॑ति॒ स ती॒व्रैः सोमैः॑ सहते पृतन्य॒तः ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> विशंविशं । मघवा । परि । अशायत । जनानां । धेनाः । अव्यचाकशत् । वृषा ।  
> यस्य । आह । शक्रः । सवनेषु । रण्यति । सः । तीव्रैः । सोमैः । सहते । पृतन्यतः ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वृषा कामानां वर्षिता मघवा धनवानिंद्रो विशंविशं सर्वान्मनुष्यास्पर्ययशायत । परिशेते । किंच जनानां स्तोतृजनानां धेनाः स्तुतीरवचाकशत् ।
> अभिपश्यति । किंच शक्र इंद्रो यस्याह यस्यैव यजमानस्य सवनेषु रण्यति रमते
> स यजमानस्तीव्रैस्तीव्रकरैः प्रदीयमानैः सोमैः पृतन्यतः संग्राममिच्छतः
> तत्रून् सहते । अभिभवति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Indra, the showerer of desires and possessor of wealth, lies around every people. He looks upon the praises of the praising folk. Indra delights in the sacrifices of whichever sacrificer; that sacrificer overcomes foes who seek battle, by means of the potent Soma offered to Indra.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वृषा (*vṛṣā*)—the giver of desired things
- मघवा (*maghavā*)—Indra, the wealthy
- विशंवितं (*viśaṃvitaṃ*)—every single man
- पर्यशायत (*paryaśāyata*)—enters
- जनानां (*janānāṃ*)—of the praisers
- धेनाः (*dhenāḥ*)—the praises
- अवचाकशत् (*avacākaśat*)—examines closely
- शक्रः (*śakraḥ*)—Indra
- यस्याह (*yasyāha*)—of whichever sacrificer
- सवनेषु (*savaneṣu*)—in the sacrifice
- रण्यति (*raṇyati*)—he rejoices
- सः (*saḥ*)—that sacrificer
- तीव्रैः (*tīvraiḥ*)—with keen, potent
- सोमैः (*somaiḥ*)—Somas
- पृतस्यतः (*pṛtasyataḥ*)—enemies
- सहते (*sahate*)—defeats

### Printed p. 34

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra, the giver of desired things and wealthy, enters the heart of every man. He examines closely the praises of the praisers. The sacrificer in whose sacrifices Indra rejoices defeats his enemies by drinking the Soma of keen potency.

##### English translation
Maghavat, the showerer (of benefits), encompasses all people : he contemplates the praises of mankind : the man in whose sacrifice Shakra delights, overcomes (foes) wishing to fight by means of his pungent Soma libations.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आपो॒ न सिंधु॑म॒भि यत्स॒मक्ष॑र॒न्सोमा॑स॒ इंद्रं॑ कु॒ल्या इ॑व  ॒
> ह्रदं ।  
> वर्धं॑ति॒ विप्रा॒ महो॑ अस्य॒ साद॑ने॒ यवं॒ न वृ॒ष्टिर्दि॒व्येन॒ दानु॑ना ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आपः । न । सिंधुं । अभि । यत् । संअक्षरन् । सोमासः । इंद्रं । कुल्याः इद ।  
> ह्रदं ।  
> वर्धंति । विप्राः । महः । अस्य । सदने । यवं । न । वृष्टिः । दिव्येन । दानुना ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यद्यदा सोमासः सोमा इंद्रमभि समक्षरन् अभिक्षरंति । अभिगच्छंतीत्कर्थः । तत्र दृष्टांतौ दर्शयति । आपो न यथापः सिंधुं समुद्रमभिसंक्षरंति ।
> कुल्या इनयथा भकुल्या प्रदमभिसंक्षरंति तद्वदित्यर्थः । तदानीमस्येंद्रस्य
> महो महत्त्वं तदने यज्ञगृहे विप्रा मेधाविनो वर्धंति । तत्र दृष्टांतमाह ।
> यवं न यथायवं यवसस्यं व्यष्टिः ॥ कर्तरि कृत् ॥ वर्षिता वर्जन्यो दिव्येन
> दिविधवेन दानुना दानेन वर्षणेन वर्धयति तद्वदित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

When the Somas flow towards Indra, as waters flow to the sea and as channels flow to a lake, then at the sacrificial house the wise worshippers increase this Indra's greatness. The illustration: as the rain-giver, Parjanya, makes the barley grow by his heavenly gift, the rain, so they increase him.

### Printed p. 35

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- आपः (*āpaḥ*)—waters
- सिंधुं स (*siṃdhuṃ sa*)—as they join a river
- तुल्याः (*tulyāḥ*)—channels
- प्रदं इव (*pradaṃ iva*)—as they join the seas
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- सोमासः (*somāsaḥ*)—Soma juices
- इंद्रं अभि (*iṃdraṃ abhi*)—towards Indra
- समक्षरन् (*samakṣaran*)—flow
- दिव्येन (*divyena*)—born of heaven
- दासुना (*dāsunā*)—by the rain
- वृष्टिः (*vṛṣṭiḥ*)—the rain
- यवं स (*yavaṃ sa*)—as it makes barley grow
- विप्राः (*viprāḥ*)—the wise
- अस्य (*asya*)—of this Indra
- महः (*mahaḥ*)—the greatness
- सदने (*sadane*)—in the sacrificial house
- वर्धंति (*vardhaṃti*)—they increase

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

When the Soma juices flow towards Indra, as waters join a river and channels join the seas, then the wise praisers increase Indra's greatness in the sacrificial house, as the rain, born of heaven, nourishes the barley.

##### English translation
When the Soma juices flow towards Indra, as waters to the sea, as rivers to a lake, then the pious worshippers augment his greatness in the (sacrificial) abode, as the rain by its heavenly gift, (augments) the barley.

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वृषा॒ न क्रु॒द्धः प॑तय॒द्रजः॒ स्वा यो अ॒र्यप॑त्नि॒रकृ॑णोदि॒मा  ॒
> अपः ।  
> स सु॑न्व॒ते म॒घवा॑ जी॒रदा॑न॒वेऽविं॑द॒ज्योति॒र्मन॑वे ह॒विष्म॑ते॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वृषा । न । क्रुद्धः । पतयत् । रजिसु । आ । यः । अर्यपत्निः । अकृणोत् ।  
> इमाः । अपः ।  
> सः । सुन्वते । मघवा । जीरदानवे । अविंदत् । ज्योतिः । मनदे । हविष्मते ॥८॥

### Printed p. 36

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> रजिसु लोकेषु वृषा न यथा वृषभः क्रुद्धः सन् प्रतिवृषभदधाय पतयद्दच्छति तद्वद्य इंद्रः क्रुद्धः सन्मेघवधाय पतयति हत्वा च मेघमर्यपत्निः
> सर्वस्य जगतः स्वामिन इंद्रस्य पालयितव्या इमाः प्रसिद्धा अपो दृष्ट्त्युदकान्याकृणोत् अकृणोति अस्मदभिमुखं करोति स मघवा धनवानिंद्रः सुस्वते सोमभिषवं कुर्वते जीरदानवे क्षिप्रदानाय हविष्मते मनदे मनुष्याय । यजमानायेत्यर्थः । ज्योतिस्तेजोऽविंदत् । प्रायच्छत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In the worlds, as an angry bull rushes to destroy a rival bull, so Indra, angry, rushes to slay the cloud. Having slain the cloud, he makes these well-known waters, the rain-waters to be guarded, belonging to Indra, the lord of the whole world, turn towards us. That wealthy Indra gives light, splendour, to the man who presses Soma, gives quickly and has oblations, that is, to the sacrificer.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- रजिसु (*rajisu*)—in the worlds
- क्रुद्धः (*kruddhaḥ*)—angry
- वृषा (*vṛṣā*)—the bull
- पतयत् स (*patayat sa*)—as it rushes forward and charges
- यः (*yaḥ*)—when the angry Indra rushes forward against the clouds
- आर्यपत्निः (*āryapatniḥ*)—the nourishing, of Indra, the protector of the world
- इमा अपः (*imā apaḥ*)—these rain-waters
- आकृणोत् (*ākṛṇot*)—fall towards us
- समघवा (*samaghavā*)—that same Indra
- सुस्वते (*susvate*)—to the presser of Soma
- जीरदानवे (*jīradānave*)—one who gives oblations quickly
- हविष्कृते (*haviṣkṛte*)—who has oblations
- मनवे (*manave*)—to the sacrificer
- ज्योतिः (*jyotiḥ*)—splendour
- अविंदत् (*aviṃdat*)—provides

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

As in the world an angry bull rushes forward and charges, so when the angry Indra rushes forward against the clouds, these rain-waters, the nourishers of Indra, protector of the world, fall towards us. That same Indra provides splendour to the sacrificer who presses Soma and quickly makes offerings.

##### English translation
As in the world an angry bull rushes (upon another), so does Indra, who directs (towards us) the waters, the brides of the lord (of earth): when (the Soma) is effused Maghavat bestows light upon the man, who gives promptly, who offers oblations.

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उज्जा॑यतां पर॒शुज्योति॑षा स॒ह भू॒या य॒तस्य॑ सु॒दुघा॑  ॒
> पुराण॒वत् ।

### Printed p. 37

> वि रोचतामरुषो भानुना शुचिः स्वर्ण शुक्रं शुशुचीत
> सत्पतिः ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उत् । जायतां । परशुः । ज्योतिषा । सह । भूयाः । यतस्य । सुदुघा । पुराणवत् ।  
> वि । रोचितां । अरुषः । भानुना । शुबिः । स्वः । न । शुक्रं । शुशुचीत । सर्पतिः ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> परशुरिंद्रस्य वज्रो ज्योतिषा तेजसा सहोज्णायतां । अस्मच्छत्रुवधे
> मेघवधे च व्याप्रियशामित्यर्थः । किंचर्तस्य सत्यस्य सुदुघा माध्यमिका वाक्
> पुराणवत् प्रश्नवत्काले भूयाः । भूयात् ॥ पुरुषव्यत्ययः ॥ किंचेंद्रोऽरुष
> आरोचमानो भानुना दीप्ता शुचिर्ज्वलन् वि रोचतां । प्रकाशतां । किंच
> सत्पतिः सतां पतिरिंद्रः स्वर्नादित्य इव शुक्रं ज्वलञ्जुशुचीत । अत्यंतं दीप्यतां ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The axe (परशुरिंद्रस्य वज्रो (*paraśuriṃdrasya vajro*)) is Indra's thunderbolt. Let it be born together with its light and splendour, active for the slaying of our enemy and for the slaying of the cloud. Further, let the well-milking speech of the middle region, belonging to truth (ऋतस्य (*ṛtasya*)), come into being "as of old" (पुराणवत् (*purāṇavat*), glossed with a word that reads प्रश्नवत्काले (*praśnavatkāle*)). **NOTE** Sāyaṇa says भूयाः (*bhūyāḥ*) stands for भूयात् (*bhūyāt*) (a change of person). Further, let Indra, ruddy and shining, bright and blazing with his radiance, shine forth. And let Indra, lord of the good, shine like the sun, blazing brightly, and burn exceedingly.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- परशुः (*paraśuḥ*)—Indra's thunderbolt
- ज्योतिषा सह (*jyotiṣā saha*)—together with splendour
- उज्जायशां (*ujjāyaśāṃ*)—let it come into being together
- ऋतस्य (*ṛtasya*)—belonging to truth
- सुदुघा (*sudughā*)—the speech of the middle region
- पुराणवत् (*purāṇavat*)—as before
- भूयाः (*bhūyāḥ*)—let it be made radiant
- अरुषः (*aruṣaḥ*)—Indra, whose form is attractive
- भानुना (*bhānunā*)—by his own splendour
- शुचिः (*śuciḥ*)—pure
- वि रोचतां (*vi rocatāṃ*)—let him shine
- सत्पतिः (*satpatiḥ*)—Indra, the protector of good men
- स्वर्न (*svarna*)—like the sun
- शुक्रं (*śukraṃ*)—bright
- शुशुचेत (*śuśuceta*)—let him shine exceedingly

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Let Indra's thunderbolt come into being together with splendour; let the speech of the middle region, belonging to truth, be made radiant as before. Let Indra, of attractive form, shine pure with his own splendour; let Indra, protector of good men, shine exceedingly bright like the sun.

##### English translation
Let the axe (of Indra) be put forth together with light: may the easily milked cow of truth be (manifested) as of old; may the pure and radiant Indra shine with splendour; may the lord of the virtuous shine brightly like the sun.

### Printed p. 38
#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> गोभि॑ष्टरे॒माम॑तिं दु॒रेवां॒ यवे॑न॒ क्षुधं॑ पुरुहूत॒ विश्वां॑ ।  
> व॒यं राज॑भिः प्रथ॒मा धना॑न्य॒स्माके॑न वृ॒जने॑ना जयेम ॥१०॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> गोभिः । तरेम । अमतिं । दुःएवां । यवेन । क्षुधं । पुरुहूत । विश्वां ।  
> वयं । राजभिः । प्रथमाः । धनानि । अस्माकेन । वृजनेन । जयेम ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे पुरुहूत बहुभिराहूतेंद्र तव प्रसादाद्दुरेवां दुष्टागमनां
> दारिद्र्यादागताममतिं दुर्बुद्धि वयं घौषेयाः सुहस्ता गोभिः पशुभिस्तरेम ।
> निस्तरेम । किंच यवेन विश्वां व्याप्तां क्षुधं शरेम । किंच प्रथमा मुख्यानि
> धनानि राजभिर्धनानामीश्वरैः । लभेमहीति शेषः । यद्वा । प्रथमा वयमिति
> संबंधः । किंचास्माकेनास्मदीयेन वृजनेन बलेन जयेम । शत्रूनिति शेषः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, invoked by many (पुरुहूत (*puruhūta*)), by your grace may we, (called) घौषेयाः (*ghauṣeyāḥ*) and सुहस्ता (*suhastā*), cross over with cattle the ill-mind (अमतिं (*amatiṃ*)) that comes of poverty and bad ways of approach (दुरेवां (*durevāṃ*)). **NOTE** Further, with barley let us cross the all-pervading hunger. Further, may we obtain the foremost riches through kings, lords of riches ("may we obtain" is to be supplied). Or else, the construction is "we the first". Further, by our own strength (वृजनेन (*vṛjanena*) = force) may we conquer (enemies are to be supplied).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पुरुहूत (*puruhūta*)—O Indra, called by many
- दुरेवां (*durevāṃ*)—that came of poverty
- अमतिं (*amatiṃ*)—the ill-mind
- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we
- गोभिः (*gobhiḥ*)—with the help of our wealth of cattle
- तरेम (*tarema*)—may we cross over
- यवेन (*yavena*)—with the help of barley
- विश्वां (*viśvāṃ*)—that surrounds us
- क्षुधं (*kṣudhaṃ*)—may we cross over hunger
- प्रथमाः (*prathamāḥ*)—most important
- धनानि (*dhanāni*)—riches
- राजभिः (*rājabhiḥ*)—through the lords of wealth (may we obtain)
- अस्माकेन (*asmākena*)—our
- वृजनेन (*vṛjanena*)—by strength
- जयेम (*jayema*)—may we conquer the enemies

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, invoked by many, may we cross over, with our wealth of cattle, the ill-mind that has come of poverty. With the help of barley may we destroy the hunger that surrounds us. May we obtain the foremost riches from the wealthy. By our strength may we conquer our enemies.

##### English translation
Indra, invoked of many may we escape all ignorance caused by poverty by means of our cattle : (may we escape) all hunger by means of

### Printed p. 39
our barley : (may we acquire) the chief riches through the princes (of wealth) : may we conquer by our valour.

#### Ṛk 11

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> बृह॒स्पति॑र्नः॒ परि॑ पातु प॒श्चादु॒तोत्त॑रस्मा॒दध॑रादघा॒योः ।  
> इंद्रः॑ पु॒रस्ता॑दु॒त म॑ध्य॒तो नः॒ सखा॒ सखि॑भ्यो॒ वरि॑वः कृणोतु

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> बृहस्पतिः । नः । परि । पातु । पश्चात् । उत । उत्तरस्मात् । अधरात् । अघयोः।  
> इंद्रः । पुरस्तात् । उत । मध्यतः । नः । सखा । सखिभ्यः । वरिवः । कृणोतु ॥ ११ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> पश्चात्पश्चिमतो योऽघायुरागच्छति तस्मादघायोः पापकाच्छत्रोर्नोS-
> Sस्मान्स्पृहस्पतिः परि पातु । रक्षतु । उतापि चोत्तरस्मादुत्तरतोऽधरादधस्ताच्च
> योऽघायुरागच्छति तस्मादघायोर्नोऽस्मान्परि पातु । उतापि चेंद्रः पुरस्तात्पूर्वतोमध्यतश्च योऽघायुरागच्छति तस्मादघायोर्नोऽस्मान्परि पातु । किंच ।
> सखा सखभूतेंद्रः सखिभ्यः प्रियेभ्योऽस्मभ्यं वरिवो धनं कृणोतु । करोतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

From behind, from the west, whatever malicious enemy comes: from that malicious, sinful enemy may Bṛhaspati (the text reads a damaged form of the name) protect us all round. Likewise from the north and from below, whoever comes with malice: from that malicious one may he protect us. And may Indra, from before (the east) and from the middle, protect us from whatever malicious one comes. Further, may Indra, the friend, grant wealth (वरिवः (*varivaḥ*)) to us, his dear friends.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- बृहस्पतिः (*bṛhaspatiḥ*)—Bṛhaspati
- पश्चात् (*paścāt*)—from behind us
- अघायोः (*aghāyoḥ*)—from the enemy who desires evil
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- परि पातु (*pari pātu*)—let him protect
- उत (*uta*)—and
- उत्तरस्मात् (*uttarasmāt*)—from above us
- अधरात् (*adharāt*)—let him protect us from the enemies who fall upon us from below
- उत (*uta*)—and
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- पुरस्तात् (*purastāt*)—from the east
- मध्यतः (*madhyataḥ*)—from the middle
- नः (*naḥ*)—let him protect us
- सखा (*sakhā*)—Indra, the friend
- सखिभ्यः (*sakhibhyaḥ*)—to us, his friends
- वरिवः (*varivaḥ*)—wealth
- कृणोतु (*kṛṇotu*)—let him provide

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May Bṛhaspati protect us from the enemy who comes from behind and desires evil for us; may he protect us also from the enemies who fall upon us from above and from below.

### Printed p. 40
May Indra protect us from the east and from the middle. May Indra, the friend, provide wealth to us, his friends.

##### English translation
May Brihaspati defend us from a malignant (foe) coming from behind, from above, or from below ; may Indra defend us from foes in front and in the middle; may he, our friend bestow wealth upon (us) his friends

## Sūkta 44 (Maṇḍala 10)

> आ यात्वित्येकादशर्चं द्वितीयं सूक्तमांगिरसस्य कृष्णस्यार्षद्मंद्रं । आदिकस्तिस्रस्त्रिष्टुभः । ततः षड्जगत्यः । ततो द्वे त्रिष्टुभौ । तथा चानुक्रांतं । आ यातु त्रित्रिष्टुबादीति ॥ अभिप्लविकेषूक्ष्येषु ब्राह्मणाच्छंसिनः स्तोमवृद्ध्भाविदमावापार्थं
> सूश्रितं च । आ यात्सिंद्रः स्वपतिरिमां धियमिति ब्राह्मणाच्छंसी । आ-३-९ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This is the second sūkta, of eleven Ṛks, with आ यातु (*ā yātu*) as its first words. The seer is Kṛṣṇa of the Āṅgirasa line; the deity is Indra. The first three verses are Triṣṭubh, then six are Jagatī, then the two that follow are Triṣṭubh. So the Anukramaṇī: आ यातु त्रित्रिष्टुबादीति (*ā yātu tritriṣṭubādīti*). In the Abhiplava Ukthya rites it is to be used by the Brāhmaṇācchaṃsin for the increase of the stoma, to supply (the missing verses); the Āśvalāyana sūtra beginning with the words of Ṛk 1 and ending with the Brāhmaṇācchaṃsin is cited (Ā. Sū. 3-9). **NOTE**

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta beginning "आ यातु (*ā yātu*)" is the second sūkta in the fourth anuvāka. It has eleven Ṛks. Its seer is Aṅgirasaḥ Kṛṣṇaḥ; the deity is Indra. The first three Ṛks of this sūkta are in the Triṣṭubh metre; the six that follow (4–9) are in Jagatī; the next two (10–11) are Triṣṭubh. In the Anukramaṇī it is said "आ यातु त्रित्रिष्टुबादि (*ā yātu tritriṣṭubādi*)". In the Ukthya sacrifices connected with the Abhiplava, the sūkta is used by the priest called Brāhmaṇācchaṃsin as a supplement (avāpa) among the mantras to be recited for the increase of the stoma; this follows from the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, "Ā yātvindraḥ svapatir imāṃ dhiyam iti brāhmaṇācchaṃsī" (Ā. 7-9).

##### Particulars

Maṇḍala 10; Anuvāka 4; Sūkta 44; Aṣṭaka 7; Adhyāya 8; Varga 26, 27. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 11. Seer: Kṛṣṇa. Deity: Indra. Metre: 1–4, 10, 11 (name garbled in the print); 4–9 Jagatī. **NOTE**

### Printed p. 41
#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ या॒त्त्विंद्रः॒ स्वप॑ति॒र्मदा॑य॒ यो धर्म॑णा तूतुज्ञा॒नस्तुवि॑ष्मान् ।  
> प्र॒त्त्व॒क्षा॒णो अति॒ विश्वा॒ सहां॑स्यपा॒रेण॑ मह॒ता वृष्णे॑न ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । यात्तु । इंद्रः । स्वपतिः । मदाय । यः । धर्मणा । तूतुजानः । तुविष्मान् ।  
> प्रत्वक्षणः । अति । विश्वा । सहांसि । अपारेण । महता । वृष्ट्येन ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> य इंद्रस्तूतुजानस्वरमाणस्तुविष्मान्वृद्धिमान्विश्वा विश्वानि सहांसि शत्रुबलान्यपारेण महताः केन वृष्ट्येन ऒलेन प्रत्यक्षाणः प्रकर्षेण तनूकुर्वन्नति ।
> गच्छतीति शेषः । स्वपतिर्धनपतिरिंद्रो मदाय मदार्थं धर्मणा धारकेण रथेना
> यातु । अस्मद्यज्ञं प्रत्यागच्छतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The Indra who is swift (तूतुजानः (*tūtujānaḥ*) = hastening), strong (तुविष्मान् (*tuviṣmān*) = grown great), who overcomes all (विश्वा (*viśvā*)) the hostile forces (सहांसि (*sahāṃsi*) = enemy powers) with his boundless, great manly strength (वृष्ट्येन (*vṛṣṭyena*) = by strength), going beyond them (अति (*ati*), "goes" is to be supplied): that Indra, lord of wealth (स्वपतिः (*svapatiḥ*)), for exhilaration, let him come with his supporting chariot (धर्मणा (*dharmaṇā*) = धारकेण रथेन (*dhārakeṇa rathena*)). Let him come to our sacrifice.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यः (*yaḥ*)—which Indra
- तूतुजानः (*tūtujānaḥ*)—swift-moving
- तुविष्मान् (*tuviṣmān*)—powerful
- विश्वा (*viśvā*)—all
- सहांसि (*sahāṃsi*)—the forces of the enemy
- अपारेण (*apāreṇa*)—abundant
- महता (*mahatā*)—great
- वृष्ट्येन (*vṛṣṭyena*)—by his own strength
- प्रत्वक्षणः (*pratvakṣaṇaḥ*)—making (them) weak
- अति (*ati*)—who surpasses them, such
- स्वपतिः (*svapatiḥ*)—the lord of wealth
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- मदाय (*madāya*)—for joy
- धर्मणा (*dharmaṇā*)—with the chariot that bears him
- आ यातु (*ā yātu*)—let him come to us

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The Indra who is swift-moving and who, making weak all the forces of the enemy by his great strength, surpasses them: that Indra, lord of wealth, let him come to us with his chariot for joy.

##### English translation
May Indra, the lord of wealth, come hither with his chariot for his exhilaration : he who is rapid, strong, who, overcoming all the forces (of the enemy) by his great insuperable might, (passes) through them.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सु॒ष्ठामा॒ रथः॑ सु॒यमा॒ हरी॑ ते मि॒म्यक्ष॒ वज्रो॑ नृपते॒  
> गभ॑स्तौ ।  
> शीभं॑ राजन्तु॒स्पथा या॑ह्व॒र्नाज् वर्धा॑म ते प्॒रपुषो॒ व्यष्ण्या॑नि ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सुस्थामा । रथः । सुयमा । हरी इति । ते । मिम्यक्ष । वज्रः । स्वपते । गभस्मा ।  
> शीभं । राजन् । सुपथा । अ । याहि । अर्वाज् । दर्धाम । ते । प्रपुषः । वृष्णानि ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे नृपते नृणां पालकेंद्र ते तव रथः सुष्मामा शोभनावस्थानो हरी
> अश्वावपि सुयमा सुयमौ । सुबद्धावित्यर्थः । किंच तद गथस्त् बाह वज्रो
> मिम्यक्ष । संहतो भवति । तथा सति हे राजन् सर्वस्येश्वरेंद्र शीभं शीघ्रं सुपथा
> शोभनेन मार्गेणार्वाणिस्मदभिमुख आ याहि । आगच्छ । अगत्य पपुषः सोमं
> पीतवशस्ते तव वृष्णानि बलानि वर्धाम । वयं वर्धयामः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, protector of men (नृपते (*nṛpate*)), your chariot has a good standing-place (सुष्ठामा (*suṣṭhāmā*), glossed शोभनावस्थानो (*śobhanāvasthāno*)), and your two horses are well controlled (सुयमा (*suyamā*) = *subaddhau*, well harnessed). Further, in your arm the thunderbolt is held fast (मिम्यक्ष (*mimyakṣa*) = it is made firm). **NOTE** That being so, O king, lord of all, Indra, come quickly (शीभं (*śībhaṃ*) = शीघ्रं (*śīghraṃ*)) by the good path (सुपथा (*supathā*)), facing us (अर्वाण् (*arvāṇ*)). Having come, we increase the powers (वृष्णानि (*vṛṣṇāni*) = बलानि (*balāni*)) of you who have drunk the Soma.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- नृपते (*nṛpate*)—O Indra, protector of men
- ते (*te*)—your
- रथः (*rathaḥ*)—chariot
- सुष्णामा (*suṣṇāmā*)—having an excellent dwelling-place
- हरी (*harī*)—your horses
- सुयमा (*suyamā*)—well trained
- गभस्तौ (*gabhastau*)—in your arm
- वज्रः (*vajraḥ*)—the thunderbolt
- मिम्मुक्षु (*mimmukṣu*)—is held
- राजन् (*rājan*)—O Indra, lord
- शीभं (*śībhaṃ*)—quickly
- सुपथा (*supathā*)—by the excellent path
- अर्वाण् (*arvāṇ*)—facing us
- आ याहि (*ā yāhi*)—deign to come
- पपुषः (*papuṣaḥ*)—of you who drink Soma
- ते (*te*)—your
- वृष्ट्यानि (*vṛṣṭyāni*)—powers
- वर्धाम (*vardhāma*)—we strengthen

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, protector of men, your chariot has an excellent dwelling-place; your horses are well harnessed; in your arm the thunderbolt is held. O Indra, lord, come quickly by the excellent path facing us. We strengthen your powers, you who drink Soma.

### Printed p. 43
King of men, your chariot is well stationed; your horses are well trained; the thunderbolt is grasped in your hand : come quickly towards us, lord of all, by a good path, we will augment your energies when you drink the Soma.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> एंद्र॒वाहो॑ नृ॒पतिं॒ वज्र॑बाहुमु॒ग्रमु॒ग्रास॑स्तवि॒षास॑ एनं ।  
> प्रत्व॑क्षसं वृष॒भं स॒त्यशु॑ष्म॒मेम॑स्म॒त्रा स॑ध॒मादो॑ वहंतु ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । इंद्रवाहः । नृपतिं । वज्रबाहुं । उग्रं । उग्रासः । तविषासः । एनं ।  
> प्रत्त्वक्षसं । वृषभं । सत्यशुष्मं । आ । ईं । अस्मत्रा । सध्वमादः । वहंतु ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> नृपतिं नरां नेतृणां पालकं वज्रबाहुं वज्रहस्तमुग्रमुद्दूर्णं प्रत्वक्षसं शत्रुबलानां प्रकर्षेण तनूकर्तारं वृषभं कामानां वर्षितारं सत्यशुष्कमविसंवादिबलमेनमिंद्रं । आ ईमिति पूर्णा । उग्रास उद्यूर्णास्तविषासः प्रवृद्धाः
> सधमादः सह माद्यंत इंद्रवाह इंद्रस्य वोढारोऽस्मत्रास्मास्वा वहंतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa glosses the verse. Indra is the protector of men (नृपतिं (*nṛpatiṃ*), of the leaders of men), the bearer of the thunderbolt in his hand (वज्रबाहुं (*vajrabāhuṃ*)), fierce (उग्रं (*ugraṃ*), "violent"), the one who greatly diminishes the strength of enemies (प्रत्वक्षसं (*pratvakṣasaṃ*)), the showerer of desires (वृषभं (*vṛṣabhaṃ*)), and one whose strength is unfailing (सत्यशुष्मं (*satyaśuṣmaṃ*)). *ā īm* is a filler (completing the sense). The horses are उग्रासः (*ugrāsaḥ*) (violent), तविषासः (*taviṣāsaḥ*) (grown strong), सधमादः (*sadhamādaḥ*) (rejoicing together) and इंद्रवाहः (*iṃdravāhaḥ*) (bearers of Indra); let them bring him to us (अस्मत्रा (*asmatrā*), among us).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- नृपतिं (*nṛpatiṃ*)—protector of men
- वज्रबाहुं (*vajrabāhuṃ*)—one who holds the thunderbolt in his hand
- उग्रं (*ugraṃ*)—one of terrible might
- प्रत्वक्षसं (*pratvakṣasaṃ*)—one who destroys enemies
- वृषभं (*vṛṣabhaṃ*)—showerer of desires
- सत्यशुष्मं (*satyaśuṣmaṃ*)—one whose strength is true
- एनं (*enaṃ*)—Indra
- उग्रासः (*ugrāsaḥ*)—those of abundant strength
- तविषासः (*taviṣāsaḥ*)—those who are strong
- सधमादः (*sadhamādaḥ*)—those who rejoice together
- इंद्रवाहः (*iṃdravāhaḥ*)—horses that carry Indra
- अस्मत्रा (*asmatrā*)—to us
- आ वहंतु (*ā vahaṃtu*)—let them bring

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May the horses, of abundant strength, sturdy, rejoicing together and carrying Indra, bring to us Indra, the protector of men, who holds the thunderbolt in his hand, of terrible might, destroyer of enemies, showerer of desires, whose strength is true.
(continued)

### Printed p. 44

##### English translation
May the fierce, vigorous, exulting horses of Indra bring him hither to us, the king of men, armed with the thunderbolt, fierce, the reducer of foes, the showerer (of benefits), whose strength is real.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ए॒वा पतिं॑ द्रोण॒साचं॒ सचे॑तसमू॒र्जः स्कं॒भं॒॒ आ वृ॑षायसे।  
> ओजः॑ कृष्ण॒ सं गृ॑भाय॒ त्वे अप्पसो॒ यथा॑ केनि॒पाना॑मि॒नो  ॒
> वृधे ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> एव । पतिं । द्रोणसाचं । सचेतसं । ऊर्जः । स्कंभं । धरुणे । आ । वृषयसे ।  
> ओजः । कृष्ण । सं । गृभाय । श्वे इति । अपि । असः । यथा । केऽविपासाः । इनः । वृधे ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र त्वमेवैवं पतिं पालकं द्रोणसाचं द्रोणकलशस्य सेवितारं
> सचेतसं सप्रज्ञमूर्जः स्कंभं बलस्य धारकं सोमं धरुणे धारके जतर आ वृषायसे । असिंचसे । धारयसीत्यर्थः । किंच । मह्यमोजो बलं कृष्ण । कुरुष्ट ।
> त्वे त्वय्यस्मानपि संगृभाय । सं गृहाण च । किंच । केनिपानां मेधाविनामस्माळं ।
> केनिप उशिज इति मेधाविनामसु पाशात् । वृधे वर्धनाय यथा यथापत्सर्वथेन
> स्वाम्यसः । भवसि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, you thus fill the protector (पतिं (*patiṃ*)), the one who resorts to the Droṇa vessel (द्रोणसाचं (*droṇasācaṃ*)), the wise one (सचेतसं (*sacetasaṃ*)), the upholder of strength (ऊर्जः स्कंभं (*ūrjaḥ skaṃbhaṃ*)), namely Soma, into your receptacle (धरुणे (*dharuṇe*)); आ वृषायसे (*ā vṛṣāyase*) means you pour in, you hold. Further, make strength in me; take us also into yourself. Further, you are the master, for the growth of us the wise (केनिपानां (*kenipānāṃ*), which Sāyaṇa glosses as 'wise', citing 'kenipa, uśija' among the names of the wise)."

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- एव (*eva*)—in this manner
- पतिं (*patiṃ*)—protector of the sacrifice
- द्रोणसाचं (*droṇasācaṃ*)—that which rests on the Droṇa vessel
- सचेतसं (*sacetasaṃ*)—filled with wisdom
- ऊर्जः (*ūrjaḥ*)—of strength
- स्कंभं (*skaṃbhaṃ*)—the upholder; Soma
- धरुणे (*dharuṇe*)—in your belly
- आ वृषायसे (*ā vṛṣāyase*)—you fill yourself
- ओजः (*ojaḥ*)—strength
- तृष्ण (*tṛṣṇa*)—fill into me
- केनिपानां (*kenipānāṃ*)—of us the wise
- वृधे (*vṛdhe*)—for growth
(continued)

### Printed p. 45
- यथा (*yathā*)—in what manner
- इनः (*inaḥ*)—as master
- आसः (*āsaḥ*)—as you are (therefore)
- त्ये (*tye*)—in you
- अपि सं गृभय (*api saṃ gṛbhaya*)—take us to yourself (make us yours)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, you fill into your belly the Soma, protector of the sacrifice, resting on the Droṇa vessel, filled with wisdom, upholder of strength. Fill vigour in me too. For the growth of us the wise, make us your own. You are our master; therefore make us grow.

##### English translation
Verily, Indra, you pour into your capacious (stomach) the protecting (Soma), serving the wooden trough, intelligent, holding strength : bestow (upon us) vigour : take us into yourself, for you are the lord of the exaltation of the wise.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> गम॑न्न॒स्मे वसू॒न्या हि शंसि॑षं स्वा॒तिषं॒ भर॒मा या॑हि सो॒मिनः॑ ।  
> त्वमित॑िषे॒ सास्मिन्ना स॑त्सि ब॒र्हिष्य॑नाधृ॒ष्ट्या तव॒ पात्रा॑णि॒ धर्म॑णा॥  
> ददनादि  
> गमन् । अस्मे इति । वसूनि । आ । संशिषं । सुआशिषं । भरं । आ । याहि । सोमिनः।  
> त्वं । ई॑शिषे॒ । सः । अस्मिन् । आ । स॑त्सि । ब॒र्हिषि । अ॑नाधृ॒ष्या । तव॒ । पात्रा॑णि॒ । धर्म॑णा॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र वसूनि त्वद्देयानि धनान्यस्मे मय्या गमन् । आगच्छंतु । हि
> यस्मात्वामहं शंसिषं स्तौमि । किंच सोमिनः सोमवशो मम स्वाशिषं शोभना
> आशिषो यस्मिंस्तं भरं यज्ञमा याहि । त्वमागच्छ । किंच त्वमीशिषे । धनस्येश्वरोऽसि । एवं विधः स त्वमस्मिन्नस्मदीये वेद्यास्तृते बर्हिष्या सत्सि । आसीद । किंच
> तव सोमपात्राणि धर्मणा राक्षसानां कर्मणानाधृष्ट्यानि धर्षयितुमशक्यानि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, let the riches that you give come to me, since I praise you. Come also to the sacrifice of me, the Soma-possessor, in which there are good blessings. You are the lord of wealth; being such, sit on this spread sacred grass of ours. Further, your Soma vessels are unassailable (*anādhṛṣṭyāni*), impossible to violate, through the action (धर्मणा (*dharmaṇā*)) of the Rākṣasas."
(continued)

### Printed p. 46

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वसूनि (*vasūni*)—the riches you provide
- अस्मे (*asme*)—to us
- आ गमन् (*ā gaman*)—let them come quickly and join
- हि (*hi*)—because
- शंसिषं (*śaṃsiṣaṃ*)—I praise you
- सोमिनः (*sominaḥ*)—of me, who have Soma
- स्वाशिषं (*svāśiṣaṃ*)—accompanied by good blessings
- भरं (*bharaṃ*)—to the sacrifice
- आ याहि (*ā yāhi*)—deign to come
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- ईशिषे (*īśiṣe*)—you are lord of wealth
- सः (*saḥ*)—you, the renowned
- अस्मिन् (*asmin*)—of this sacrifice
- बर्हिषि (*barhiṣi*)—on the seat of darbha grass
- आ सत्सि (*ā satsi*)—sit
- तव (*tava*)—your
- पात्राणि (*pātrāṇi*)—Soma vessels
- धर्मणा (*dharmaṇā*)—by the action of asuras and others
- अनाधृष्या (*anādhṛṣyā*)—are not defiled

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, may the riches you provide come quickly to us. I praise you. Deign to come to the sacrifice of me, who have Soma, accompanied by good blessings. You are lord of wealth. Renowned as you are, sit on the seat of darbha grass of this sacrifice. Your Soma vessels are not defiled by the action of asuras and others.

##### English translation
Let the wealth (to be given by you) come quickly to me, for verily I have glorified you : come to the blessed sacrifice of the offerer of the Soma ; for you are the lord : sit down upon this sacred grass : these your cups (of Soma) are not to be insulted by the act(of evil spirits)

> न ये शे॒कुर्य॒ज्ञियां॒ नाव॑मा॒रुह॑मी॒र्मैव ते न्य॑विशंत॒  ॑

> क्रीपयः ॥ ६ ॥

### Printed p. 47

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र तव प्रसादात्ते पृथक् प्रायन् देवलोकान्नृथग्गच्छंति ये
> प्रथमाः पूर्व मुख्या ना देवहूतयो देवानामाह्वातारः ॥ कर्तरि कृत् ॥ किंच
> दुष्टरान्यॆर्दुस्तराणि श्रवण्यानि श्रवणीयानि यशांस्यकृण्वत अकुर्वत । किंच ये
> तत्पसादरहिता जना यज्ञियां यज्ञसंबंधिनीं । यज्ञमयीमित्यर्थः । नावमारुहमारोढुं न शेकुः न शक्नु वंति ते केपयः कुत्सितपूयकर्माणः पापकर्माणो जना ईर्मैव ऋणेनैव स्यविशंत । नीचैरविशंत । अधोगतिं प्राप्ता
> इत्यर्थः । तथा च यास्कः । पृथक् प्रायन् पृथक् प्रथतेः प्रथमा देवहूतयो
> ये देवानाह्वयंताकुर्वत श्रवणीयानि यशांसि दुरनुकरणान्र्येऽशक्नु वन्यज्ञॆयां नावमारोळ्ळुमथ ये नाशक्कुवन्यज्जियां नावमारोळ्ळुमीर्मैव ते
> न्यविशंत ऋणेनैव ते स्यविशंतास्मिन्नेव लोक इति वा । नि. ५-२५ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, by your grace they go separately from the world of the gods: those who are first, foremost, callers of the gods (देवहूतयः (*devahūtayaḥ*); the affix is in the agent sense). Further, they have made difficult-to-attain (दुस्तरा (*dustarā*)) glories worthy of being heard (श्रवस्यानि (*śravasyāni*)). Further, those people without that grace who are unable to ascend the sacrificial boat, i.e. the boat consisting of sacrifice, those of evil, sinful action (केपयः (*kepayaḥ*)), sink down by debt alone (ईर्मा (*īrmā*) = ऋणेनैव (*ṛṇenaiva*)), attaining a downward course." Sāyaṇa also cites Yāska: पृथक् प्रायन्प्रथमा देवहूतयोऽकृण्वत श्रवस्कानि दुष्टरा। न ये शेकुर्यज्ञियां नावमारुहमीर्मैव ते न्यविशंत केपयः ॥ ६ ॥ पृथक् । प्र । आयन् प्रथमाः । देवहूतयः । अकृण्वत । श्रवस्यानि । दुस्तरा । न । ये । शीकुः । यज्ञियां । नावं । आरुहं । ईर्मा । एव । ते । नि । अविशंत । क्रीपयः ॥ ६ ॥ हे इंद्र तव प्रसादात्ते पृथक् प्रायन् देवलोकान्नृथग्गच्छंति ये प्रथमाः पूर्व मुख्या ना देवहूतयो देवानामाह्वातारः ॥ कर्तरि कृत् ॥ किंच दुष्टरान्यॆर्दुस्तराणि श्रवण्यानि श्रवणीयानि यशांस्यकृण्वत अकुर्वत । किंच ये तत्पसादरहिता जना यज्ञियां यज्ञसंबंधिनीं । यज्ञमयीमित्यर्थः । नावमारुहमारोढुं न शेकुः न शक्नु वंति ते केपयः कुत्सितपूयकर्माणः पापकर्माणो जना ईर्मैव ऋणेनैव स्यविशंत । नीचैरविशंत । अधोगतिं प्राप्ता इत्यर्थः । तथा च यास्कः । पृथक् प्रायन् पृथक् प्रथतेः प्रथमा देवहूतयो ये देवानाह्वयंताकुर्वत श्रवणीयानि यशांसि दुरनुकरणान्र्येऽशक्नु वन्यज्ञॆयां नावमारोळ्ळुमथ ये नाशक्कुवन्यज्जियां नावमारोळ्ळुमीर्मैव ते न्यविशंत ऋणेनैव ते स्यविशंतास्मिन्नेव लोक इति वा । नि. ५-२५ । (*pṛthak prāyanprathamā devahūtayo 'kṛṇvata śravaskāni duṣṭarā| na ye śekuryajñiyāṃ nāvamāruhamīrmaiva te nyaviśaṃta kepayaḥ ‖ 6 ‖ pṛthak | pra | āyan prathamāḥ | devahūtayaḥ | akṛṇvata | śravasyāni | dustarā | na | ye | śīkuḥ | yajñiyāṃ | nāvaṃ | āruhaṃ | īrmā | eva | te | ni | aviśaṃta | krīpayaḥ ‖ 6 ‖ he iṃdra tava prasādātte pṛthak prāyan devalokānnṛthaggacchaṃti ye prathamāḥ pūrva mukhyā nā devahūtayo devānāmāhvātāraḥ ‖ kartari kṛt ‖ kiṃca duṣṭarānyèrdustarāṇi śravaṇyāni śravaṇīyāni yaśāṃsyakṛṇvata akurvata | kiṃca ye tatpasādarahitā janā yajñiyāṃ yajñasaṃbaṃdhinīṃ | yajñamayīmityarthaḥ | nāvamāruhamāroḍhuṃ na śekuḥ na śaknu vaṃti te kepayaḥ kutsitapūyakarmāṇaḥ pāpakarmāṇo janā īrmaiva ṛṇenaiva syaviśaṃta | nīcairaviśaṃta | adhogatiṃ prāptā ityarthaḥ | tathā ca yāskaḥ | pṛthak prāyan pṛthak prathateḥ prathamā devahūtayo ye devānāhvayaṃtākurvata śravaṇīyāni yaśāṃsi duranukaraṇānrye 'śaknu vanyajñèyāṃ nāvamāroḷḷumatha ye nāśakkuvanyajjiyāṃ nāvamāroḷḷumīrmaiva te nyaviśaṃta ṛṇenaiva te syaviśaṃtāsminneva loka iti vā | ni. 5-25 |*) (Ni. 5-25), which expands the verse in the same sense, adding the alternative that they sank by debt in this very world.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पृथक् (*pṛthak*)—two kinds of separate courses
- प्रायन् (*prāyan*)—men attain
- प्रथमाः (*prathamāḥ*)—the foremost
- देवहूतयः (*devahūtayaḥ*)—sacrificers who call the gods to the sacrifice
- दुष्टरा (*duṣṭarā*)—not to be climbed by others
- श्रवस्यानि (*śravasyāni*)—a glorious divine abode
- अकुर्वत (*akurvata*)—they make for themselves (obtain)
- ये (*ye*)—those who
- यज्जियां (*yajjiyāṃ*)—consisting of sacrifice
- नावं (*nāvaṃ*)—boat
- आरुहं (*āruhaṃ*)—to ascend
- न शेकुः (*na śekuḥ*)—are not able
- ते (*te*)—they
- केपयः (*kepayaḥ*)—being of sinful action
- ईर्मैव (*īrmaiva*)—afflicted by debt alone
- नृविशंत (*nṛviśaṃta*)—attain a downward course

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, men have two different courses. The sacrificers, the foremost, who call the gods to the sacrifice, obtain a glorious divine abode not to be climbed by others. Those who cannot ascend the boat of sacrifice, being of sinful action, are afflicted by debt and attain a downward course.

##### English translation
They go separately (to the worlds of the gods), first those who offer oblations to the gods have attained reputation difficult to surpass : those who have not been able to ascend the ship of sacrifice, have gone down (in the world), wretches (plunged) in debt.

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> एवैनैवमेव दूढ्यो दुर्बुद्धयोऽपरेऽन्ये । अयज्ञान इत्यर्थः । अपागपाचीना अधोगंतारः संतु भवंतु येषां यज्ञमकुर्वतामश्वा दुर्युजो
> दुर्योजाः प्रबला आश्वा आयुयुज्रे रथेषु युज्यंते । ये सत्यपि सामर्थ्ये
> न यजंति ते नरकं गच्छंतीति भावः । किंचेत्थेत्थं ते स्वर्गगामिनो भवंति य
> उपरे यज्ञानः प्राग्मरणात्पूर्वमेव दावने देवानां हविर्दानाय संति भवंति ।
> यत्र येषु च यज्ञकारिसु वयुनानि प्रज्ञानानि कांतानि वा भोजना भोगानि
> धनानि पुरूणि बहूनि देवेभ्यो दानाय संति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"In just this way let the others, of evil mind, i.e. non-sacrificers, be downward-going (अपाक् (*apāk*)), those whose strong horses (दुर्युजः (*duryujaḥ*)) are yoked to chariots while they do not perform sacrifice. The sense is: those who do not sacrifice though able go to hell. Further, thus (इत्था (*itthā*)) those others, sacrificers, who before death (प्राग् (*prāg*)) are ready to give oblations to the gods, go to heaven; and among those sacrificers there are knowledge (वयुनानि (*vayunāni*), wisdom, or desirable things) and many enjoyable riches (*bhojanā purūṇi*) for giving to the gods."

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- येषां (*yeṣāṃ*)—of those fallen from ritual action
- दुर्युजः (*duryujaḥ*)—of powerful strength
- अश्वाः (*aśvāḥ*)—horses
- आयुयुचे (*āyuyuce*)—are yoked to the chariot (though they have such strength)
- अपरे (*apare*)—such others fallen from ritual action
- दूढ्यः (*dūḍhyaḥ*)—being of evil mind
- एवैव (*evaiva*)—just as described above
- अपाक् (*apāk*)—going downward
- संतु (*saṃtu*)—let them be
- ये उपरे (*ye upare*)—those sacrificers
- प्राक् (*prāk*)—before death
- दापने (*dāpane*)—to offer oblation
- संति (*saṃti*)—who are ready
- यत्र (*yatra*)—among which sacrificers
- वयुनानि (*vayunāni*)—excellent knowledge
- भोजना (*bhojanā*)—cattle as means of enjoyment
- पुरूणि (*purūṇi*)—are abundant, they
- इत्था (*itthā*)—as described above, obtain an excellent course

### Printed p. 49

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Even though they have the strength to yoke powerful horses to the chariot and win wealth, those who, being of evil mind, do not perform the rite go only downward. The sacrificers who are ready to offer the oblation before death, and in whom abundant excellent knowledge and wealth that serves enjoyment bear fruit, attain the excellent state described above.

##### English translation
Thus may those others the ignorant go downwards, whose restive horses are yoked ; otherwise (is it with those) who before (death) are near unto liberality, amongst whom precious gifts (to the gods) are numerous.

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> गि॒रीरज्रा॒नेज॑माना आधारय॒द्दौः क्रं॑ददं॒तरि॑क्षाणि कोपयत् ।  
> स॒मी॒ची॒ने धि॒षणे॒ वि ष्क॑भायति॒ वृष्णः॑ पी॒त्वा मद॑ उ॒क्धानि॑  
> शंसति ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> गिरीन् । अज्रान् । रेजमानान् । आधारयत् । द्यौः । क्रंदत् । अंतरिक्षाणि ।  
> कोपयत् ।  
> समीचीने इति संईचीने । धिषणे इति । वि । स्वभायति । वृष्णः । पीत्वा । मदे ।  
> उक्धानि । शंसति ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सायुक्तं
> गिरीश्वेघानज्रान्गमनशीलांज्रेजमानान्यंपमानानाधारयत् । इंद्रो धारितवान् । निश्चलान्कृतवानित्यर्थः । किंच द्यौर्द्युलोकस्तन्निवासी वा जनोऽसुरराक्षसेभ्यो भीतः सन् क्रंदत् । क्रंदति । इंद्रमाह्वयत्यात्मनः परित्राणार्थं । किंचें.
> द्रोऽंतरिक्षणि कोपयत् । कोपयति । संक्षोभयतीत्यर्थः । किंचेंद्रः समीचीने परस्परं संगते धिषणे द्यावापृथिव्यै वि ष्कभायति । अंतरिक्षेण स्तंभाति । स्तंभ-

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Indra held steady the moving, trembling गिरीन् (*girīn*), which Sāyaṇa glosses as clouds (the printed word is damaged) **NOTE**. That is, he made them motionless. Heaven, or the people dwelling in it, being afraid of the asuras and rākṣasas, cries out: it calls Indra for its own protection. Indra also shakes the atmospheric regions, that is, throws them into commotion. Further, Indra firmly fixes the two धिषणे (*dhiṣaṇe*), heaven and earth, which are joined together: he props them up by means of the atmosphere.

### Printed p. 50

> यतीत्यर्थः । किंचेंद्रो वृष्टः स्नोदरस्यासेक्तान्नोमान्पीत्वा मदे सत्क्यादि
> प्रतस्यानि वचनानि तंसति । प्रब्रवीति ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Having drunk the Soma that fills his belly, Indra, in his exhilaration, utters words of praise **NOTE**.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अज्रान् (*ajrān*)—the moving ones
- रेजमानान् (*rejamānān*)—the trembling
- गिरीस् (*girīs*)—the clouds
- आधारयत् (*ādhārayat*)—Indra made them motionless
- दौः (*dauḥ*)—the heaven-world
- क्रंदक् (*kraṃdak*)—cries out to Indra for protection
- अंतरिक्षाणि (*aṃtarikṣāṇi*)—the atmospheric regions
- कोपयत् (*kopayat*)—are thrown into commotion
- समीचीना (*samīcīnā*)—joined together
- धिषणे (*dhiṣaṇe*)—heaven and earth
- विष्कभायति (*viṣkabhāyati*)—firmly establishes
- वृष्णः (*vṛṣṇaḥ*)—the Soma juices that fill his belly
- पीश्वा (*pīśvā*)—having drunk
- मदे (*made*)—when exhilaration arises
- उक्कानि (*ukkāni*)—excellent words of praise
- शंसति (*śaṃsati*)—recites

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra made the moving, trembling clouds motionless. The heaven-world cries out to Indra for protection. The atmospheric regions are thrown into commotion. Indra firmly establishes heaven and earth, which are joined together. Having drunk the Soma juices that fill his belly, he recites excellent words of praise when exhilaration arises.

##### English translation
(Indra) has arrested the wandering tremulous clouds : the heaven cries out (in alarm) : he agitates the firmament ; he fixes the combined heaven and earth : having drunk of the effused Soma, he repeats praises in his exhilaration.

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इ॒मं बि॑भर्मि॒ सुकृ॑तं ते अंकु॒शं येवा॑रु॒जासि॑ मघवज्ञाफा॒रुजः॑ ।  
> अ॒स्मिन्नु ते॒ सव॑ने अस्तो॒क्यं॑ सु॒त इ॒ष्टौ म॑घवन्नो॒ध्याभ॑गः

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अनुं । विधर्मि । सुकृतं । के । अंकुशं । येन । आर्यनागि । पुत्रस्यदन् ।  
> तत्त्वज्ञः ॥

### Printed p. 51

> अस्मिन् । सु । ते । सवने । अस्तु । ओक्कं । सुते । इष्टौ । मघवन् । बोधि ।  
> आभगः ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मघदन्नवन्निंद्र ते तव सुकृतं संस्कृतमिममंकुशं बिभर्मि । धारयामि । अश्रांकुशशब्देन प्रेरकत्वगुणयोगात्सुतिरुच्यते । स्तुतिं धारयामीत्यर्थः येन स्तुतिलक्षणांकुशेन स्तुतः संस्कृं शफारुज अभिमुख्येन परबलानां
> हंत्यनैरावतादीन्गजानारुजासि अभिमुख्येन रुजसि । पीडयसीत्यर्थः । किंच ते
> तवास्मिन्नस्मदीये सवन ओक्कमोको निवासोऽस्तु । भवतु । किंच हे मघदन्नवन्निंद्र आभग अभजनीयस्यं सुते सुतसोम इष्टौ यज्ञॆ बोधि । अस्मदीयाः
> स्तुतीर्बुध्यस्व ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O wealthy Indra, I bear this well-prepared goad of yours, अंकुशं (*aṃkuśaṃ*). Here, because the goad has the quality of urging on, "goad" means praise: the meaning is "I hold praise". By this goad of praise, being praised, you press upon and torment, facing them, Airāvata and the other elephants that destroy the strength of the enemy **NOTE**. Further, let your dwelling ओक्कं (*okkaṃ*) be in this our Soma-pressing. And, O wealthy Indra, worthy of worship, be aware of our praises at the sacrifice in which Soma has been pressed.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मघवन् (*maghavan*)—O wealthy Indra
- येन (*yena*)—by which goad in the form of praise
- तफारुजः (*taphārujaḥ*)—your elephants such as Airāvata, that fall upon the enemies
- आरुजासि (*ārujāsi*)—you prod
- ते (*te*)—your
- सुकृतं (*sukṛtaṃ*)—well prepared
- इदुं अंकुशं (*iduṃ aṃkuśaṃ*)—this goad in the form of praise
- बिभर्मि (*bibharmi*)—I hold
- ते (*te*)—for you
- ओक्क्यं (*okkyaṃ*)—a dwelling-place
- अस्मिन् सदने (*asmin sadane*)—in this our sacrifice
- अस्तु (*astu*)—let it be
- मघवन् (*maghavan*)—O wealthy Indra
- अभगः (*abhagaḥ*)—you, the worshipful
- सुते (*sute*)—in which Soma has been pressed
- इष्टौ (*iṣṭau*)—in our sacrifice
- बोधि (*bodhi*)—know our words of praise

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O wealthy Indra, I hold this goad of yours, well prepared and in the form of praise, with which you prod your elephants such as Airāvata that fall upon the enemy armies. Let there be a dwelling for you in this our sacrifice. O wealthy Indra, worshipful one, know our words of praise in our sacrifice where Soma has been pressed.

##### English translation
I bear this your well-made goad, (excited) by which, Maghavat, you urge on the slayers of powerful foes. Let your abode be at this my sacrifice. Accept, Maghavat, who are to be adored, (my praise) at the sacrifice at which (the Soma) is effused.

### Printed p. 52

#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> गोभि॑ष्टरे॒माम॑तिं दु॒रेवां॒ यवे॑न॒ क्षुधं॑ पुरुहूत्॒त विश्वां॑ ।  
> व॒यं राज॑भिः प्रथ॒मा धना॑स्य॒स्माके॑न वृ॒जने॑वा जयेम ॥१०॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> गोभिः । शरेम । अमतिं । दुःखिवां । यवेन । क्षुधं । पुरुषूत । विश्वां ।  
> वयं । राजभिः । प्रथमाः । धनानि । आस्माकेन । वृजनेस । जयेम ॥ १० "

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे पुरुहूत बहुभिराहूतेंद्र तव प्रसादाद्दुरेवां दुष्टागदुसां
> दारिद्रादागताममतिं दुर्बुद्धिं वयं घौषेयाः सुहस्त्या गोभिः पशुभिस्तरेम ।
> निस्तरेम । किंच यवेन विश्वां व्याप्तां क्षुधं शरेम । किंच प्रथमा मुख्यापि
> धनानि राजभिर्धनानामीश्वरैः । लभेमहीति शेषः । यद्वा । प्रथमा वयमिति
> संबंधः । किंचास्माकेनास्मदीयेन वृजनेन बलेन जयेम । शत्रुविनिति शेषः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Puruhūta, Indra invoked by many, by your grace may we cross over अमतिं (*amatiṃ*), evil-mindedness, which has come from poverty (दुरेवां (*durevāṃ*) is glossed as "bad … coming from poverty") **NOTE**, by means of cattle: may we get past it. And by means of barley may we get past all-pervading hunger. Further, may we obtain the foremost riches through the kings, the lords of wealth (the verb "may we obtain" is supplied). Or else the construction is "we, the first". And by our own strength वृजनेन (*vṛjanena*) may we conquer (the enemy is supplied).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पुरुहूत (*puruhūta*)—O Indra, called upon by many
- दुरेणां (*dureṇāṃ*)—arisen from poverty
- अमतिं (*amatiṃ*)—evil-mindedness
- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we
- गोभिः (*gobhiḥ*)—with the help of our wealth of cattle
- तरेम (*tarema*)—may we cross over
- यवेन (*yavena*)—with the help of barley
- विश्वां (*viśvāṃ*)—that surrounds us
- क्षुधं (*kṣudhaṃ*)—hunger; may we cross over it
- प्रथमाः (*prathamāḥ*)—the most important
- धनानि (*dhanāni*)—riches
- राजभी (*rājabhī*)—from the lords of wealth (may we obtain them)
- अस्माकेन (*asmākena*)—our
- वृजनेन (*vṛjanena*)—by strength
- जयेम (*jayema*)—may we conquer the enemies

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Puruhūta Indra, may we cross over the evil-mindedness that has come from poverty by means of our wealth of cattle. May we destroy the hunger that surrounds us with the help of barley. May we obtain the foremost riches from the wealthy. May we conquer our enemies by our strength.

##### English translation
Indra, invoked of many, may we escape all ignorance caused by poverty by means of our cattle : (may we escape) all hunger by means of our barley : (may we acquire) the chief riches through the princes (of wealth) : may we conquer by our valour.

### Printed p. 53

#### Ṛk 11

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> बृह॒स्पति॑र्नः॒ परि॑ पातु प॒श्चादु॒तोत्त॑रस्मा॒दध॑रादघा॒योः ।  
> इंद्रः॑ पु॒रस्ता॑दु॒त म॑ध्य॒तो नः॒ सखा॒ सखि॑भ्यो॒ वरि॑वः कृणोतु

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> बृहस्पतिः । नः । परि । पातु । पश्चात् । उत । उत्तरस्मात् । आधरात् । अघयोः।  
> इंद्रः । पुरस्तात् । उत । मध्यतः । नः । सखा । सखिभ्यः । वरिवः । कृणोतु ॥ ११ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> पश्चात्पश्चिमतो योऽघायुरागच्छति तस्मादघायोः पापकाच्छत्रोर्नोS-
> Sस्मास्पृहस्पतिः परि पातु । रक्षतु । उतापि चोत्तरस्मादुत्तरतोऽधरादधस्ताच्च
> योऽघायुरागच्छति तस्मादघायोर्नोऽस्मान्परि पातु । उतापि चेंद्रः पुरस्तात्पूर्वतोमध्यतश्च योऽघायुरागच्छति तस्मादघायोर्नोऽस्मान्सरि पातु । किंच ।
> सखा सखीभूतेंद्रः सखिभ्यः प्रियेभ्योऽस्मभ्यं वरिवो धनं कृणोतु । करोतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

From the evil-minded foe who comes from behind, from the west, may Bṛhaspati protect us. And from the evil-minded one who comes from the north, उत्तरस्मात् (*uttarasmāt*), and from below, अधरात् (*adharāt*), may he protect us. Likewise may Indra protect us from the evil-minded one who comes from the front, from the east, and from the middle. Further, may Indra, the friend, make वरिवः (*varivaḥ*), wealth, for us, his dear friends. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- बृहस्पतिः (*bṛhaspatiḥ*)—Bṛhaspati
- पश्चात् (*paścāt*)—from behind us
- अघायोः (*aghāyoḥ*)—from the enemy who wishes us harm
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- परि पातु (*pari pātu*)—may he protect
- उत (*uta*)—and
- उत्तरस्मात् (*uttarasmāt*)—from the part above us
- अधरात् (*adharāt*)—may he protect us from the enemies who fall upon us from the part below
- उत (*uta*)—and
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- पुरस्तात् (*purastāt*)—from the eastern direction
- मध्यतः (*madhyataḥ*)—from the middle
- नः (*naḥ*)—may he protect us
- सखा (*sakhā*)—Indra, the friend
- सखिभ्यः (*sakhibhyaḥ*)—to us, his friends
- वरिवः (*varivaḥ*)—wealth
- कृणोतु (*kṛṇotu*)—may he provide

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May Bṛhaspati protect us from the enemy who comes from behind us wishing us harm. May he protect us also from the enemies who fall upon us from above and from below. May Indra protect us from the eastern direction and from the middle. May Indra, the friend, provide wealth to us, his friends.

### Printed p. 54

##### English translation
May Brihaspati defend us from a malignant (foe) coming from behind, from above, or from below; may Indra defend us from foes in front and in the middle; may he, our friend bestow wealth upon (us) his friends.

## Sūkta 45 (Maṇḍala 10)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> दिवस्परीति द्वादशर्चं तृतीयं सूक्तं भालंदनस्य वत्सप्रेरार्षं त्रैष्टुधमाग्नेयं । तथा चानुक्रांतं । दिवस्परि द्वादश वत्सप्रिराग्नेयं त्विति ॥ प्रातरनुपाकाश्चिनशस्त्रयोरिदमादिके द्वे सूक्ते अस्य सूक्तस्यांत्यां वर्जयित्वा । सूत्रितं चदिवस्परीति सूक्तयोः पूर्वस्कोत्तमामुद्धरेत् । आ-४-१३। इति ॥ क्षामवत्यामक्रंद
> दग्निरित्येषा याज्या । सूत्रितं च । अग्ने त्वमस्मद्युयोध्यमीवा अक्रंददग्निः
> स्तनयॆन्निवद्यौः । आ-३-१३ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta beginning दिवस्परि (*divaspari*) has twelve Ṛks and is the third sūkta. Its seer is Vatsapri, son of Bhalandana (the printed Sanskrit reads वत्सप्रेरार्षं (*vatsaprerārṣaṃ*)) **NOTE**; its deity is Agni; its metre is Triṣṭubh. The Anukramaṇī is quoted accordingly. In the Prātaranuvāka and the Āśvina-śastra, the two sūktas beginning with this one, the first and the following, are used, leaving out the last Ṛk of this sūkta. The sūtra is cited: दिवस्परीति सूक्तयोः पूर्वस्कोत्तमामुद्धरेत् (*divasparīti sūktayoḥ pūrvaskottamāmuddharet*) (Ā. 4-13). The fourth Ṛk, beginning अक्रंददग्निः (*akraṃdadagniḥ*), is the yājyā in the oblation to Agni Kṣāmavat. The sūtra is cited: अग्ने त्वमस्मद्युयोध्यमीवा (*agne tvamasmadyuyodhyamīvā*) and the rest (Ā. 3-13). **NOTE**

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta, beginning दिवस्परि (*divaspari*), is the third sūkta in the fourth anuvāka. It has twelve Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Vatsapri Bhālandana; the deity is Agni; the metre is Triṣṭubh. The Anukramaṇī says दिवस्परि द्वादश वत्सप्रिराग्नियं तु (*divaspari dvādaśa vatsaprirāgniyaṃ tu*). At the time of reciting the Prātaranuvāka and the Āśvinaśastra mantras, this sūkta, except its last Ṛk, and the following sūkta are used, as explained by the Āśvalāyana-Śrautasūtra sūtra दिवस्परीति सूक्तयोः पूर्वस्कोत्तमामुद्धरेत् (*divasparīti sūktayoḥ pūrvaskottamāmuddharet*) (Ā. 4-13). In the homa addressed to Agni Kṣāmavat, the fourth Ṛk of this sūkta, अक्रंददग्निः (*akraṃdadagniḥ*), is to be recited as the yājyā mantra, as explained by the Āśvalāyana-Śrautasūtra sūtra अग्ने त्वमस्मद्युयोध्यमीवा (*agne tvamasmadyuyodhyamīvā*) and the rest (Ā. 4-15).

Sūkta 45. Maṇḍala 10. Anuvāka 4. Sūkta 85. Aṣṭaka 7. Adhyāya 8. Vargas 24, 29. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 12. Seer: Vatsapri. Deity: Agni. Metre: Triṣṭubh.

### Printed p. 55
#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> दि॒वस्परि॑ प्रथ॒मं ज॑ज्ञे अ॒ग्निर॒स्मद्वि॒तीयं॒ परि॑ जा॒तवे॑दाः ।  
> तृ॒तीय॑म॒प्पु नृ॒मणा॒ अज॑स्र॒मिंधा॑न एनं जरते स्वा॒धीः ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> दिवः । परि । प्रथमं । जज्ञे । अग्निः । अस्मत् । द्वितीयं । परि । ज्ञातवेदाः ।  
> द्वितीयं । अप्सु । सृमनाः । आजस्रं । इंधानः । एनं । जरते । सुआधीः ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अग्निः प्रथमं पूर्वं दिवो द्युलोकस्य पर्युपर्यादित्यात्मना जज्ञे
> जातः । जातवेदा जातप्रज्ञो जातधनो वाग्निर्द्वितीयमस्मदस्माकं पर्युपरि
> पार्थिवात्मना जज्ञे । नृमणा नृषु मनुष्येष्टनुग्राहकतयासक्तचित्तोऽग्निस्तृतीयमप्संतरिक्षे वैद्युतात्मना जज्ञे । एवंविधमेनमग्निं स्वाधीः सुप्रज्ञा ताजस्रमनवरतमिंधानो दीपयञ्चरते । स्तौति । यद्दा । वाजसनेयकोक्तप्रकारेणास्म
> मंत्रस्यार्थो द्रष्टव्यः । तद्यथा । दिवस्परि प्रथमं जज्ञे अग्निरिति प्राणो वै द्यौः
> प्राणाद्वा एष प्रथममजायतास्मद्वितीयं परि जातवेदा इति यदेनमदो
> द्वितीयं पुरुषविधोऽजनयत्कृतीयमप्सिति यदेनमदस्तृतीयमद्भोऽजनयन्मणा अजस्रमिति प्रजापतिर्वै नृमणा अग्निरजस्रमिंधान एनं जरते स्वाधीरिति
> यो वा एनमिंधे स एनं जनयते स्वाधीः । शत ६-७-४.३ । इति ॥

**NOTE**

> विद्युदादि रूपगळल्लि अंतरिक्षदल्लि जनिसिदनु. उत्तमवाद प्रज्ञॆयुळ्ळ ऋषियु यावागलू अग्नि
> यन्नु प्रज्वलिसुवंतॆ माडुत्त स्तुतिसुत्तानॆ.

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Agni was born first, above (परि (*pari*)) the heavenly world, in the form of the Sun. Jātavedas, meaning either "one whose wisdom is born" or "one whose wealth is born", was born second, above us, in the form of the earthly fire. Nṛmaṇāḥ, "one whose mind is attached to men by way of favour towards them", was born third in the waters, that is, in the atmosphere, in the form of lightning. The Svādhīḥ, the one of good wisdom, praises this Agni, kindling him without cease.
Or else the meaning of this mantra should be seen according to the manner stated by the Vājasaneyins. Sāyaṇa quotes the Brāhmaṇa (Śata. 6-7-4.3): breath is heaven, and from breath he was first born; "second" means that when the man-shaped one begot him, and "third in the waters" means that when the waters begot him the third time; Prajāpati is Nṛmaṇāḥ; "whoever kindles him begets him"; and so on to Svādhīḥ. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अग्निः (*agniḥ*)—Agni
- प्रथमं (*prathamaṃ*)—first
- दिवः परि (*divaḥ pari*)—in the upper part of the heavenly world
- जक्ष्मी (*jakṣmī*)—was born
- जातवेदाः (*jātavedāḥ*)—in the form of Jātavedas
- द्वितीयं (*dvitīyaṃ*)—the second time
- अस्मत् परि (*asmat pari*)—was born upon our world, the earth
- तृतीयं (*tṛtīyaṃ*)—the third time
- नृमनाः (*nṛmanāḥ*)—Agni, who is beneficial to men
- अप्पु (*appu*)—was born in the atmosphere
- एनं (*enaṃ*)—this Agni, who has such greatness
- स्वाधीः (*svādhīḥ*)—the seer of excellent wisdom
- अजस्रं (*ajasraṃ*)—always
- इंधानः (*iṃdhānaḥ*)—causing him to blaze
- जरते (*jarate*)—praises

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Agni was born the first time above the heavenly world; the second time he was born on the earth in the form of Jātavedas. That same Agni, beneficial to men, was born the third time in the atmosphere

### Printed p. 56
in forms such as lightning. The seer of excellent wisdom praises Agni, always making him blaze.

##### English translation
Agni was first born above the sky; as Jatavedas he was born the second time amongst us: the friend of man, he was born the third time in the waters; the sage kindling him eternally praises him.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The description of the greatness of Agni, who pervades the universe and sustains it, is in Sūkta 10-45 extremely poetic. The manifestations of Agni, who is without birth and immortal, in his different forms are here denoted as the varieties of his birth. The Saṃhitā and other texts declare that all the other deities are only varieties of this Agni, who is without beginning, without a second and all-pervading. Agni, the sovereign of the whole universe, shines in the three worlds in three kinds of forms and is famed by three names. Āditya in the heavenly world and Indra of the middle world are only other forms of this Agni and nothing different. There are also many subordinate varieties relating to terrestrial Agni. Thus the aim of the present sūkta is to praise the greatness of Agni, who displays his own nature in many forms and sustains and nourishes the universe.
1) The manifestation of Agni in three forms in the three worlds.
2) The three forms visible to the perception of the world, and the divine nature that lies hidden within.
3) Agni's leadership of the sacrifice and, through it, his sustaining of the universe, and similar matters, are described in the present sūkta. The first Ṛk states the threefold order of Agni's birth. Although this matter was touched upon briefly earlier, when the purport of the Ṛk beginning श्रीणि जाना (*śrīṇi jānā*) was explained, here its detail is set out at length; many passages that bear on the birth of Agni are cited and the special points of the several contexts are given. **NOTE**
The purport of the first Ṛk is: "Agni was first born in the heavenly world. Afterwards, in the form of Jātavedas, he was born on the earth. That same Agni, a friend to men, was born the third time in the atmosphere."
द्युलोकस्य उपरि आदित्यात्मना जातः द्वितीयं पार्थिवात्मना जच्छे तृतीयं अंतरिक्षे वैद्युतात्मना जच्छे (*dyulokasya upari ādityātmanā jātaḥ dvitīyaṃ pārthivātmanā jacche tṛtīyaṃ aṃtarikṣe vaidyutātmanā jacche*) — Sāyaṇa's explanation is that the Agni who is of the nature of Āditya in the heavenly world and of the nature of lightning in the atmosphere was born also on the earth, in the form of terrestrial Agni. Such transformations of Agni will be set out in the special topics of the next Ṛk. What are the kinds of his birth? What are the sources of the birth? What are the forms of the birth? How these points are described is examined here.

### Printed p. 57
The passages that explain the birth of Agni are many, and to one who looks at them superficially they seem to contradict one another. But on close examination no contradiction remains. It is said that the first birth of Agni is in the heavenly world. How is that? The order of the birth is explained at length.
**The birth of Agni in the heavenly world**
स जायमानः परमे व्योमन्याविरग्नि रभवन्मातरिश्वने । अस्य क्रत्वा समिधानस्य मज्मना प्र द्यावा शोचिः पृथिवी आरोचयत् ॥ (*sa jāyamānaḥ parame vyomanyāviragni rabhavanmātariśvane | asya kratvā samidhānasya majmanā pra dyāvā śociḥ pṛthivī ārocayat ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-143-2)
When the purport of this mantra was explained earlier, the account of how Agni became manifest before Mātariśvan was given. In this mantra the manifestation of Agni in the three worlds, in three different ways, is also indicated.
First: मातरि अंतरिक्षे श्वसिति प्राणिति सूर्यरूपेण चेष्टत इति सूर (*mātari aṃtarikṣe śvasiti prāṇiti sūryarūpeṇa ceṣṭata iti sūra*) — the one who moves in the atmosphere in the form of the Sun (Sūra).
Second: the Agni who is of the nature of wind.
Third: the sacrificer on earth who carries out the sacrifice. This is the purport given.
Since it is said that the Agni who is visible in these three forms made all three worlds shine, the identity of Agni and Mātariśvan is shown.
उदु ष्टुतः समिधा यहो अद्यौद्वर्मन्बिवो अधि नाभा पृथिव्याः । मित्रो आग्निरीड्यो मातरिश्वा दूतो वक्षद्यजथाय देवान् ॥ (*udu ṣṭutaḥ samidhā yaho adyaudvarmanbivo adhi nābhā pṛthivyāḥ | mitro āgnirīḍyo mātariśvā dūto vakṣadyajathāya devān ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-5-9)
In passages such as this the word Mātariśvan is explained as a word that tells a special quality of Agni. If it is asked what the original form of Agni was, before he took manifestation in these forms, the answer is as follows.
असच्चॆ सच्च परमे व्योमन्नहस्य जन्मन्नदिशेरुपस्थे । अग्निर्ह नः प्रथमजा यतस्य पूर्व आयुनि वृशभश्च धेनुः ॥ (*asaccè sacca parame vyomannahasya janmannadiśerupasthe | agnirha naḥ prathamajā yatasya pūrva āyuni vṛśabhaśca dhenuḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-5-7)

### Printed p. 58
In the supreme divine world, the seat of Dakṣa, Agni was first, in an unmanifest form and in a manifest form, the overseer of all the affairs of the world. Afterwards he was born before all the creatures of the world; thus both the supramundane and the mundane forms of Agni are described. His first birth in the heavenly world is in the form of Āditya, and his eternal radiance is there itself.
स जायमानः परमे व्योमनि व्रतान्यग्निर्वतपा अरक्षत । व्यंतरिक्षममिमीत सुक्रतुर्वैश्वानरो महिना नाकमस्पृशत् ॥ (*sa jāyamānaḥ parame vyomani vratānyagnirvatapā arakṣata | vyaṃtarikṣamamimīta sukraturvaiśvānaro mahinā nākamaspṛśat ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-8-2)
Agni, called Vaiśvānara, born in the highest sky in the form of the Sun, protects the sacred rites. This same Agni has built the three worlds. Established in the heavenly world by his greatness, he shines. Such descriptions are praises of Agni in the form of Āditya.
शुचिं न यामन्निषिरं स्वर्द्भशं केतुं दिवो रोचनस्थामुषर्बुधं । अग्निं मूर्धानं दिवो अप्रतिष्टुतं तमीमहे नमसा वाजिनं बृहत् ॥ (*śuciṃ na yāmanniṣiraṃ svardbhaśaṃ ketuṃ divo rocanasthāmuṣarbudhaṃ | agniṃ mūrdhānaṃ divo apratiṣṭutaṃ tamīmahe namasā vājinaṃ bṛhat ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-2-14)
This Agni is the chief of the heavenly world. He is one who shines in the heavenly world, a great one, the one who makes the heavenly world known. We worship this Agni, who is worshipful in the sacrifice. Such praises denote Agni in the form of Āditya. The greatness of this Agni in the form of Āditya is as follows:
यो विश्वाभि विपश्यति भुवना सं च पश्यति । स नः वर्षदति द्विषः ॥ (*yo viśvābhi vipaśyati bhuvanā saṃ ca paśyati | sa naḥ varṣadati dviṣaḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-187-4)
यो अस्य पारे रजसः शुक्रो अग्निरजायत । स नः वर्षदति द्विषः ॥ (*yo asya pāre rajasaḥ śukro agnirajāyata | sa naḥ varṣadati dviṣaḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-187.5)
The Agni in the form of the Sun, who by his radiance makes all the worlds shine, who can see all the worlds, who shines with his light in a high region beyond the region of the atmosphere, that same Agni can protect us all. This Agni in the form of Āditya is born in the heavenly world.
असौ ना आदित्य एष रुक्मस्त्वितीव वा असावादित्योऽथो, . . . दुर्मरं वा एतस्यायुः श्रियो एष रोचतेग्निरम्यतोऽभवद्वयोभिरिति सर्वैदा एष वयोभिरमृतोऽभवद्यदेनं द्यौरजनयदिति द्यौर्वा एतमजनयत्सुरेशा इति सुरेता एषा यस्या एषरेतः ॥ (*asau nā āditya eṣa rukmastvitīva vā asāvādityo 'tho, . . . durmaraṃ vā etasyāyuḥ śriyo eṣa rocategniramyato 'bhavadvayobhiriti sarvaidā eṣa vayobhiramṛto 'bhavadyadenaṃ dyaurajanayaditi dyaurvā etamajanayatsureśā iti suretā eṣā yasyā eṣaretaḥ ‖*) (Ka. Brā. 6-7.22) **NOTE**

### Printed p. 59
The golden disc of Āditya shines in the heavenly world with its immortal radiance. This is the power of Agni, who pervades all the worlds. The heavenly world bears this Agni in the form of Āditya. Because the heavenly world bore this Agni in its womb, it was as though it bore an excellent child. In this way and the like, the praise of the Agni born in the heavenly world occurs.
**Praise of the lightning Agni born in the atmosphere**
Agni who dwells in the atmosphere, shining in the form of lightning, is described as अपां नपात् (*apāṃ napāt*), अपां गर्बः (*apāṃ garbaḥ*) and so on.
प्र केतुना बृहता यात्यग्निरा रोदसी वृषभो रोरवीति । दिवश्चिदंता उपमा उदानळपामुपस्थे महिषो ववर्ध ॥ (*pra ketunā bṛhatā yātyagnirā rodasī vṛṣabho roravīti | divaścidaṃtā upamā udānaḷapāmupasthe mahiṣo vavardha ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-8-1)
The lightning Agni, born of the water that dwells in the atmosphere, who lies and plays on the lap of the waters, pervades heaven and earth entire. He bellows like a bull. In this way the praise of the Agni born in the atmosphere occurs. This same Agni is also designated Āpta. Although Agni is born also of the water that is on earth, the word Āpta is, in general, a qualifier only of the lightning Agni born of the water dwelling in the atmosphere. In the contexts where there is a description such as धनोरधि प्रवतः (*dhanoradhi pravataḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-144-5), this point is clear.
स श्शितानस्तन्यतू रोचनस्था अजरेभिर्नानदद्भर्यविष्ठः । यः पादकः पुरुतमः पुरूणि पृथून्यग्निरनुयाति भर्वन् ॥ (*sa śśitānastanyatū rocanasthā ajarebhirnānadadbharyaviṣṭhaḥ | yaḥ pādakaḥ purutamaḥ purūṇi pṛthūnyagniranuyāti bharvan ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6.6-2)
In passages such as this the description of the lightning Agni is clear. This Agni in the form of lightning shines exceedingly. Shining in the atmosphere he produces a great sound. He spreads and pervades with rays that are undiminishing and sound-making. Thus he is praised. The description that Agni is born of the water of the atmosphere is very frequent in the Ṛksaṃhitā.
वनेषु व्यंन्तरिक्षं तशान वाजमर्वत्सु पय उस्रियासु । हृत्तु क्रतुं वरुणो अप्पंग्निं दिवि सूरमदधात्सोमदौ ॥ (*vaneṣu vyaṃntarikṣaṃ taśāna vājamarvatsu paya usriyāsu | hṛttu kratuṃ varuṇo appaṃgniṃ divi sūramadadhātsomadau ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-85-2)
Where it is said that the god Varuṇa placed Agni in the water just as he placed Āditya and the others in the heavenly world and the rest, the purport is that the word अप् (*ap*) means the water of the atmosphere, and that the Agni placed in it is the lightning Agni. The statement "तृतीयमप्पु (*tṛtīyamappu*)" in the present Ṛk is likewise a praise of this lightning Agni.

### Printed p. 60
**The glory of terrestrial Agni**
**The many kinds of birth of Agni on the earth**
This matter has been touched upon briefly in some places earlier; yet, since it is the main subject here, only some points not stated before are indicated. Agni, produced for the sacrifice by the act of churning in the aranis (fire-sticks), and so on: thus the praise of terrestrial Agni occurs. In many contexts this description is in excellent poetic form. For example:
उत स्म यं शिशुं यथा नवं जनिष्टारणे । धर्तारं मानुषीणां विशामग्निं स्वध्वरं ॥ (*uta sma yaṃ śiśuṃ yathā navaṃ janiṣṭāraṇe | dhartāraṃ mānuṣīṇāṃ viśāmagniṃ svadhvaraṃ ‖*) (Ka. Ka. Ka. Ka.) **NOTE**
The aranis have produced Agni, the sustainer of all men and the one with an excellent sacrifice, as one gives birth to a new child; thus the relation of mother and son between the aranis and Agni is shown. **NOTE**
Just as the water that dwells in the atmosphere is the source of the birth of Agni, so too the water of the earth is a source of his birth. The plants and the trees bear him like a womb.
अपां गर्भं दर्शतमोषधीनां वना जजान सुभगा विरूपं । देवासश्चिन्मनसा सं हि जग्मुः पनिष्कं जातं तवसं दुवस्मन् ॥ (*apāṃ garbhaṃ darśatamoṣadhīnāṃ vanā jajāna subhagā virūpaṃ | devāsaścinmanasā saṃ hi jagmuḥ paniṣkaṃ jātaṃ tavasaṃ duvasman ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-1-15)
The attractive aranis produced Agni, who is the womb-child of the waters and the plants. The gods too approached this worshipful Agni and praised him. In this way the order of the birth of the sacrificial Agni is told. Since this point has been explained in many contexts up to here, it need not be set out here.
Besides the threefold birth described thus in the three worlds, many other kinds of birth are also told. For example:
एता उ त्याः प्रत्यदृश्रन्नुरस्ताज्योतिर्यच्छंतीरुषसो विभातीः । अजेजननद्स्सर्यं यज्ञमग्निमपाचीनं तमो अगाढभुत् ॥ (*etā u tyāḥ pratyadṛśrannurastājyotiryacchaṃtīruṣaso vibhātīḥ | ajejananadssaryaṃ yajñamagnimapācīnaṃ tamo agāḍhabhut ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7.28.1)
The shining dawns have spread their radiance. The same dawns have produced the sun and the sacrificial Agni. This Agni is the son of the three worlds. How is that?
यं त्वा द्यावापृथिवी यं त्वापश्वस्टा यं त्वा सुजनिमा जहानः । पंथामनु प्रविद्वान्तित्रयाणं द्युमदग्ने समिधानो वि भाहि ॥ (*yaṃ tvā dyāvāpṛthivī yaṃ tvāpaśvasṭā yaṃ tvā sujanimā jahānaḥ | paṃthāmanu pravidvāntitrayāṇaṃ dyumadagne samidhāno vi bhāhi ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-26)

### Printed p. 61

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

O Agni, you whom heaven and earth begot, you whom the waters of the atmosphere begot, you whom the god Tvaṣṭṛ begot: having such a divine birth, and knowing the path of the fathers, you shine forth exceedingly.

"The plants bore this very sacrificial Agni in their womb. The waters too begot this same Agni. The trees also bore him in their womb. The plants and the rest beget Agni, who is of the nature of an embryo."

"The gods begot Agni, who is like the head of the heavenly world, who moves on the earth, who is the universal sovereign, who is the navel of the sacrifices and the guide of the sacrificers. In the same way the priests too begot him." (6. 7. 1-2) Though he is praised in this manner and in other ways, he is also described as begotten by whomever begotten; and he is praised as the father-like protector of all, gods, humans and the rest.

Ya. Saṃ. 1-69-1 **NOTE**

Describing this manifold origin of Agni, the present Ṛk praises him in the words दिवस्परि प्रथमं जज्ञे (*divaspari prathamaṃ jajñe*) and so on. Explaining the purport of this very mantra, the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa says as follows:

दिवस्परि प्रथमं जज्ञे अग्निरिति । प्राणो वै द्यौः प्राणाद्वा एष प्रथममजायतास्मद्वितीयं परि जातवेदा यदेनमदो द्वितीयं पुरुषविधोऽजनयत्तृतीयमप्सिति यदेनमदस्तृतीयमद्भोऽजनयन्नृमणा अजस्रमिति प्रजापतिर्वै नृमणा अग्निरजस्रमिंधान एनं जरते स्वाधीरिति यो वा एनमिंधे स एनं जनयते स्वाधीः ॥ (*divaspari prathamaṃ jajñe agniriti | prāṇo vai dyauḥ prāṇādvā eṣa prathamamajāyatāsmadvitīyaṃ pari jātavedā yadenamado dvitīyaṃ puruṣavidho 'janayattṛtīyamapsiti yadenamadastṛtīyamadbho 'janayannṛmaṇā ajasramiti prajāpatirvai nṛmaṇā agnirajasramiṃdhāna enaṃ jarate svādhīriti yo vā enamiṃdhe sa enaṃ janayate svādhīḥ ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 6-7-405) **NOTE**

Agni was first born in the heavenly world. Breath (prāṇa) is what is called the heavenly world; that is, Agni's first manifestation is from breath.
