These three ṛks cited above from the Ṛksaṃhitā describe the extraordinary qualities of Agni in one and the same manner. They are composed in the Triṣṭubh metre as praise of Agni. To the extent that they praise Agni, to that extent they have Agni's own nature. Because they are composed in the Triṣṭubh metre of eleven syllables, they have the nature of Indra. Agni's nature also has a twofold existence, as Indra and Agni. To the extent of Agni's greatness, to that extent he manifests worthiness of worship. In Indra and Agni all the gods are united. As great as Agni's greatness is, so great is Agni's worthiness of worship.
यद्वेव विष्णु क्रमवात्सप्रे भवतः । विष्णु क्रमैर्वै प्रजापतिरिमं लोकमसृजत वात्सप्रेणाग्निं विष्णु क्रमैर्वै प्रजापतिरंतरिक्षमसृजत वात्सप्रेण वायुं विष्णु क्रमैर्वै प्रजापतिर्दिवमसृजत वात्सप्रेणादित्यं विष्णु क्रमैर्वै प्रजापतिर्दिशोऽसृजत वात्सप्रेण चंद्रमसं विष्णु क्रमैर्वै प्रजापतिर्भूतमसृजत वात्सप्रेण भविष्यद्विष्णु क्रमैर्वै प्रजापतिर्वित्तमसृजत वात्सप्रेणाशां विष्णु क्रमैर्वै प्रजापतिरहरसृजत वात्सप्रेण रात्रिं विष्णु क्रमैर्वै प्रजापतिः पूर्वपक्षानसृजत वात्सप्रेणापरपक्षान्विष्णु क्रमैर्वै प्रजापतिरर्धमासानसृजत वात्सप्रेण मासान् विष्णु क्रमैर्वै प्रजापतिर्युक्तूगसृजत वात्सप्रेण संवत्सरं तद्यद्विष्णु क्रमवात्सप्रे भवत एतदेव तेन सर्वग्ं सृजते ॥ (*yadveva viṣṇu kramavātsapre bhavataḥ | viṣṇu kramairvai prajāpatirimaṃ lokamasṛjata vātsapreṇāgniṃ viṣṇu kramairvai prajāpatiraṃtarikṣamasṛjata vātsapreṇa vāyuṃ viṣṇu kramairvai prajāpatirdivamasṛjata vātsapreṇādityaṃ viṣṇu kramairvai prajāpatirdiśo 'sṛjata vātsapreṇa caṃdramasaṃ viṣṇu kramairvai prajāpatirbhūtamasṛjata vātsapreṇa bhaviṣyadviṣṇu kramairvai prajāpatirvittamasṛjata vātsapreṇāśāṃ viṣṇu kramairvai prajāpatiraharasṛjata vātsapreṇa rātriṃ viṣṇu kramairvai prajāpatiḥ pūrvapakṣānasṛjata vātsapreṇāparapakṣānviṣṇu kramairvai prajāpatirardhamāsānasṛjata vātsapreṇa māsān viṣṇu kramairvai prajāpatiryuktūgasṛjata vātsapreṇa saṃvatsaraṃ tadyadviṣṇu kramavātsapre bhavata etadeva tena sarvagṃ sṛjate ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 6-7-4-7)
In this context the question naturally arises: what is the need for the Kramas (Viṣṇu's striding) and the Vātsapra rite to be performed together? The need for the two to be performed together rests on their mutual relation. Whatever supporting things Prajāpati created by the Viṣṇu-stride, with the Vātsapra he created along with them the principles that pervade those supports and complete and nourish them. Thus:

Prajāpati created the earth, and with it, as its complement, Agni.
The same: the atmosphere, and the wind.
The same: the heavenly world, and the sun.
The same: the quarters, and the moon.
The same: things of the past, and things of the future.
The same: wealth, and desire.
The same: the day, and the night.

### Printed p. 96
The same: the first fortnights, and the latter fortnights.
The same: the half-months, and the months.
The same: the seasons, and the years.

Therefore, since these two rites are mutually related, there is a rule that both are to be performed together. By their association the work of creation also continues in unbroken sequence.
यद्वेव विष्णु क्रमवात्सप्रे भवतः । विष्णुक्रमैन्रै प्रजापतिः स्वर्गं लोकमभिप्रायात्स्य एतदवसानमपश्यद्वात्सप्रं तेनावास्यदप्रदाहाय युद्धि युक्तं न विमुच्यते प्र तद्दह्यते यथैवैतद्यजमानो विष्णुक्रमैन्रेव स्वर्गं लोकमभिप्रयाति वात्सप्रेणावस्यति ॥ (*yadveva viṣṇu kramavātsapre bhavataḥ | viṣṇukramainrai prajāpatiḥ svargaṃ lokamabhiprāyātsya etadavasānamapaśyadvātsapraṃ tenāvāsyadapradāhāya yuddhi yuktaṃ na vimucyate pra taddahyate yathaivaitadyajamāno viṣṇukramainreva svargaṃ lokamabhiprayāti vātsapreṇāvasyati ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 6-7-4-8)
If the question is asked again, what is the need for the association of the Viṣṇu-strides and the Vātsapra, the answer tells that need. By the Viṣṇu-stride Prajāpati reached heaven. There, seeing the Vātsapra, which releases from bonds, he released the part that had been bound. If an animal, or any other living being, that has been bound is not released, its burning or its being cut off will certainly happen. Prajāpati prevented this. In the same way the sacrificer too goes to heaven with the help of the Viṣṇu-stride, and by the help of the Vātsapra attains release from bondage.
स वै विष्णुक्रमान् क्रान्ता । अथ तदानीमेव वात्सप्रेणोपतिष्ठते यथा प्रयायाथ तदानीमेव विमुंचेत्तादृक्तद्देवानां वै विधामनु मनुष्यास्तस्मादु हेदमुत मानुषो ग्रामः प्रयायाथ तदानीमेवावस्यति । (*sa vai viṣṇukramān krāntā | atha tadānīmeva vātsapreṇopatiṣṭhate yathā prayāyātha tadānīmeva vimuṃcettādṛktaddevānāṃ vai vidhāmanu manuṣyāstasmādu hedamuta mānuṣo grāmaḥ prayāyātha tadānīmevāvasyati |*) (Śa. Brā. 6-7-4-9)
After following and crossing the Viṣṇu-strides, the sacrificer, standing near Agni, worships Agni with the Vātsapra rite. As one who stops at one place at the end of a journey and removes his fatigue, so the sacrificer too attains rest in this setting. Human beings in general follow the path of the gods.
तद्वा अहोरात्रे एव विष्णुक्रमा भवंति । अहोरात्रे वात्सप्रमहोरात्रे एव तद्यात्यहोरात्रे क्षेम्यो भवति तस्मादु हेदमुत मानुषो ग्रामोऽहोरात्रे यात्वाहोरात्रे क्षेम्यो भवति । (*tadvā ahorātre eva viṣṇukramā bhavaṃti | ahorātre vātsapramahorātre eva tadyātyahorātre kṣemyo bhavati tasmādu hedamuta mānuṣo grāmo 'horātre yātvāhorātre kṣemyo bhavati |*) (Śa. Brā. 6-7-4-10)

### Printed p. 97
The Viṣṇukramas are themselves day-and-night forms, and the Vatsapra too has the form of day and night. The Viṣṇukrama is a matter of travelling, whereas the Vatsapra gives rest. Therefore the sacrificer who has followed the Viṣṇukrama travels during one day-and-night and rests during another day-and-night. Even in worldly affairs, if people travel for a whole day (night included), that body of people rests on another day (night included).

स वा अर्धमेव संवत्सरस्य विष्णुक्रमान् क्रमते । अर्धं वात्सप्रेणोपतिष्ठते मध्यॆ ह संवत्सरस्य स्वर्गो लोकः स यत्कनीयोऽर्धात्ममेत न हैतग्ं स्वर्गं लोकमभिप्राप्नुयादथ यद्भूयोऽर्धात्पराज हैतग्ं स्वर्गं लोकमति प्रणश्येदथ यदर्थं क्रमतेऽर्धमुपतिष्ठते तत्संप्रति स्वर्गं लोकमाप्ता विमुंचते ॥ (*sa vā ardhameva saṃvatsarasya viṣṇukramān kramate | ardhaṃ vātsapreṇopatiṣṭhate madhyè ha saṃvatsarasya svargo lokaḥ sa yatkanīyo 'rdhātmameta na haitagṃ svargaṃ lokamabhiprāpnuyādatha yadbhūyo 'rdhātparāja haitagṃ svargaṃ lokamati praṇaśyedatha yadarthaṃ kramate 'rdhamupatiṣṭhate tatsaṃprati svargaṃ lokamāptā vimuṃcate ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 6-7-4-11 **NOTE**) **NOTE**

Following the Viṣṇukrama he travels only for half a year, and in the remaining half year he worships Agni with the Vatsapra rite. The heavenly world lies in the middle of the year. Therefore, if he travels for less than half a year he does not reach heaven at all. If he travels for more than half a year, heaven is passed over and slips from his hand. Therefore, if he travels for just half a year and worships Agni with the Vatsapra rite for the other half year, he reaches heaven as well and obtains release from bondage.

ताभ्यां वै विपर्यासमेति । यथा महांतमध्वानं विमोकग्ं समश्नुनीत तादृक्तात्स वै पुरस्ताज्योपरिष्टाज्योभे विष्णु क्रमवात्सप्रे समस्यत्यहर्वै विष्णुक्रमा रात्रिर्वात्सप्रमेतद्वा इदग्ं सर्वं प्रजापतिः प्रजनयिष्यंश्च प्रजनयित्वा चाहोरात्राभ्यामुभयतः परिगृह्णाति ॥ ( (*tābhyāṃ vai viparyāsameti | yathā mahāṃtamadhvānaṃ vimokagṃ samaśnunīta tādṛktātsa vai purastājyopariṣṭājyobhe viṣṇu kramavātsapre samasyatyaharvai viṣṇukramā rātrirvātsaprametadvā idagṃ sarvaṃ prajāpatiḥ prajanayiṣyaṃśca prajanayitvā cāhorātrābhyāmubhayataḥ parigṛhṇāti ‖ (*) (Śa. Brā. 6-7-4-12)

Just as one who travels to a distant land keeps his journey and his rest in alternation (one after the other), so the Viṣṇukrama and the Vatsapra rite are to be in alternation. But both are to be joined together before the dīkṣā and again after the dīkṣā period. The Viṣṇukrama is the day and the Vatsapra is the night. At the beginning and at the end the two are joined together; only in the middle period do the two revolve in alternation. Prajāpati, at the beginning of the time of creation and again after creation, made the whole universe created by himself to be encompassed by day and night. In the same way the performer of the sacrifice too

### Printed p. 98
when he wishes to create the universe in the form of the sacrifice, must make both sides of his creation, at its beginning and after creation, to be encompassed by the day and night that take the forms of Viṣṇukrama and Vatsapra.

तदाहुः । युदहर्विष्णुक्रमा रात्रिर्वात्सप्रमथो एवाहन्भवतो न रात्र्यां कथमस्यापि रात्र्यांकृते भेभदत इत्येतद्वा एने अदो दीक्षमाणः पुरस्तादपराह्न उभे समस्यति रात्रिर्हैतद्यदपराह्नोऽथै ने एतत्संनिवप्स्यन्नु परिष्टात्पूर्वाह्न उभे समस्यत्यहर्हैतद्यत्पूर्वाह्न एवमु हास्योभे एवाहन् कृते भवत उभे रात्र्यां । (*tadāhuḥ | yudaharviṣṇukramā rātrirvātsapramatho evāhanbhavato na rātryāṃ kathamasyāpi rātryāṃkṛte bhebhadata ityetadvā ene ado dīkṣamāṇaḥ purastādaparāhna ubhe samasyati rātrirhaitadyadaparāhno 'thai ne etatsaṃnivapsyannu pariṣṭātpūrvāhna ubhe samasyatyaharhaitadyatpūrvāhna evamu hāsyobhe evāhan kṛte bhavata ubhe rātryāṃ |*) (Śa. Brā. 6-7-4-13)

An objection may also arise against the above injunction. If the Viṣṇukramas reflect the day and the Vatsapra reflects the night, the two should be performed in alternation at their respective times; what is the meaning of performing both rites in the day alone? When this objection arises, the injunction that answers it is as follows: the one who has been consecrated first joins the two rites together and performs them in the afternoon. The afternoon too has the form of night. Thus, after joining the two, they are performed together in the forenoon. The forenoon has the form of day. In this way both rites are performed in the day, and likewise both are performed in the night.

स यदहः संनिवप्स्यन्तास्त् तदहः प्रातरुदित आदित्ये भस्मैव प्रथममुद्वपति भस्मोदुप्य वाचं विसृजते वाचं विसृज्य समिधमादधाति समिध मादाय भस्मापोऽभ्यवहरति यथैव तस्याभ्यवहरणं तथापादाय भस्मनः प्रत्येत्योखायामोपॊप्योतिष्ठतेऽथ प्रायश्चित्तीकरोति ॥ ( (*sa yadahaḥ saṃnivapsyantāst tadahaḥ prātarudita āditye bhasmaiva prathamamudvapati bhasmodupya vācaṃ visṛjate vācaṃ visṛjya samidhamādadhāti samidha mādāya bhasmāpo 'bhyavaharati yathaiva tasyābhyavaharaṇaṃ tathāpādāya bhasmanaḥ pratyetyokhāyāmopòpyotiṣṭhate 'tha prāyaścittīkaroti ‖ (*) (Śa. Brā. 6-7-4-14)

On the day on which he wishes to join the two rites in the morning after sunrise, he first scatters ashes. Then, having uttered speech and taken up a kindling stick, he throws that ashes into the water. Then, in the same way, he takes a small remainder of the same ashes and returns. Then he puts it into the vessel called Ukhā and stands devoutly before Agni. After that the expiatory rite is performed.

स यदि विष्णु क्रमीयमहः स्यात् । विष्णुक्रमान् क्रान्ता वात्सप्रेणोपतिष्ठेताथ यदि वात्सप्रीयं वात्सप्रेणोपस्थाय विष्णुक्रमान् क्रान्ता वात्सप्रमन्ततः कुर्याद्यथा प्रयाय न विमुंचेत्तादृक्तदथ यद्वात्सप- मंततः करोति प्रतिष्ठा वै वात्सप्रं यथा प्रतिष्ठापयेदवसाययेत्तादृक्तत्तस्मादु वात्सप्रमेवांततः कुर्यात् ॥ (*sa yadi viṣṇu kramīyamahaḥ syāt | viṣṇukramān krāntā vātsapreṇopatiṣṭhetātha yadi vātsaprīyaṃ vātsapreṇopasthāya viṣṇukramān krāntā vātsapramantataḥ kuryādyathā prayāya na vimuṃcettādṛktadatha yadvātsapa- maṃtataḥ karoti pratiṣṭhā vai vātsapraṃ yathā pratiṣṭhāpayedavasāyayettādṛktattasmādu vātsapramevāṃtataḥ kuryāt ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 6-7-4-15)

### Printed p. 99
If the day of the rite is a Viṣṇukrama day, he should cross over (complete) the Viṣṇukrama and worship Agni with the Vatsapra. If it is a day of the Vatsapra rite, he should first perform the Vatsapra, then finish the Viṣṇukrama, and then perform the Vatsapra again. He should not finish in the Viṣṇukrama itself; if he does so, the state arises of having gone round for travelling without rest. If he brings it to completion with the performance of the Vatsapra rite, complete rest is obtained. Having thus declared the greatness of the Vatsapra, the text again explains the relation between Agni and the Vatsapra sūkta in the following way.

वनीवाह्वेताग्निं बिभ्रदित्याहुः । देवाश्चासुराश्चोभये प्राजापत्या अस्पर्धंत ते देवाश्चक्रमचरञ्खालमसुरा आसंस्ते देवाश्चक्रेण चरंत एतत्कर्मापश्यंश्चक्रेण हि वै देवाश्चरंत एतत्कर्मापश्यंस्तस्मादानस एव पौरोडाशेषु यजूग्‌ष्यनसोऽग्नौ ॥ (*vanīvāhvetāgniṃ bibhradityāhuḥ | devāścāsurāścobhaye prājāpatyā aspardhaṃta te devāścakramacarañkhālamasurā āsaṃste devāścakreṇa caraṃta etatkarmāpaśyaṃścakreṇa hi vai devāścaraṃta etatkarmāpaśyaṃstasmādānasa eva pauroḍāśeṣu yajūgṣyanaso 'gnau ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 6-8-1-1)

There is an injunction, "Let Agni be urged forward and sent on while he is being borne." Why? Its reason is explained as follows.

The gods and the Asuras, both born of Prajāpati, used to contend with each other. The gods used to travel on a chariot furnished with wheels; the Asuras stayed in their own place. Because the gods travelled on a wheeled chariot, they obtained the vision of this prescribed rite. If they had not travelled on a wheeled chariot, they would not have had the vision of it. Therefore the Paurodāśa rite belongs to the chariot.

स यो वनीवाह्यते । देवान् कर्मणैति दैवग्ं हास्य कर्म कृतं भवत्यथ यो न वनीवाह्यतेऽसुरान् कर्मणैत्यसुरग्ं हास्यकर्मकृतं भवति ॥ (*sa yo vanīvāhyate | devān karmaṇaiti daivagṃ hāsya karma kṛtaṃ bhavatyatha yo na vanīvāhyate 'surān karmaṇaityasuragṃ hāsyakarmakṛtaṃ bhavati ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 6-8-1-2)

He who urges Agni on and makes him travel approaches the gods alone, by the means of venerable rite. If he does not so urge Agni, he approaches only the Asuras through his rites. Such a rite, which does not urge Agni, has the character of the Asuras.

### Printed p. 100
तद्वैक आहुः । स्वयं ना एष वनीवाहितो विष्णुक्रमैर्ना एष प्रयाति वात्सप्रेणावस्यतीति न तथा विद्याद्दैवं वा अस्य तत्पयाणं यद्विष्णुक्रमादैवमवसानं तद्वात्सप्रमथास्येदं मानुषं प्रयाणं यदिदं प्रयाति मानुषमवसानं यदस्यति ॥ (*tadvaika āhuḥ | svayaṃ nā eṣa vanīvāhito viṣṇukramairnā eṣa prayāti vātsapreṇāvasyatīti na tathā vidyāddaivaṃ vā asya tatpayāṇaṃ yadviṣṇukramādaivamavasānaṃ tadvātsapramathāsyedaṃ mānuṣaṃ prayāṇaṃ yadidaṃ prayāti mānuṣamavasānaṃ yadasyati ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 6-8-1-3)

But here room remains for another doubt: Agni needs no other help to urge him on; with the help of the Viṣṇukrama he himself moves and travels, and by the Vatsapra he reaches the end of that travelling. If it is objected thus, the objection is wrong. Why? If Agni's movement and travelling occurred without human effort, it would benefit only the gods and be of no use to men. Therefore, if a man employs Agni by his own act, the fruit of it comes to the man; otherwise it does not.

प्रजापतिरेषोऽग्निः । उभयमेतत्प्रजापतिर‍्यच्च देवा यच्च मनुष्यास्तद्यद्विष्णु क्रमवात्सप्रे भवतो यद्वेवास्य दैवग्ं रूपं तदस्य तेन संस्करोत्यथ यद्वनीवाह्यते तदेवास्य मानुषग्ं रूपं तदस्य तेन संस्करोति स ह वा एतग्ं सर्वं कृत्स्नं प्रजापतिग्ं संस्करोति य एवं विद्वान्वनीवाह्यते तस्मादु वनीवाह्यतैव ॥ (*prajāpatireṣo 'gniḥ | ubhayametatprajāpatiryacca devā yacca manuṣyāstadyadviṣṇu kramavātsapre bhavato yadvevāsya daivagṃ rūpaṃ tadasya tena saṃskarotyatha yadvanīvāhyate tadevāsya mānuṣagṃ rūpaṃ tadasya tena saṃskaroti sa ha vā etagṃ sarvaṃ kṛtsnaṃ prajāpatigṃ saṃskaroti ya evaṃ vidvānvanīvāhyate tasmādu vanīvāhyataiva ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 6-8-1-4)

Prajāpati is Agni, and Agni is Prajāpati. Prajāpati, who has the form of Agni, is all the gods and men. By the performance of the Viṣṇukrama and Vatsapra he fashions the divine form. By the act that urges Agni forward he fashions the human form. Therefore, those who urge Agni forward with the help of both these acts fashion, as it were, the complete form of Prajāpati. Thus the greatness of the Vatsapra is praised.

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अच्छाम इति चतुर्थेऽनुवाकेऽष्टादश सूक्तानि । तत्र प्र होतेति दशर्चं
> चतुर्थं सूक्तं । प्र होता दशेत्यनुक्रांतं । अनुवर्तमानाद्भालंदनस्य वत्सप्रेरार्षं । अनादेशपरिभाषया त्रैष्टुभं ! आग्नेयं ॥ पूर्वसूक्तेन सहोक्तो
> विनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In the fourth anuvāka, which begins with अच्छाम (*acchāma*), there are eighteen sūktas. Among them the fourth sūkta, प्र होता (*pra hotā*), has ten ṛks; the Anukramaṇī says प्र होता दश (*pra hotā daśa*). By continuation from the preceding sūkta, its seer is Vatsapri, son of Bhalandana. By the rule for what is not specified, its metre is Triṣṭubh. Its deity is Agni. Its use in ritual (viniyoga) has been stated together with the preceding sūkta.

### Printed p. 101

##### Anuvāda

In the fourth anuvāka of the tenth maṇḍala of the Ṛgveda Saṃhitā, beginning with the ṛk अच्छाम (*acchāma*), there are eighteen sūktas. Of these, the first three sūktas have been explained in the previous chapter. The sūkta प्रहोता (*prahotā*), which is now to be begun, is the fourth in this anuvāka. It has ten ṛks. The Anukramaṇī says प्रहोता दश (*prahotā daśa*). The seer of this sūkta is Vatsapri Bhālandana; the deity is Agni; the metre is Triṣṭubh. The use of this sūkta in ritual has been stated in the introduction of the previous sūkta.

Sūkta 46
Maṇḍala 10; Anuvāka 4; Sūkta 46; Aṣṭaka 8; Adhyāya 1; Vargas 1, 2.
Number of ṛks in the sūkta: 10.
Seer: Vatsapri.
Deity: Agni.
Metre: Triṣṭubh.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र होता॑ जा॒तो म॒हान्न॑भो॒विन्नृ॒षद्वा॑ सीदद॒पामु॒पस्थे॑ ।  
> दधि॒र्यो धायि॒ स ते॒ वयां॑सि यं॒ता वसू॑नि विध॒ते त॑नू॒पाः ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । होता । जातः । महान् । नभःवित् । नृसद्वा । सीदत् । अपां । उपस्थे !  
> दधिः । यः । धायि । सः । ते । वयांसि । यंता । वसूनि । विधते । तनूपाः ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यो नृषद्वाग्निरसां । अंतरिक्षनामैतत् । अंतरिक्षस्योपस्थ उत्संगे वैद्युतरूपेण निषण्णोऽभूत् । स इदानीं होता यजमानानां होमनिष्पादको जातः
> प्रादुर्भूतो महान् गुणैः पूज्यो नभोविदंतरिक्षस्य वेत्ता । यतस्तत्रोत्पन्नः
> अतस्तस्य ज्ञाता ॥ नृषद्वा नृषु सीदन् । सदेः क्वनिप् कृत्स्वरः ॥ प्र सीदत् । वेद्यां
> प्रसीदति । अपामुपस्थे महिषा अगृभ्यत । ऋग्वे-८-४-१ । इति हि निगमः । यद्वा ।
> अपां । अपसामित्यर्थः । कर्मणामुपस्थ उपस्थाने समीपे वेद्यामुक्तलक्षणः सन्।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The Agni who is नृषद्वा (*nṛṣadvā*) sat in the lap of the अपां (*apāṃ*) — this is a name of the atmosphere — that is, in the form of lightning he was seated in the lap of the atmosphere. He has now become the होता (*hotā*), the accomplisher of the sacrificers' offerings; जातः (*jātaḥ*) means manifested; महान् (*mahān*) means worthy of worship by his qualities; नभोवित् (*nabhovit*) means knower of the atmosphere, since he was born there and therefore knows it. नृषद्वा (*nṛṣadvā*) means one who sits among men. प्र सीदत् (*pra sīdat*) means he sits down on the altar. Sāyaṇa quotes the nigama अपामुपस्थे महिषा अगृभ्यत (*apāmupasthe mahiṣā agṛbhyata*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-4-1). Or else अपां (*apāṃ*) means "of rites"; उपस्थे (*upasthe*) means in the neighbourhood of the rites, he who has the stated character being (seated) on the altar.

### Printed p. 102

> अथवा । अपामुदकानामुपस्थे मध्ये योऽग्निर्हविर्वोढुमसहमानो निगूढः
> स देवैः पुनः प्रार्थित उक्तविधः सन् वेद्यां प्रसीदति । योऽग्निर्दधिर्धाता यज्ञस्य
> धारकः सन् धायि वेद्यां निहितोऽभूत् सोऽग्निर्हे वत्सप्रे विधते परिचरते ते
> तुभ्यं वयांस्यन्नानि वसूनि धनानि च यंता । नियमिता भवतु । किंच तनूपास्ते
> तन्वः पाता च भवत्विति शेषः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Or else, अपां (*apāṃ*) means "of the waters": the Agni who, unable to bear the carrying of the oblation, was hidden in the midst of the waters, and who, being entreated again by the gods and being of the stated character, sits on the altar. The Agni who, as दधिः (*dadhiḥ*), the supporter of the sacrifice, was placed (धायि (*dhāyi*)) on the altar — may that Agni, to you, Vatsapri, who attend (विधते (*vidhate*)) on him, be the controller (यंता (*yaṃtā*)) of food (वयांसि (*vayāṃsi*)) and riches (वसूनि (*vasūni*)). Moreover, may he be तनूपाः (*tanūpāḥ*), the protector of your body; "may be" is to be supplied.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (यः) नृषद्वा (*(yaḥ) nṛṣadvā*)—which Agni
- अपां (*apāṃ*)—of the atmosphere
- उपस्थे (*upasthe*)—in the lap, was in the form of lightning
- महान् (*mahān*)—worthy of worship
- नभोवित् (*nabhovit*)—knower of the atmospheric world
- सः (*saḥ*)—that same Agni
- होता (*hotā*)—as the hotṛ who invokes the gods at the sacrificer's sacrifice
- जातः (*jātaḥ*)—having been born
- प्र सीदत् (*pra sīdat*)—has become manifest on the sacrificial altar
- यः (*yaḥ*)—which Agni
- दधिः (*dadhiḥ*)—as supporter of the sacrifice
- धायि (*dhāyi*)—is seated on the sacrificial altar
- सः (*saḥ*)—that Agni
- विधते (*vidhate*)—who serves
- ते (*te*)—to you (the seer called Vatsapri)
- वयांसि (*vayāṃsi*)—food and other nourishment
- वसूनि (*vasūni*)—riches
- यंता (*yaṃtā*)—may he be the giver (and)
- तनूपाः (*tanūpāḥ*)—may he be the protector of your body

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The Agni who is worthy of worship, who knows the atmospheric world and who dwells in the atmosphere in the form of lightning, has now become manifest and is seated on the sacrificer's altar as the hotṛ whom the gods invoke. May that same Agni, the supporter of the sacrifice, established on the sacrificial altar, give you, O seer Vatsapri, food and other nourishment and riches, and protect you.

##### English translation
A mighty ministrant priest, one cognizant of heaven, though abiding with men, having been born, is seated on the lap of the waters : may he, the upholder (of sacrifice), who has been stationed (on the altar), be to you when offering worship, the giver of food and riches, the protector of your person.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

नृषद्वा (*nṛṣadvā*)—नृषु सीदन् (*nṛṣu sīdan*). One who sits among men, or in the sacrifice that men are performing; that is, the Agni established on the sacrificial altar in the middle of the sacrifice which men are performing.

### Printed p. 103

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

उपस्थे—उत्संगे (*upasthe—utsaṃge*). "On the lap", that is, in the middle region of the atmosphere.
नभोवित्—अंतरिक्षस्य नेत्ता (*nabhovit—aṃtarikṣasya nettā*). Because he was born there, he is its knower; the meaning is: one who knows its nature, since he was born in the atmosphere itself.
अपां (*apāṃ*)—this is a name of the atmosphere. The words आपः समुद्रः (*āpaḥ samudraḥ*) and the like are read among the names of the atmosphere (Ni.). The word अपः (*apaḥ*) also means "work" (Ni.). In this context कर्मणामुपस्थ उपस्थाने समीपे वेद्यां (*karmaṇāmupastha upasthāne samīpe vedyāṃ*): it can also be said to mean "on the sacrificial altar where the sacrificial work is performed". For the words अपामुपस्थे (*apāmupasthe*) the commentator has given another sense as well: ु अपां मध्यॆ योऽग्नि हर्विवोडुमसहमानो निगूढः स देवैः पुनः प्रार्थितस्सन् वेद्यां प्रसीदति । (*u apāṃ madhyè yo 'gni harvivoḍumasahamāno nigūḍhaḥ sa devaiḥ punaḥ prārthitassan vedyāṃ prasīdati |*). Formerly Agni, for some reason, became weary of the task of carrying the oblation to the gods and, telling no one, ran away and hid in the middle of the water. The gods, learning of this with great effort, went there, entreated Agni in many ways, gave him boons and the like, appeased him, and made him take up the carrying of the oblation again. The commentator indicates that the Agni who rose again from the water has now come and sat on the sacrificial altar. Since this matter is explained in the 51st sūkta of this same maṇḍala, we shall describe it at length when we explain that sūkta.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इ॒मं वि॒धंतो॑ अ॒पां स॒धस्थे॑ प॒शुं न न॒ष्टं प॒द्यरनु॑ ग्मन् ।  
> गुहा॒ चतं॑तमु॒शिजो॒ नमो॑भिरि॒च्छंतो॒ धीरा॒ भृग॑वोऽविं  
> दन् ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इमं । विधंतः । अपां । सधस्थे । पशुं । न । नष्टं । पद्यैः । अनु । ग्मन् ।  
> गुहा । चतंतं । उशिजः । नमभिः । इच्छंतः । धीराः । भृगवः । अविंदन् ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इममग्निमपामुदकानां सधस्थे सहस्थाने मध्यॆ निगूढं विधंतः परिचरंतः
> उपचारं वदंतो ऋषयश्च पद्यैः पलायनमार्गव्यंजकैर्नष्टं चोरादिभिरपहृतं

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa takes *imamagniṃ* (this Agni) as hidden in the midst of the waters, सधस्थे (*sadhasthe*) being "the common abode". The seers, विधंतः (*vidhaṃtaḥ*), serve him, that is, speak words of homage; and as one follows a lost animal, stolen by thieves and the like, by footprints that mark the way of its flight, they followed him.

### Printed p. 104

> पशुमिवानुग्मन् । अनुजग्मुः ॥ गमर्लुजि मंत्रे घसेति ज्लेर्लुक् ॥ तेषु मध्यॆ
> गुहा गुहायां चतंतं । चतिर्गतिकर्मा । अप्पु विलीनमित्यर्थः । उशिजः कामयमाना नमोभिर्नमस्कारैः स्तोत्रैर्वेच्छंत आत्मन इच्छंतो धीरा धीमंतो
> भृगवोऽविंदन् । लब्धवंतः । अग्नेर्हविर्वोढुमसहमानस्य पलाय्याप्सु प्रवेशो
> देवानामन्वेषणं जाग्रेस्रयो ज्यायांस इत्यत्र स्पष्टमाम्नातं । तै. स. २-६-६ ।
> अन्वेषणं कुर्वतां मध्यॆ भृगूणामग्निलाभ इमं विधंतो अपां सधस्थे द्वितादधुर्भृगव इत्यत्रोक्तं ॥ ऋ-२-४-२ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

अनुग्मन् (*anugman*) is अनुजग्मुः (*anujagmuḥ*). Among those waters, गुहा (*guhā*) in a cave, चतंतं (*cataṃtaṃ*): चति (*cati*) is a root meaning motion; the sense is "dissolved, hidden in the waters". The उशिजः (*uśijaḥ*), desiring (him), नमोभिः (*namobhiḥ*) with homages, with hymns of praise, wishing him for themselves, the wise Bhṛgus अविंदन् (*aviṃdan*), found him. That Agni, unable to carry the oblation, fled and entered the waters, and that the gods searched for him, is plainly handed down in the passage जाग्रेस्रयो ज्यायांस (*jāgresrayo jyāyāṃsa*) (Tai. Saṃ. 2-6-6). That among those searching the Bhṛgus obtained Agni is stated in इमं विधंतो अपां सधस्थे द्वितादधुर्भृगव (*imaṃ vidhaṃto apāṃ sadhasthe dvitādadhurbhṛgava*) (Ṛ. 2-4-2).
**NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इमं (*imaṃ*)—this Agni
- अपां (*apāṃ*)—of the waters
- सधस्थे (*sadhasthe*)—in the place of confluence
- विधंतः (*vidhaṃtaḥ*)—the seers who were serving or appeasing (him)
- नष्टं (*naṣṭaṃ*)—stolen by thieves and the like
- पशुं (*paśuṃ*)—cattle
- पदैः (*padaiḥ*)—as one goes following and searches by footprints
- अनुग्यन् (*anugyan*)—followed and searched
- गुहा (*guhā*)—in the middle of that water
- चतंतं (*cataṃtaṃ*)—Agni, who had gone and hidden
- उशिजः (*uśijaḥ*)—desiring to bring (him) back again
- नमोभिः (*namobhiḥ*)—with recitations of praise accompanied by homage and the like
- इच्छंतः (*icchaṃtaḥ*)—wishing
- धीराः (*dhīrāḥ*)—the wise
- भृगवः (*bhṛgavaḥ*)—the seers beginning with Bhṛgu
- अविंदन् (*aviṃdan*)—obtained Agni

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

To seek Agni, who was hidden in the midst of the waters, the seers, devoted to the service of Agni, searched as one searches, following by their footprints, for cattle stolen by thieves. Wishing (praying for) Agni, who was hidden secretly in the midst of the water so that no one knew, with homages and hymns, the wise Bhṛgu and the other seers finally came and reached the place where Agni was.

##### English translation
The Rishis worshipping him, (when hiding) in the midst of the waters, followed him by his footprints (as men follow) an animal that is lost : the wise Bhrigus desiring his presence, and anxious (to find him), dis- covered him by their prayers lurking in the cave.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

(What was said in the previous Ṛk is here said again in another way.) भृगवः गुहा चतंतं इमं (अग्निं) अविंदन् (*bhṛgavaḥ guhā cataṃtaṃ imaṃ (agniṃ) aviṃdan*): the Bhṛgus first discovered

### Printed p. 105
Agni, who was hidden in the cave in the form of water, and obtained his favour; this is the purport of the present Ṛk. When trouble came upon Agni's brothers, Agni took fright and hid in a cave, and the gods tried to find him: these matters were explained briefly earlier, quoting the Taittirīya Saṃhitā passage. Since the word अप् (*ap*) means not only water but also the atmosphere, the purport in this context can also be stated with reference to the lightning-fire (vaidyuta Agni) dwelling in the atmosphere. Then one must examine the manner in which the lightning-fire is hidden in a cave, how it became visible only to the Bhṛgus and to none of the other gods, and hence the distinction of the Bhṛgus over the other gods. This topic is explained in two parts: first, the distinction of the Bhṛgus; second, the purport of the Bhṛgus' obtaining the Agni who was within the cave.

When it says भृगवः अविंदन् (*bhṛgavaḥ aviṃdan*), the meaning is the seers born in the line of Bhṛgu. Some of the seers born in the lineage of Bhṛgu, who arose from a divine portion, are seers of certain sūktas of the Ṛk Saṃhitā. Bhṛgu too is the seer of two sūktas. This famous Bhṛgu is one of the seven most excellent seers belonging to the group of human beings (Atri, Bhṛgu, Kutsa and so on); so it is customary to say. But there are also Vedic passages that praise Atri and the other seers as not human, but as divine principles reflecting the spiritual principle; or there is also the view that, since all of them arose from a divine portion, they belong to the group of the gods.

भृगुवैर्न वारुणिः । वरुणं पितरमुपससार ॥ (*bhṛguvairna vāruṇiḥ | varuṇaṃ pitaramupasasāra ‖*) (Tai. U. 3-1).
In such places Bhṛgu is clearly stated to be the son of Varuṇa. Telling how Bhṛgu was born from Varuṇa, the Bṛhaddevatā relates Bhṛgu's origin in the form of a story, as follows.

त्रिसांवत्सरिकं सत्त्रं प्रजाकामः प्रजापतिः । आहरत्सहितः साध्यैर्विश्वेदैवै सहेति च ॥ (*trisāṃvatsarikaṃ sattraṃ prajākāmaḥ prajāpatiḥ | āharatsahitaḥ sādhyairviśvedaivai saheti ca ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 5-97)
Prajāpati desired offspring. Then, together with the Sādhyas and the Viśvedevas, he performed a sattra lasting three years.

तत्र वाग्दीक्षणीयायां आजगाम शरीरिणे । तां दृष्ट्वा युगपत्तत्र कस्याथ वरुणस्य च ॥ (*tatra vāgdīkṣaṇīyāyāṃ ājagāma śarīriṇe | tāṃ dṛṣṭvā yugapattatra kasyātha varuṇasya ca ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 5-98)
14

### Printed p. 106
Then Vāc, the goddess of speech, came to the place of that sattra in the form of a beautiful embodied woman. On seeing her, both Prajāpati and Varuṇa were at once overcome and bewildered by her divine beauty.

शुक्रं चस्कंद तद्वायुरग्नौ प्रास्यद्यदृच्छया । करोऽर्चिभ्यो भृगुर्जज्ञे अंगारेष्टंगिराऋषिः ॥ (*śukraṃ caskaṃda tadvāyuragnau prāsyadyadṛcchayā | karo 'rcibhyo bhṛgurjajñe aṃgāreṣṭaṃgirāṛṣiḥ ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 5.99)
Then the seed of both of them was discharged. Vāyu, according to his power, caused that seed to spread in the fire. Then from that flame Bhṛgu was born; from the embers Aṅgiras was born: this is the account of Bhṛgu's birth. From this narrative, of the nature of an itihāsa, it is understood that Bhṛgu has the divine portion of both Prajāpati and Varuṇa. The Aitareya Brāhmaṇa explains this same matter and praises Bhṛgu's divine portion. But in many respects the explanation given above differs from that of the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa.

प्रजापतिर्वै स्वां दुहितरमभ्यध्यायत् दिवमित्यन्य आहुरुषसमित्यन्ये . . . . . . . . . इदं प्रजापते रेतः सिक्तमधावत्तत्सरोऽभवत्ते देवा अब्रुवन्मेदं प्रजापते रेतोऽदुषदिति यदब्रुवन् मेदं प्रजापते रेतोऽदुषदिति तन्मादुषमभवत्तन्मादुषस्य मादुषत्वं माडुषं ह वॆ नामैतद्यन्मानुषं तन्नानुषं सन्मानुषमित्याचक्षते परोक्षेण परोक्षप्रिया इव हि देवाः ॥ (*prajāpatirvai svāṃ duhitaramabhyadhyāyat divamityanya āhuruṣasamityanye . . . . . . . . . idaṃ prajāpate retaḥ siktamadhāvattatsaro 'bhavatte devā abruvanmedaṃ prajāpate reto 'duṣaditi yadabruvan medaṃ prajāpate reto 'duṣaditi tanmāduṣamabhavattanmāduṣasya māduṣatvaṃ māḍuṣaṃ ha vè nāmaitadyanmānuṣaṃ tannānuṣaṃ sanmānuṣamityācakṣate parokṣeṇa parokṣapriyā iva hi devāḥ ‖*) (Ai. Brā. 2-22)
Prajāpati looked on his own daughter and was infatuated. Some say she was the presiding deity of the heavenly world, others that she was Uṣas. She had taken the form of a female deer; he took the form of a male deer and pursued her. The gods, seeing this conduct of Prajāpati and not enduring it, looked for a means to punish him. Among themselves they found no one who could punish Prajāpati. Then they gathered together all the terrible forms of the world and produced one being. When that being pierced Prajāpati, he leapt upward. Because he leapt, he was called "मृग (*mṛga*)". The female deer became named "रोहिणि (*rohiṇi*)". As soon as the three-jointed arrow (iṣu) pierced him, Prajāpati's seed was discharged. It became a lake. Then the gods thought, "Prajāpati's seed must not be spoiled (māduṣat)". Hence it became "मादुष (*māduṣa*)". "मानुष (*mānuṣa*)" means connected with man. Because the gods love the indirect, they called "मानुष (*mānuṣa*)" "मादुष (*māduṣa*)".

### Printed p. 107
तदग्निना पर्यादधुस्तन्मरुतो धून्वंस्तदग्निर्न प्राच्यावयत्तदग्निना वैश्वानरेण पर्यादधुस्तन्मरुतो धून्वंस्तदग्निर्वैश्वानरः प्राच्यावयत्तस्य यद्रेतसः प्रथममुददीप्यत तदसावादित्योऽभवद्यद्दितीयमासीत्तद्भृगुरभवत्तं वरुणो नृगृह्णीत तस्मात्स भृगुर्वारुणिरथ यत्कृतीयमदीदेदिव त आदित्या अभवन्येऽंगारा आसंस्तेऽंगिरसो अभवन् ॥ (*tadagninā paryādadhustanmaruto dhūnvaṃstadagnirna prācyāvayattadagninā vaiśvānareṇa paryādadhustanmaruto dhūnvaṃstadagnirvaiśvānaraḥ prācyāvayattasya yadretasaḥ prathamamudadīpyata tadasāvādityo 'bhavadyadditīyamāsīttadbhṛgurabhavattaṃ varuṇo nṛgṛhṇīta tasmātsa bhṛgurvāruṇiratha yatkṛtīyamadīdediva ta ādityā abhavanye 'ṃgārā āsaṃste 'ṃgiraso abhavan ‖*) (Ai. Brā. 2-24)
With the good purpose that Prajāpati's seed should not be spoiled, they made Agni surround it. Then the Maruts blew on that Agni and made it blaze. Agni could not make it move. Then they made it be surrounded by Agni Vaiśvānara, and blew on that Agni Vaiśvānara and made it blaze. Then Agni Vaiśvānara was able to make it move. One part of Prajāpati's seed, made to blaze, was transformed into the form of Āditya; another part appeared in the form of Bhṛgu. Varuṇa took him over. Therefore he became famed as भृगुर्वारुणिः (*bhṛgurvāruṇiḥ*).

The same Bhṛgu who in the Taittirīya and Śatapatha Brāhmaṇas is said to have come forth from Prajāpati's seed and to have been taken over by Varuṇa, is stated in the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa to be a portion of Īśvara in the form of Indra.

इंद्रस्य सुषुवाणस्य त्रेधेंद्रियं वीर्यं परापतत् । भृगुस्तृतीयमभवत् । श्रायंतीयं तृतीयं । सरस्वती तृतीयं । भार्गवो होता भवति । श्रायंतीयं ब्रह्मसामं भवति । वारवंतीयमग्निष्टोमसामं ॥ ( (*iṃdrasya suṣuvāṇasya tredheṃdriyaṃ vīryaṃ parāpatat | bhṛgustṛtīyamabhavat | śrāyaṃtīyaṃ tṛtīyaṃ | sarasvatī tṛtīyaṃ | bhārgavo hotā bhavati | śrāyaṃtīyaṃ brahmasāmaṃ bhavati | vāravaṃtīyamagniṣṭomasāmaṃ ‖ (*) (Tai. Brā. 1-8-2-5)
The virility of Indra, who desired the sacrifice, was discharged divided into three parts. From one part Bhṛgu, from another the Śrāyantīya Sāman, and from the remaining third Sarasvatī came into being. Among these the Bhārgava is the Hotṛ.

Although there are such differences of opinion regarding Bhṛgu's origin, there is nowhere any difference of view that he arose from a divine portion. Even so, in some places it is evident that Bhṛgu reveals his own ignorance. For example—

भृगुर्ह वै वारुणिः । वरुणं पितरं विद्ययातिमेने तद्ध वरुणो विदां चकाराति वै मा विद्यया मन्यत इति । स होवाच । प्राज् पुत्रक व्रजतात्तत्र यत्पश्येस्तद्दृष्ट्वा दक्षिणा व्रजतात्तत्र यत्पश्येत्तद्दृष्ट्वा ... मे आचक्षीया इति ॥ (*bhṛgurha vai vāruṇiḥ | varuṇaṃ pitaraṃ vidyayātimene taddha varuṇo vidāṃ cakārāti vai mā vidyayā manyata iti | sa hovāca | prāj putraka vrajatāttatra yatpaśyestaddṛṣṭvā dakṣiṇā vrajatāttatra yatpaśyettaddṛṣṭvā ... me ācakṣīyā iti ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 11-6-1-1)

### Printed p. 108
Bhṛgu, son of Varuṇa, thought himself more learned than his father Varuṇa. Varuṇa learned of this conceit of wisdom in Bhṛgu and understood: "He thinks he is greater than I in knowledge."

Then he called his son Bhṛgu and ordered: "Child, go about in all directions. First go to the eastern direction and, whatever you see there, learn it and tell me. After that go to the south, then to the west and the north, and finally to the north-east, see with your own eyes the doings in each place and report them to me." Then Bhṛgu travelled in all directions and saw that the creatures in all directions were full of hatred and envy, injuring one another, slaying one another. Astonished at it, he came back and told his father all this. Then the father said that all these were marks of saṃsāra, and that if one gains knowledge through rites such as the agnihotra one can escape this terrible saṃsāra. Thus Bhṛgu received Varuṇa's instruction. The teaching of truth set out in the Taittirīya Upaniṣad differs in many respects from the teaching here. In still other places Bhṛgu is praised as a seer who knew the truth of the sacrifice.

आपो ना अस्पर्धंत वयं पूर्वं यज्ञं वक्ष्यामो वयमिति याश्चेमाः पूरैद्युर्वसतीवरो गृह्यंते याश्च प्रातरेकधनास्ता भृगुरपश्यदापो वै स्पर्धंत इति ता एतयर्चासमज्ञपयत्समन्यायंती . . . . (*āpo nā aspardhaṃta vayaṃ pūrvaṃ yajñaṃ vakṣyāmo vayamiti yāścemāḥ pūraidyurvasatīvaro gṛhyaṃte yāśca prātarekadhanāstā bhṛgurapaśyadāpo vai spardhaṃta iti tā etayarcāsamajñapayatsamanyāyaṃtī . . . .*) (Ai. Brā. 2-20)
**NOTE**
Once a rivalry arose, in the task of bearing the sacrifice, between the waters named Vasatīvarī, collected the previous day, and the waters named Ekadhanā, collected that morning. When discord arose among them, the smooth conduct of the sacrificial work was hindered. Then Bhṛgu, perceiving the discord of these waters, recited the mantra आपो न देवीरुप यंति (*āpo na devīrupa yaṃti*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-82-2) and brought harmony among them. So it is stated.

In sum, Bhṛgu's greatness, as one arisen from a divine portion and as a divine knower, is praised in many Śruti passages. In the Ṛk Saṃhitā he is famed as the author of the sūktas 99-5 and 10-19.
**NOTE**

Moreover, in the excellent conduct of the sacrifice Bhṛgu is praised as the model man for all human beings. For example—

### Printed p. 109

In short, in many Śruti passages the greatness of Bhṛgu is praised: he is described as of divine origin and as possessing divine knowledge. In the Ṛksaṃhitā he is famed as the author of the sūktas 9-99 **NOTE** and 10-19.

Moreover, in the proper conduct of sacrifice Bhṛgu is praised as the ideal man for all mankind. For example:

उत त्वा भृगुवच्चुचे मनुष्पदग्न आहुत । अंगिरस्वद्धनामहे ॥ (*uta tvā bhṛguvaccuce manuṣpadagna āhuta | aṃgirasvaddhanāmahe ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-43-13)

O pure Agni, as the sage Bhṛgu did, as Manu did, and as the Aṅgirases did, we worship you and satisfy you. We call you to our help. In this way the verse shows Bhṛgu's role as guide in the performance of rites.

The ṛṣis born in the lineage of this Bhṛgu, who was foremost in ritual and wise, are also known as Bhṛgus. Some of them are famed as seers of certain sūktas of the Ṛksaṃhitā. They are:

1. इटभार्गवः (*iṭabhārgavaḥ*) — Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-171
2. कविर्भार्गवः (*kavirbhārgavaḥ*) — Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-43-49; 9-75-79
3. कृत्स्नुर्भार्गवः (*kṛtsnurbhārgavaḥ*) — Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-79
4. गृत्समदो भार्गवः (*gṛtsamado bhārgavaḥ*) (शौनकः (*śaunakaḥ*)) — Ṛ. Saṃ. 2.1-3; 2-843; 9-86-46-47-48 **NOTE**
5. च्यवनोभार्गवः (*cyavanobhārgavaḥ*) — Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-19
6. जमदग्निर्भार्गवः (*jamadagnirbhārgavaḥ*) — Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-62-16, 17, 18; 8-101; 9-92; 9-95; 9-67-16, 17-18; 10-110; 10.137-6; 10-167
7. नेमो भार्गवः (*nemo bhārgavaḥ*) — Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-100-1, 2, 3; 6-12
8. प्रयोगो भार्गवः (*prayogo bhārgavaḥ*) — Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-102
9. वेनो भार्गवः (*veno bhārgavaḥ*) — Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-85; 10-123
10. सोमाहुतिर्भार्गवः (*somāhutirbhārgavaḥ*) — Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-4-5, 6, 7
11. स्युमरश्मिर्भार्गवः (*syumaraśmirbhārgavaḥ*) — Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-77; 10-78

Just as Bhṛgu is skilled in ritual, so these maharṣis born in the Bhṛgu lineage are also famed as skilled in ritual. Praising their skill in ritual, Sāyaṇa has explained as follows:

एतं नां स्तोममश्विनावकर्मातक्षाम बृगवो न रथं । न्यमृक्षाम योषणां न मर्ये नित्यं न सूनुं तनयं दधानाः ॥ (*etaṃ nāṃ stomamaśvināvakarmātakṣāma bṛgavo na rathaṃ | nyamṛkṣāma yoṣaṇāṃ na marye nityaṃ na sūnuṃ tanayaṃ dadhānāḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-39-14)

In this Ṛk the skill in ritual of the Bhṛgus is described. Here, giving the etymology of the word भृगवः (*bhṛgavaḥ*), Sāyaṇa explains: कर्मयोगादृभवो भृगव उच्यंते (*karmayogādṛbhavo bhṛgava ucyaṃte*).

### Printed p. 110

The skill in ritual of the Ṛbhus has been examined and explained at length earlier. Here the distinctive character of the Bhṛgus is shown. Likewise:

एनेदिंद्राय वृषभाय वृष्टे ब्रह्माकर्म भृगवो न रथं । न चिद्यदा नः सख्या वियोषदसन्न उग्रोविता तनूपाः ॥ (*enediṃdrāya vṛṣabhāya vṛṣṭe brahmākarma bhṛgavo na rathaṃ | na cidyadā naḥ sakhyā viyoṣadasanna ugrovitā tanūpāḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-16-20)

Here the word भृगु (*bhṛgu*) is explained as "shining takṣans (carpenters, woodworkers)".

Thus the Bhṛgus are famed for the very power for which the Ṛbhus are famed. As the Ṛbhus have, so the Bhṛgus too have a share in the sacrifice together with the gods.

विश्वेर्देष्ट्यस्त्रिभिरेकादशैरिहाद्भिर्मरुद्भिर्भृगुभिः सचाभुवा । सजोषसा उषसा सूर्येण च सोमं पिबतमश्विना ॥ (*viśverdeṣṭyastribhirekādaśairihādbhirmarudbhirbhṛgubhiḥ sacābhuvā | sajoṣasā uṣasā sūryeṇa ca somaṃ pibatamaśvinā ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-35-3)

O Aśvins, in this sacrifice drink the Soma together with all the gods, with the Maruts, with the Bhṛgus and with Uṣas. In such passages the Bhṛgus' share in the sacrifice together with the gods is clear.

Bhṛgu and the ṛṣis born in his lineage are famed as the first to establish the sacrifice. The favour of Agni, the root form of the sacrifice, was obtained by the Bhṛgu maharṣis as by no one else. How close and sacred the relation between these ṛṣis and Agni is can be seen from some examples.

1. Mātariśvan first brought Agni to the Bhṛgus. (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-60-1)
2. O Agni, the Bhṛgus were the first to establish you among men. (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-58-6; 6-15-2)
3. We pray to and desire Agni, the bestower of what is wished, for the Bhṛgus. (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-2-4)
4. The Ṛbhus, intent on ritual, made Agni full of radiance.
5. The Bhṛgus, with their praises, made Agni full of splendour. (10-122-5)

In these and many other passages it is praised that the ṛṣis born in the Bhṛgu lineage worshipped Agni and established the sacrifice among men for men's welfare. To convey this same greatness, Sāyaṇa has given the etymology of the word भृगवः (*bhṛgavaḥ*) as follows.

### Printed p. 111

भृगवः ऎंब पदद निर्वचनवन्नु ई रीति तिळिसिद्दारॆ. भृगवः—भ्रष्टारो हविषां पापानां भर्जका ना । (*bhṛgavaḥ èṃba padada nirvacanavannu ī rīti tiḷisiddārè. bhṛgavaḥ—bhraṣṭāro haviṣāṃ pāpānāṃ bharjakā nā |*)

Sāyaṇa explains that, since the word means either those who offer oblations or those who destroy sins, and since these ṛṣis had these two kinds of power, this name is theirs. The Nirukta-writers also (say):

अर्चिषि भृगुः संबभूव भृगुर्भृज्यमानो न देहे । (*arciṣi bhṛguḥ saṃbabhūva bhṛgurbhṛjyamāno na dehe |*) (Sukta-3-17) **NOTE**

Born of the flame, Bhṛgu arose from Agni's flame; that is, though boiling within Agni's flame he was not burnt. They state the intent of showing this to all people, namely that this is why he has this name. This wonderful power helps the great sacrificial rite, and likewise helps in the destruction of sin. This greatness is praised in passages such as तेजो यद्भृगुरंगिरसां (*tejo yadbhṛguraṃgirasāṃ*) (Śa. Brā. 1-2-1-13).

The present Ṛk says that the Bhṛgus, possessing such greatness, found Agni hidden in a cave. What is the reason for Agni's having to be hidden in a cave, or what is its meaning? And what is the import of "the Bhṛgus obtained Agni hidden in a secret place"? These two matters are examined here.

That Agni was concealed in a cave is described in different ways in different places. How Agni, when trouble befell his brothers, hid in a secret region out of fear, and how the gods prayed to Agni, has been explained earlier by citing passages of the Taittirīya Saṃhitā. Likewise his entering the cave is described in the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa as follows.

चतुर्धा विहितो ह ना अग्रेऽग्निरास । स यमग्रेऽग्निग्ं होत्राय प्रावृणत स प्राधन्वद्यं द्वितीयं प्रावृणत स प्रैवाधन्वद्यं तृतीयं प्रावृणत स प्रैवाधन्वदथ योऽयमेतर्ह्यग्निः स भीषा निलिल्ये सोऽपः प्रविवेश तं देवा अनुविद्य सहसैवाद्भ्य आनिन्युः सोऽपोऽभितिष्ठॆनावष्टूता स्थ या अप्रपदनग्स्थ याभ्यो वो मामकामं नयंतीति तत आप्त्याः संबभूवुस्त्रितो द्वित एकतः ॥ (*caturdhā vihito ha nā agre 'gnirāsa | sa yamagre 'gnigṃ hotrāya prāvṛṇata sa prādhanvadyaṃ dvitīyaṃ prāvṛṇata sa praivādhanvadyaṃ tṛtīyaṃ prāvṛṇata sa praivādhanvadatha yo 'yametarhyagniḥ sa bhīṣā nililye so 'paḥ praviveśa taṃ devā anuvidya sahasaivādbhya āninyuḥ so 'po 'bhitiṣṭhènāvaṣṭūtā stha yā aprapadanagstha yābhyo vo māmakāmaṃ nayaṃtīti tata āptyāḥ saṃbabhūvustrito dvita ekataḥ ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 1-2-3-1)

In these passages there is no mention of Agni's three brothers. But three other forms of Agni are described separately. The situation is as follows.

Agni took four forms. First they prayed to Agni to grace the office of Hotṛ. Agni, thus first requested, became invisible. When requested a second time, he again became invisible. When requested a third time, he again became invisible. Then, seeing this, the fourth form of Agni too hid itself in a secret region. At that time it entered the water and

### Printed p. 112

concealed itself. Then the gods entered the water and brought out the Agni who was hiding. Agni, enraged because he had not been properly protected, showed his displeasure against the water. At that time three small drops fell from his mouth. From those drops Ekata, Dvita and Trita were born, it says. In the same way another passage elsewhere describes how Agni went beyond Prajāpati's sight and took a hidden form.

प्रजापतिरग्नि रूपाण्यभ्यध्यायत् । स योऽयं कुमारो रूपाण्यन्नप्रविष्ट आसीत्तमन्वैच्छत्सोऽग्निरवेदनु वै मा पिता प्रजापतिरिच्छति हंत तद्रूपमसानि यन्म एष न वेदेति ॥ (*prajāpatiragni rūpāṇyabhyadhyāyat | sa yo 'yaṃ kumāro rūpāṇyannapraviṣṭa āsīttamanvaicchatso 'gniravedanu vai mā pitā prajāpatiricchati haṃta tadrūpamasāni yanma eṣa na vedeti ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 6-2-1-1)

Prajāpati reflected, in the form of meditation, in order to know the forms of Agni. He tried to find the youth who had taken various forms. Agni thought: "My father Prajāpati is trying to find me. I shall take other forms so as not to come within his sight." And so,

स एतान्पंच पशूनपश्यत् । पुरुषमश्वं गामविमजं यदपश्यत्तस्मादेते पशवः ॥ स एतान् पंच पशून् प्राविशत् । स एते पंच पशवोऽभवत्तमु वै प्रजापतिरन्वेवैच्छत् ॥ (*sa etānpaṃca paśūnapaśyat | puruṣamaśvaṃ gāmavimajaṃ yadapaśyattasmādete paśavaḥ ‖ sa etān paṃca paśūn prāviśat | sa ete paṃca paśavo 'bhavattamu vai prajāpatiranvevaicchat ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 6-2-1-2,3)

Prajāpati saw these five creatures: man, horse, cow, ram and goat. Because he saw (paś) them thus, they got the name paśu. Agni entered them and took on their form. Prajāpati was still searching.

स ऐक्षत्त । इमे वा अग्निरिमानेवात्मानमभिसंस्करवै यथा वा अग्निः समिद्वो दीप्यते एवमेषां चक्षुर्दिप्यते . . . . इमे वाग्निरिमानेवात्मानमभिसंस्करवा इति तान्नानादेवताभ्य आलप्सत वैश्वकर्मणं पुरुषं वारुणमश्वमैंद्रमृषभं त्वाष्ट्रमविमाग्नेयमजं ॥ (*sa aikṣatta | ime vā agnirimānevātmānamabhisaṃskaravai yathā vā agniḥ samidvo dīpyate evameṣāṃ cakṣurdipyate . . . . ime vāgnirimānevātmānamabhisaṃskaravā iti tānnānādevatābhya ālapsata vaiśvakarmaṇaṃ puruṣaṃ vāruṇamaśvamaiṃdramṛṣabhaṃ tvāṣṭramavimāgneyamajaṃ ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 6-2-1-5)

Prajāpati, seeing those animals, thought: "These must be the other forms of Agni. I shall make them one with myself. As kindled Agni shines, their eyes shine. As smoke rises from Agni, breath goes out of their mouths. Since all Agni's marks are in them, Agni himself may have taken this form." So he wished to offer them as oblations to the various gods who are Agni's own other forms. The man

### Printed p. 113

to Viśvakarman, the horse to Varuṇa, the bull to Indra, the ram to Tvaṣṭṛ and the goat to Agni: this he resolved to offer.

आत्मा वै यज्ञो नेन्मेयमात्मा विकृष्णोऽसदित्येतेन पशूनेष्ट्वा तत्प्रजापतिरपश्यद्यथैतस्याग्नेरंतं न पर्यैत् ॥ (*ātmā vai yajño nenmeyamātmā vikṛṣṇo 'sadityetena paśūneṣṭvā tatprajāpatirapaśyadyathaitasyāgneraṃtaṃ na paryait ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 6-2-1-7)

Prajāpati, having thus reflected, performed the sacrifice as he had resolved. He offered the animals in due order to the respective gods. Even so, he realised that he had not found the end of Agni.

स ऐक्षत । यमिममात्मानमप्पु प्रापिप्लवं तमन्विच्छानीति तमन्वैच्छद्यदेषामप्पु प्रविद्वा नां प्रत्यतिषत्ता अपः समभरदथ यदस्यां तां मृदं यदुभयग्० संभृत्य मृदं चापश्चेष्टकामकरोत्तस्मादेतदुभयमिष्टका भवति मृच्चापश्च ॥ (*sa aikṣata | yamimamātmānamappu prāpiplavaṃ tamanvicchānīti tamanvaicchadyadeṣāmappu pravidvā nāṃ pratyatiṣattā apaḥ samabharadatha yadasyāṃ tāṃ mṛdaṃ yadubhayag0 saṃbhṛtya mṛdaṃ cāpaśceṣṭakāmakarottasmādetadubhayamiṣṭakā bhavati mṛccāpaśca ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 6-2-1-8)

"I must seek and obtain the form of Agni that has spread in the water." Thinking so, he searched there too, and gathered in one place all the portions of Agni that had spread in the form of water. He gathered the portion of Agni hidden in the clay (earth) contained in the water. Then, joining the water and the clay that held portions of Agni, he made bricks (iṣṭaka). Hence the bricks used for the sacrificial altar also contain portions of Agni. Thus, it says, Prajāpati did a service to creatures by causing Agni, who lay hidden in a secret form, to be transformed into the form of sacrifice.

That Agni is hidden in the forms of animals became known to the gods also, through Prajāpati. This knowledge brought them another benefit: they came to know where Agni, who is beyond sight, lies hidden. How?

एतस्यामाहुत्याग्ं हुतायां । अग्निर्देवेभ्य उदक्रामत्ते देवा अब्रुवन् पशुर्वा अग्निः पशुभिरिममन्विच्छाम स स्वाय रूपायाविर्भवति गौर्वा गवे आश्वोवाश्वय पुरुषो ना पुरुषाय ॥ (*etasyāmāhutyāgṃ hutāyāṃ | agnirdevebhya udakrāmatte devā abruvan paśurvā agniḥ paśubhirimamanvicchāma sa svāya rūpāyāvirbhavati gaurvā gave āśvovāśvaya puruṣo nā puruṣāya ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 6-3-1-22)

Once the gods performed a sattra and offered an oblation addressed to Agni; Agni withdrew from them. Then they said: "Since Agni is hidden in animal forms, let us seek him by animal forms." When they began to seek him with a cow and the other forms, Agni appeared to a man in the form of a man, to a horse in the form of a horse, to a cow in the form of a cow,

### Printed p. 114

it says. In the same way the search for Agni hidden in secrecy is described in many other contexts.

The lightning Agni too, who lies hidden in the root region of the atmosphere beyond the sight of men, is likewise described in many places. For example:

स जायत प्रथमः पस्ता ्यसु महो बुध्ने रजसो अस्य योन् । अपादशीर्षा गुहमानो अंतायोयुवानो वृषभस्य नीळे ॥ (*sa jāyata prathamaḥ pastā yasu maho budhne rajaso asya yon | apādaśīrṣā guhamāno aṃtāyoyuvāno vṛṣabhasya nīḷe ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-1-11)

Here is described the hidden region where Agni lay concealed before being born in the sacrificial house. Where it says that Agni, footless and headless, lay hidden in the secret region of the atmosphere, the words योन् (*yon*) and नीळे (*nīḷe*) are noteworthy. The hidden region that stays concealed, not falling within worldly sight, is also called the seat of Ṛta. It is an utterly secret region. (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-1-12)

It is mentioned in many ways that in some contexts men brought out Agni who lay hidden in a cave so described. Whoever among gods or men is said to be the first to see Agni face to face and make him manifest on earth, with all of them Agni's oneness and identity is stated. In this connection Mātariśvan, Bṛhaspati, Aṅgiras, Atharvan, Bhṛgu and others are famed.

1) That Mātariśvan brought Agni from the atmosphere for Bhṛgu has been stated earlier. Examining one passage as an example among such contexts, one learns the meaning of the word मातरिश्वा (*mātariśvā*), and also that this Mātariśvan who found Agni is himself of the form of Agni.

उदस्तंभीत्समिधा नाकमृष्टोग्निर्भवन्नुत्तमो रोचमानां । यदी भृगुभ्यः परि मातरिश्वा सुहा संतं हव्यवाहं समीधे ॥ (*udastaṃbhītsamidhā nākamṛṣṭognirbhavannuttamo rocamānāṃ | yadī bhṛgubhyaḥ pari mātariśvā suhā saṃtaṃ havyavāhaṃ samīdhe ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-5-10)

Here the account of Mātariśvan's bringing Agni to the Bhṛgus is evident. But who this Mātariśvan is:

मातरि अंतरिक्षे श्वसिति प्राणिति सूर्यरूपेण चेष्टत इति मातरिश्वा अथवा मातरि अरण्यां श्वसिति निवसतीति मातरिश्वा (*mātari aṃtarikṣe śvasiti prāṇiti sūryarūpeṇa ceṣṭata iti mātariśvā athavā mātari araṇyāṃ śvasiti nivasatīti mātariśvā*) Sāyaṇa explains that Agni has this name either because he moves in the atmosphere in the form of the sun, or because he dwells in the mother-like araṇis (fire-sticks).

### Printed p. 115

(Ṛ. Saṃ. Bhā. 3-5-9) **NOTE** This explanation supports the etymology that the author of the Nirukta gave for this word (Ni. 7-26).

2) In the same way Bṛhaspati also is well known as the one who first discovered Agni and brought him to the earth.

सोषामविंदत्स स्वंःसो अग्निं सो अर्कॆण वि बबाधे तमांसि । बृहस्पतिर्गो वपुषो वलस्य निर्मज्ञानं न पर्वणो जभार ॥ (*soṣāmaviṃdatsa svaṃḥso agniṃ so arkèṇa vi babādhe tamāṃsi | bṛhaspatirgo vapuṣo valasya nirmajñānaṃ na parvaṇo jabhāra ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-68-9)

When Bṛhaspati strove to find the cows, he obtained Uṣas. He obtained Agni and Āditya and destroyed the darkness. In this way the verse praises Bṛhaspati's effort and his success. The same Bṛhaspati is shown to be identical with both Agni and Mātariśvan. For example:

अच्छा वदा तना गिरा जरायै ब्रह्मणस्पतिं । अग्निं मित्रं न दर्शतं ॥ (*acchā vadā tanā girā jarāyai brahmaṇaspatiṃ | agniṃ mitraṃ na darśataṃ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-38-13)

तमृत्विया उप वाचः सचंते सर्गो न यो देवयतामसर्जि । बृहस्पतिः स ह्यंजो वरांसि विभ्वाभवत्समृते मातरिश्वा ॥ (*tamṛtviyā upa vācaḥ sacaṃte sargo na yo devayatāmasarji | bṛhaspatiḥ sa hyaṃjo varāṃsi vibhvābhavatsamṛte mātariśvā ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-190-2)

In these and similar passages identity is shown in some places with Agni and in others with Mātariśvan. Where ब्रह्मणस्पतिः अग्निः (*brahmaṇaspatiḥ agniḥ*) is said, the meaning is "lord of the mantras". Where बृहस्पतिः मातरिश्वा (*bṛhaspatiḥ mātariśvā*) is said, the meaning is "one who abides in the sacrifice, the maker of its fruit". In many other places this identity can be recognised. (2-1-3; 3-26-2, etc.)

3) Aṅgiras: the matter of Aṅgiras, the relation of the sage Aṅgiras to Agni, and the statement that Agni is the first of the Aṅgirases have been explained earlier. Moreover:

तव श्रिये व्यजिहीत पर्वतो गवां गोत्रमुदसृजो यदंगिरः । इंद्रेण युजा तमसा परीवृतं बृहस्पते निरपामौब्बो अर्णवं ॥ (*tava śriye vyajihīta parvato gavāṃ gotramudasṛjo yadaṃgiraḥ | iṃdreṇa yujā tamasā parīvṛtaṃ bṛhaspate nirapāmaubbo arṇavaṃ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-23-18)

In Ṛks like this Bṛhaspati too is addressed as Aṅgiras. That is, there are also many instances in which one word in the group Bṛhaspati, Mātariśvan, Aṅgiras, Agni and so on serves as an epithet of another. It can be said with certainty that all of them indicate an aspect of Agni.

### Printed p. 116

8) The Agni who was said earlier to have been produced by the sage called Atharvan, in such statements as त्वामग्ने पुष्करादथर्वा निरमंथत (*tvāmagne puṣkarādatharvā niramaṃthata*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-17-13), is likewise himself denoted by "Atharvan".

In statements such as अग्निर्जातो अथर्वणा विदद्विश्वासि काव्या (*agnirjāto atharvaṇā vidadviśvāsi kāvyā*) (10-21-5) too it is shown that Atharvan is the producer and that Agni is knowledge from him. **NOTE** But:

आ नूनमश्विनोर्यषिः स्तोमं चिकेत नामया । आ सोमं मधुमत्तमं घर्मं सिंचादधर्वण ॥ (*ā nūnamaśvinoryaṣiḥ stomaṃ ciketa nāmayā | ā somaṃ madhumattamaṃ gharmaṃ siṃcādadharvaṇa ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-9-7)

Here अथर्वणि (*atharvaṇi*) is a word denoting Agni; it has been explained as "the non-violent Agni". Or else the meaning "one fashioned by the sage Atharvan" is also possible. There are many more statements that show the identity of Atharvan and Agni; they are only pointed out here, and their explanation is not relevant in this place.

In the present context the main point is that the Bhṛgus found Agni, who was hidden within houses, worshipped him themselves and also helped the people. This matter was explained earlier in Part 11.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इ॒मं त्रि॒तो भूर्य॑विंददि॒च्छन्वै॑भूव॒सो मू॒र्धन्यघ्न्या॑याः ।  
> स शेवृ॑धो जा॒त आ ह॒र्म्येषु॒ नाभि॒र्युवा॑ भवति रोच॒नस्य॑ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इमं । त्रितः । भूरि । अविंदत् । इच्छन् । वैभुवसः । मूर्धनि । अघ्न्यायाः ।  
> सः । शेवृधः । जातः । आ । हर्म्येषु । नाभिः । युवा । भवति । रोचनस्य ॥३॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इमं भूरि महांतमग्निं वैभूवसो विभूवसः पुत्रस्त्रित ऋषिरिच्छल्लब्धुमिच्छन्नघ्न्यायाः । अघ्न्या भूमिः । तस्या भूम्या मूर्धनि । भूम्यामित्यर्थः ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The seer Trita, son of Vibhūvasu, desiring to obtain this great Agni, found him अघ्न्यायाः मूर्धनि (*aghnyāyāḥ mūrdhani*). Sāyaṇa explains that अघ्न्या (*aghnyā*) is the earth, so that "on the head of the Aghnyā" means "on the earth".

### Printed p. 117

> तत्राविंदत् । लब्धवान् । सोग्निः शेवृधः सुखस्य वर्धयिता सन् हर्म्येषु
> यजमानगृहेष्वा सर्वतो जातः प्रादुर्भूतः सन्नोचनस्य रोचमानस्य
> स्वर्गाख्यस्य फलस्कोक्तलक्षणस्य यज्ञस्यादित्यस्य ना नाभिर्बंधको भवति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

There he found him. That Agni, increasing happiness, was born, that is, appeared, in the houses of the sacrificer on all sides; and he becomes the bond (नाभिः (*nābhiḥ*)), the binder, of the shining sacrifice that is the Āditya, called heaven, whose character is the fruit stated earlier.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इमं (*imaṃ*)—the well-known
- भूरि (*bhūri*)—Agni, of great form
- वैभुवसः (*vaibhuvasaḥ*)—the son of Vibhūvasu
- त्रितः (*tritaḥ*)—the sage named Trita
- इच्छन् (*icchan*)—wishing to obtain
- अघ्न्यायाः मूर्धनि (*aghnyāyāḥ mūrdhani*)—on the earth
- अविंदत् (*aviṃdat*)—obtained
- सः (*saḥ*)—that Agni
- शेवृधः (*śevṛdhaḥ*)—as the giver of happiness
- हर्म्येषु (*harmyeṣu*)—in the houses of the sacrificer or in the sacrificial halls
- आ जातः (*ā jātaḥ*)—having appeared
- रोचनस्य (*rocanasya*)—of the shining heaven
- नाभिः (*nābhiḥ*)—as the one who brings one to it
- भवति (*bhavati*)—becomes

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Trita, the sage son of Vibhūvasu, wished to obtain this great Agni and accordingly acquired him on the earth. That Agni, who increases happiness, appears in the sacrificial halls of the sacrificer and provides heaven for those who perform sacrifice.

##### English translation
Trita, the son of Vibhuvas, searching (for him), found mighty Agni on the surface of the earth : he the augmenter of happiness manifested in the dwellings (of the pious), the youthful (connecting) bond of the resplendent (Sun).

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> मं॒द्रं होता॑रमु॒शिजो॒ नमो॑भिः॒ प्रांचं॑ य॒ज्ञं  
> ने॒तार॑मध्व॒राणां॑ ।  
> वि॒शाम॑कृण्वन्नर॒तिं पा॑व॒कं ह॑व्य॒वाहं॒ दध॑तो॒ मानु॑षेषु ॥४॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> मंद्रं । होतारं । उशिजः । नमःभिः । प्रांचं । यज्ञं । नेतारं । अध्वराणां ।  
> विशां । अकृण्वन् । अरतिं । पावकं । हव्यवाहं । दधतः । मानुषेषु ॥ ४ ॥

### Printed p. 118

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> उशिजः कामयमाना ऋत्विजो मंद्रं मादनीयं होतारं होमनिष्पादकं प्रांचं गार्हपत्यादाहवनीयं प्रति प्रागंचंतं यज्ञं यजनीयमध्वराणां यागानां नेतारं प्रापयितारमरतिं गंतारं सर्वदा यागगृहे वर्तमानं
> पावकं शोधकं हव्यवाहं हविषो वोढारं मानुषेषु मनुष्येषु मध्यॆ
> दधतो दधाना ऋत्विजो विशां यजमानानामर्थायि। यद्वा। विशां प्रजानामरतिमर्यं । स्वामिनमित्यर्थः । नमोभिर्नमस्कारैः । स्तुतिभिरित्यर्थः । अकृण्वन् ।
> अकुर्वन् । सुप्रीतं कृतवंतः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The उशिजः (*uśijaḥ*) are the priests who desire the gods. They made Agni well pleased with their adorations (नमोभिः (*namobhiḥ*)), that is, with praises. Agni is मंद्रं (*maṃdraṃ*), the delighter; होतारं (*hotāraṃ*), the accomplisher of the offering; प्रांचं (*prāṃcaṃ*), he who goes forward from the Gārhapatya towards the Āhavanīya; यज्ञं (*yajñaṃ*), the one to be worshipped; the leader of the sacrifices (अध्वराणां (*adhvarāṇāṃ*)), who brings them to completion; अरतिं (*aratiṃ*), the goer, always present in the sacrificial house; पावकं (*pāvakaṃ*), the purifier; हव्यवाहं (*havyavāhaṃ*), the bearer of the oblation. The priests establish (दधतः (*dadhataḥ*)) him among men, for the sake of the sacrificers (विशां (*viśāṃ*)). Or else *viśāṃ aratiṃ* is "the lord of the people", the master.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- उशिजः (*uśijaḥ*)—the priests who desire the gods
- मंद्रं (*maṃdraṃ*)—the giver of joy
- होतारं (*hotāraṃ*)—the accomplisher of the offering
- प्रांचं (*prāṃcaṃ*)—he who is carried from the fire-pit called Gārhapatya to the fire-pit called Āhavanīya
- यज्ञं (*yajñaṃ*)—the worthy of worship
- अध्वराणां (*adhvarāṇāṃ*)—of the sacrifices
- नेतारं (*netāraṃ*)—the leader, or the chief deity in the sacrifices
- अरतिं (*aratiṃ*)—he who is always in the sacrificial house
- पावकं (*pāvakaṃ*)—the purifier
- हव्यवाहं (*havyavāhaṃ*)—he who carries the oblation to the gods
- मानुषेषु (*mānuṣeṣu*)—among men
- विशां (अरतिं) (*viśāṃ (aratiṃ)*)—Agni, the lord of all the people
- दधतः (*dadhataḥ*)—the priests who perform the rite
- नमोभिः (*namobhiḥ*)—with adorations or with praises
- अकृण्वन् (*akṛṇvan*)—made well pleased

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The priests, who desire the gods and perform the rites in men's sacrifices, please with adorations and praises Agni, the giver of happiness, the accomplisher of the offering, the worthy of worship, the leader of the sacrificial rites, he who always dwells in the sacrificial houses, the carrier of the oblation to the gods, the lord of the people.

##### English translation
(The priests) desirous (to propitiate him), detaining him amongst men have by their adoration made him lord over all people, him the exhilarator, the presenter of burnt offerings, the migrator, the object of sacrifice, the leader of rites, the purifier, the bearer of oblations.

### Printed p. 119

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र भू॒र्जयं॑तं म॒हां वि॑पो॒धां मू॒रा अमू॑रं पु॒रां  ॒॑
> दर्माणं ।  
> नयं॑तो॒ गर्भं॑ व॒नां धियं॑ धु॒र्हिरि॑श्मश्रुं॒ नार्वा॑णं॒ धन॑र्चं ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । भूः । जयंतं । महां । विपःधां । मूराः । अमूरं । पुरां । दर्माणं  
> नयंतः । गर्भं । वनां । धियं । धुः । हिरिश्मश्रुं । न । अर्वाणं । धनअर्चं ।

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे स्तोतः त्वं जयंतं जेतव्याइत्रूञयंतं महां महांतं
> विपोधां मेधाविनो धर्तारमग्निं प्र भूः । प्रभव । समर्थो भव । स्तोतुमिति
> शेषः । उद्दीथस्तु भूर्जयंतमित्येकपदं मत्वा भूरादील्लोकाञयंतमिति
> व्याचकार । न केवलं त्वमेक एव आपि तु मूरा मूढाः सर्वॆऽपि पुरुषा अमूरममूढं पुरां दर्माणं दारकं ॥ औणादिको मनिः । प्रत्ययस्वरः ॥ गर्भमरण्योर्गर्भभूतं वनां वननीयं ॥ वा छंदसि । पा-६-१-१०६,१०७ । इति वचनादमि
> पूर्वाभावः ॥ हिरिश्मश्रुं नार्वाणं । नेत्युपमार्थीयः । श्मश्रुशब्दोऽत्र रोमसामान्यवचनः । हरितरोमोपेतमर्वाणमरणशीलमश्वमिव । उक्तलक्षणाश्ववद्धरितकेशस्थानीयज्वालोपेतं धनर्चं प्रीणनस्तुतिं ॥ शकंध्वादित्वात्पररूपत्वं ॥ ईदृशमग्निं नयंतो हविर्भिः साप्रयंतो धियं कर्म स्तोत्रं ना धुः । प्रापयंति ॥
> गातिस्थेति सिचो लुक् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O praiser, be able (प्र भूः (*pra bhūḥ*)) to praise Agni", who is victorious, conquering the enemies to be conquered, great, and the sustainer of the wise. Uddītha, taking भूर्जयंतं (*bhūrjayaṃtaṃ*) as one word, explained it as "conquering the worlds, Bhūr and the rest". Not you alone: even all foolish men (मूराः (*mūrāḥ*)) praise him who is not foolish, the one who splits cities (पुरां दर्माणं (*purāṃ darmāṇaṃ*)). He is the embryo of the two fire-sticks, to be praised (वनां (*vanāṃ*)). हिरिश्मश्रुं नार्वाणं (*hiriśmaśruṃ nārvāṇaṃ*): न (*na*) is used for comparison; श्मश्रु (*śmaśru*) here means hair in general. He is like a swift horse with green-golden hair, that is, he has flames resembling green hair, and is a lover of the pleasing praise (धनर्चं (*dhanarcaṃ*)). Leading such an Agni with oblations, the priests bring about (धुः (*dhuḥ*)) the rite or the hymn (धियं (*dhiyaṃ*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O praiser, you) जयंतं (*jayaṃtaṃ*)—him who conquers enemies
- महां (*mahāṃ*)—the great one
- विपोधां (*vipodhāṃ*)—Agni, the sustainer of the wise priests
- प्रभूः (*prabhūḥ*)—be able to praise
- मूराः (*mūrāḥ*)—even foolish men
- अमूरं (*amūraṃ*)—the discerning one
- पुरां (*purāṃ*)—the enemy cities
- दर्माणं (*darmāṇaṃ*)—the splitter
- गर्भं (*garbhaṃ*)—he who is in the fire-sticks in the form of an embryo
- वनां (*vanāṃ*)—praised by all
- हिरिश्मश्रुं (*hiriśmaśruṃ*)—having hair gleaming like gold

### Printed p. 120

- अर्वाणं न (*arvāṇaṃ na*)—like a horse, accompanied by flames of golden hue
- धनर्चं (*dhanarcaṃ*)—Agni, the lover of praise, they praise
- नयंतः (*nayaṃtaḥ*)—the priests who serve with oblations and the like
- धियं (*dhiyaṃ*)—for the performance of the sacrificial rite, or for hearing the hymns
- धुः (*dhuḥ*)—bring to the place of sacrifice

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O praiser, be able to praise Agni, the great one, who sustains the wise priests. Even foolish men praise Agni, the discerning, the splitter of enemy cities, who is in the fire-sticks in the form of an embryo, praised by all, a horse-like one with a mass of hair gleaming like gold, accompanied by golden flames, a lover of praise. The priests who carry and offer oblations and the like bring Agni to the sacrificial altar for the performance of the sacrificial rite.

##### English translation
Be competent, (priest, to glorify) the victorious mighty Agni; the sustainer of the wise, (even) fools (adore) him, the exempt from folly, the demolisher of cities ; conducting the embryo of touchwood, (Agni), like a golden-haired, swift-moving (horse), loving praise and wealth, they bring him to the sacrifice.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> नि प॒स्त्या॑सु त्रि॒तः स्त॑भू॒यन्परि॑वीतो॒ योन्॑ सीददं॒तः ।  
> अतः॑ सं॒गृभ्या॑ वि॒शां दमू॑ना॒ विध॑र्मणायं॒त्रैरी॑यते॒न्यन् ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> नि । पस्त्यासु । त्रितः । स्तभुयन् । परिवीतः । योन् । सीदत् । अंतरिति ।  
> अतः । संगृभ्य । विशां । दमूनाः । विधर्मणा । अयंत्रैः । ईयते । न्यन् ॥६॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> त्रितस्त्रिषु स्थानेषु गार्हपत्यादिषु तायमानः स्तभूयन् यजमानगृहान्
> स्तंभयितुमिच्छन् ॥ इच्छार्थे क्यचि तदंताच्छतरि रूपं ॥ परिवीतः परितो ज्वालाभि-

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Trita, extended (तायमानः (*tāyamānaḥ*)) in the three places, the Gārhapatya and the others, is स्तभूयन् (*stabhūyan*), wishing to make the houses of the sacrificer firm; परिवीतः (*parivītaḥ*) is "surrounded on all sides by flames".

### Printed p. 121

> व्याप्तः सन् पस्त्यासु यागगृहेषु योनावंतः स्वीये वेद्यात्मके स्थाने नि
> षीदत् । निषीदति । अतोर्स्मात्स्थानाद्विशां प्रजानां संबंधीनि हवींषि संगृभ्य
> संग्रहेंद्रादीनुद्दिश्य व्रत्तानि चरुपुरोडाशादीनि स्वीकृत्य दमूना देवेभ्यो
> दानमना यजमानाय वाभिमतप्रदानमना विधर्मणा तासां विविधेन कर्मणा
> निमित्तेन दानमना देवेभ्यो दानपक्षे विविधगमनरूपेण कर्मणायंत्रैः
> शत्रूणां नियमनैः सह नृंदेवानीयते । गच्छति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa completes the gloss begun on the previous page. Agni, surrounded all round by flames, sits down in the sacrificial houses, in his own place, the altar (योनावंतः (*yonāvaṃtaḥ*) ... निषीदत् (*niṣīdat*)). From this place he takes up the oblations of the people (विशां (*viśāṃ*)): the cooked offerings, cakes and the like, offered to Indra and the other gods. He is दमूनाः (*damūnāḥ*): either intent on giving to the gods, or intent on granting the sacrificer's wishes. By विधर्मणा (*vidharmaṇā*) he does this through varied acts; or, on the view that he gives to the gods, through varied movement. With the *ayaṃtraiḥ*, the restraints of foes, he goes (ईयते (*īyate*)) to the gods (नृन् (*nṛn*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- त्रितः (*tritaḥ*)—Agni, established in the three fire-pits Gārhapatya, Āhavanīya and Dakṣiṇāgni
- स्तभूयन् (*stabhūyan*)—firmly establishing the houses of the sacrificers
- परिवीतः (*parivītaḥ*)—pervaded by flames all round
- पस्त्यासु (*pastyāsu*)—in the sacrificial houses
- योन् (*yon*)—on the sacrificial altar in the middle
- नि षीदत् (*ni ṣīdat*)—sits down
- अतः (*ataḥ*)—sitting in this his own place
- विशां (*viśāṃ*)—the oblations and the like offered by the people
- संगृभ्य (*saṃgṛbhya*)—having accepted; that is, having accepted the cooked offerings, cakes and the like offered in him with reference to Indra and the other gods, in order to convey them to those gods
- दमूनाः (*damūnāḥ*)—desirous of giving those oblations to the gods, or desirous of giving the sacrificer his desired ends
- विधर्मणा (*vidharmaṇā*)—by various acts or by various paths
- यंत्रैः (*yaṃtraiḥ*)—holding the enemies in check
- नृन् (*nṛn*)—to the gods
- ईयते (*īyate*)—goes

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Agni, established in the three fire-pits Gārhapatya, Āhavanīya and Dakṣiṇāgni, makes the houses of the sacrificers firm, is pervaded by the flames spread all round, and sits on the sacrificial altar in the middle of the sacrificial house. While seated on this altar he accepts the oblations that the people offer in Agni for Indra and the other gods. Desiring to carry them to the gods, and holding his enemies in check, he goes to the gods by various acts or paths.

##### English translation
Occupying three (stations), consolidating (the dwelling of the worshipper), surrounded (by flames), he sits down upon the altar in the chambers (of sacrifice); from thence, having accepted (the oblations) of the people, with intent to give (them to the gods), in various ways, he (Agni) goes to the gods holding (his foes) in check.

### Printed p. 122

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The Ṛk in hand praises Agni, described as Trita. For the word Trita Sāyaṇa gives the sense त्रिषु स्थानेषु गार्हपत्यादिषु तायमानः (*triṣu sthāneṣu gārhapatyādiṣu tāyamānaḥ*): the Agni who, being in the three places in three forms (Dakṣiṇāgni, Gārhapatya, Āhavanīya), protects the sacrifice. The seer named Trita has been dealt with earlier (Ṛgveda Saṃhitā vol. 8, p. 443-7). Here we examine which deity this epithet, used of a deity, refers to.

It is not easy to state a precise meaning for the word Trita used of a deity. Scholars who give etymologies following the Vedic tradition, and many English scholars as well, have discussed it, each giving a different meaning. If what Trita is can be learnt as far as possible from the Ṛksaṃhitā and other sources, the opinion of Yāska and the other Vedic commentators must be considered first.

The Nirukta-kāra gives an explanation of this word in two places. First there is an explanation concerning the seer:
त्रितं कूपेवहितमेतत्तूक्तं प्रतिबभौ । तत्र ब्रह्मतिहासमिश्रमृज्मिश्रं गाथामिश्रं भवति ॥ (*tritaṃ kūpevahitametattūktaṃ pratibabhau | tatra brahmatihāsamiśramṛjmiśraṃ gāthāmiśraṃ bhavati ‖*)
(Ni. 4-6)
An explanation concerning the seer, beginning thus, has been given earlier.

Likewise, in explaining Ṛk 1-187-1, Sāyaṇa takes क्षित्यादिस्थानेषु तायमानः इंद्रः (*kṣityādisthāneṣu tāyamānaḥ iṃdraḥ*) to mean Indra, the protector in the earth, the atmosphere and the heaven. The Nirukta-kāra, explaining the same Ṛk, says त्रिस्थान इंद्रः (*tristhāna iṃdraḥ*). He does not say what "three-placed" means; probably, as Sāyaṇa says, it is the three worlds beginning with the earth. Moreover, this epithet also belongs to Vāyu and Soma. We must therefore examine whether the word Trita has a meaning according to each context, or is an epithet denoting a single deity only.

The name Trita is used in the Ṛksaṃhitā in forty places in all (the name does not occur in Maṇḍalas 3, 4 and 7). No sūkta is addressed to a deity named Trita, nor is there any context praising this deity alone; nor does the name occur among the deities classified by Yāska. Although they do not fully know the Vedic tradition, the opinions of some English scholars who have laboured and made inquiries from many points of view in this matter are given first.

### Printed p. 123
After that we examine whether those opinions are right or not.

"Indra and Trita both denote the same deity; Trita is an epithet specific to Indra alone," is the opinion of Kuhn. Roth has said it is "an epithet describing the nature of Vāyu."

"A deity who destroys the evil power and sends down rain. Soma is the strength-giving juice of this deity. Hence the power of the two deities Indra and Vāyu is manifest," Benfey has conjectured. There are many more such opinions.

The explanation and etymology that Yāska shows for this word are given above. In about twenty contexts Sāyaṇa has taken this word to mean Trita. In those places, stating the explanation त्रिषु स्थानेषु तायमानः (*triṣu sthāneṣu tāyamānaḥ*), he has taken it, according to each context, to refer to all of these deities: Indra, Vāyu, Varuṇa and Agni.

Because of this variety of purport, one single precise meaning cannot be given for this word. Therefore, noting the contexts in which the word is used and examining what each context is, it is explained as best one can.

Of the forty uses in all:
1. (1) Sixteen times it is used together with Indra.
(2) Once only, individually, he is praised as the slayer of Vṛtra.
(3) Once identity with the Vāruṇa horse is stated.
(4) Four times association with the Maruts is stated.
(5) Seven times a distinction of Agni is praised.
(6) Once identity with Varuṇa is stated.
(7) Description of Soma's qualities in four places.
(8) Six times he is described as a deity dwelling in a hidden region.

1) In Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-2-24 Indra and Trita are described as together facing Vṛtra, and the Maruts are said to give Trita strength. Sāyaṇa explains Trita here as a royal seer, Trita Āptya. (The usage Trita Āptya will be explained later.)
2) In 1-52-4, 5 too a battle with the Asura Vala is described. Indra and Trita both face Vala and help the fall of rain. Sāyaṇa's explanation is that he is one of the three, Ekata, Dvita and Trita.

### Printed p. 124
3) 5-86-1 is a mantra praising Indra and Agni together. Sāyaṇa gives alternative explanations: "Trita, a seer; or Agni, existing in the three places": a seer, or Agni pervading all three places.
4) 5-8-7: the context of Trita preparing to fight Vṛtra. Sāyaṇa takes him as a seer; some others as Vāyu.
5) 5-8-8 and 9: the context in which Trita, impelled by Indra and receiving his help, faces and splits Viśvarūpa, son of Tvaṣṭṛ. He is also described as the maker of rain.
6) 10-99-6 त्रितोन्नोजसा वृधानो विषा वराहमयोअग्रया हन् (*tritonnojasā vṛdhāno viṣā varāhamayoagrayā han*): with an iron-tipped thunderbolt he destroyed the demon Varāha. Indra and Trita are identical.
7) 10-48-2 त्रिताय गा अजनयमहेरधि (*tritāya gā ajanayamaheradhi*): "For Trita I brought forth the cow from the serpent"; Indra himself declares the help he gave Trita. Sāyaṇa's explanation here is that it was help given to Trita Āptya, who had fallen into a well.
8, 9) 2-11-19, 20: as a result of Trita pressing Soma and offering it to Indra, Indra was stimulated and destroyed Vala. In this context some show identity of Trita and Soma. Here Sāyaṇa takes the word त्रितस्य (*tritasya*) as त्रिताय (*tritāya*): Indra hurled his discus for Trita; hence he holds that Trita here means the seer so named.
10) 8-12-16, 17: Sāyaṇa's view is that Trita Āptya is a royal seer.
11) 8-52-1: the same.
12-16) 9-32-2; 9-38-2; 9-34-4; 9-86-20; 1-163-2. Although these five contexts show the same meaning, the last Ṛk has a special sense. Sāyaṇa takes the first four places as a seer and shows the meaning Vāyu in the last Ṛk. For the sentence यमेन दत्तं त्रितः (*yamena dattaṃ tritaḥ*) the explanation is पृथिव्यादिषु त्रिषु स्थानेषु वर्तमानः तीर्णितनो वा वायुः (*pṛthivyādiṣu triṣu sthāneṣu vartamānaḥ tīrṇitano vā vāyuḥ*).

II. Unity with the Vāruṇa horse
17) असि यमोअस्यादित्यो अर्वन्नसि त्रितः (*asi yamoasyādityo arvannasi tritaḥ*) (1-163-2): here it is explained that the Vāruṇa horse has a share of three deities, Yama, Āditya and Trita. What the word Trita means

### Printed p. 125
Sāyaṇa has not stated precisely. He takes Yama to mean Agni. He holds that Trita may be Vāyu moving in the three regions, or else a seer.

III. Trita as slayer of Vṛtra
18) 1-187-1: it was stated above that Sāyaṇa conjectures the meaning of the word Trita here to be Indra.

IV. Contexts showing the association of Trita with the Maruts
19) 2-34-10: the purport of this Ṛk is that the Maruts, sons of Rudra, freed Trita from bondage. Many have tried to explain what the bondage was and who Trita is; no one has understood properly. In this context the scholar Max Müller, after great effort to state a precise meaning for the word, finally said "Its true meaning remains as unfathomable as ever": its meaning remained in the end obscure.
20-21) 2-34-14; 5-54-2. In these two contexts too the association with the Maruts is shown, as in the Ṛk cited above. The purport is probably a seer; but in explaining Ṛk 5-54-2 Sāyaṇa holds that त्रिषु स्थानेषु तायमानो मेघो मरुद्दणो वा (*triṣu sthāneṣu tāyamāno megho maruddaṇo vā*): a cloud, or the troop of Maruts, protecting by pervading the three places.
22) 10-115-4: the purport of the Ṛk is that the Maruts, eager for battle, discovered Agni, who lay hidden among the clouds in the form of Trita. Sāyaṇa too takes the word Trita here to mean Agni.

V. Identity or association of Trita with Agni
23) 5-9-5: in this Ṛk, in which Trita, dwelling in the atmosphere, gives impulse to the spread of Agni's flame extended in the atmosphere, some take the word Trita as Vāyu and some as Agni. By Sāyaṇa's method the meaning is Agni.
24) 5-41-4: a praise of Trita, who has come to our sacrifice together with Vāta, Agni and Pūṣan. Some hold that Trita and Agni are different; some hold that the word Trita is here a synonym of Agni. Sāyaṇa alone holds that it is an epithet of the word Vāta.

### Printed p. 126
25) 5-41-9, 10: the epithets Āptya and अपां नपातं (*apāṃ napātaṃ*) describe Agni in the form of Trita. Sāyaṇa too takes it as Agni.
26) 10-46-3: since the splendour of Trita is described here, some hold it to be Agni. Sāyaṇa takes it to mean a seer.
27) 10-46-6: this is the Ṛk in hand; its explanation is given above. Here it means Agni as lord of the house.
28) 10-64-3: both of these are addressed to the lightning fire.
29) 2-31-6: the same.

VI. Indication of identity of Trita with Varuṇa
30) 8-41-6: it may be Agni, the navel of the deities Mitra, Varuṇa, Aryaman and the rest; or, as Sāyaṇa says, it may be the deity Varuṇa. Here there is also a good opening to say it is the lightning fire.

VII. Identity or association of Trita with Soma
31-34) 9-37-4; 9-95-4; 9-102-2; 9-102-3: in all four contexts the close relation of Soma and Trita is stated. In some places it is stated that Trita pressed Soma and offered it to Indra. In others it is stated that Soma himself is in the form of Trita.

VIII. Trita hidden in the hidden atmosphere
35) 1-105-9: it is explained that Trita Āptya knows also the seven rays of Āditya (Agni). Earlier, in explaining the same Ṛk, it was stated that Trita Āptya is a seer. There is also scope for taking him as the lightning fire within the cloud. Likewise,
36-40) 1-105-17; 8-47-13; 8-47-14; 8-47-15; 8-47-16: in all these contexts there is scope for taking him as the lightning fire within the cloud.

In the sentences cited above the nature of Trita is described in many ways. The question whether he is of Indra's form, of Vāyu's form or of Agni's form arises naturally. But if all the above contexts are examined closely, only one element of Trita is found equally in all contexts.

The precise meaning that Trita is none other than the lightning fire finds support in all the above sentences.

### Printed p. 127
The battle of Indra with Vṛtra and the others has the purpose of loosening the cloud that was held bound and making rain fall. In this connection the help of Trita (the lightning-fire) must be present. For this very reason the Vāruṇa horse also bears the name Trita. It was stated earlier that this Vāruṇa horse is the divine light, a portion of the Sun. Since this lightning-fire is itself a portion of Agni, the identity of Agni and Trita comes about naturally. It was also explained earlier that Agni, who has three seats, is the one named Trita (1-45-1, 2). The same meaning is to be understood when identity with Soma, Varuṇa and the others is spoken of.
