> कृ॑णोति ।  
> स ति॒ग्मशृं॑गं वृष॒भं युयु॑त्सन् द्रु॒हस्त॑स्थौ बहु॒ले ब॒द्धो  ॒
> अंतः ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र। नेमस्मिन् । ददृशे । सोमः । अंतः । गोपाः । नेमं । आविः। अस्था । कृणोति ॥  
> सः । तिग्मशृंगं । वृषभं । युयुत्सन् । द्रुहः । तस्थौ । बहुले । बद्धः । अंतरिति ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> नेमस्मिन् । नेमशब्दोऽर्धपर्याय । यष्टायष्टा च द्वौ पुरुषौ
> युध्यंतौ । तयोरेकस्मिन्यष्टरीत्यर्थः । तस्मिन्नंतः सोमो ददृशे । दृश्यते । तं

### Printed p. 159

> नेममर्धमेकं गोपा गोपायितेंद्रोऽस्था क्षेपणसाधनेन वज्रेणाविष्कृ
> णोति । प्रकटयति । शत्रुभिरनभिभूतं करोतीत्यर्थः । स यत्र सोमो न दृश्यते
> स तिग्मशृंगं तीक्ष्णायुधं वृषभं बाणान्वर्षयंतमिंद्रॆणानुगृहीतं प्रति युयुत्सन् योद्धुमिच्छन् द्रुहो द्रोग्धा बद्धः सन् बहुले०धकारेऽंतस्तस्य मध्यॆ
> तस्मै । तिष्ठति । यद्वा । एवं व्याख्येयं । नेमस्मिन्निंद्रभक्तेऽनः सोमो दवृशे ।
> नेममन्यं गोपाः स्वभक्तरक्षक इंद्रोऽस्था वज्रेणाविष्कृणोति । आविष्करोति ।
> वज्रेण पीडयतीत्यर्थः । स नेमो द्रुहस्तिग्मशृंगं वृषभमिंद्रं युयुत्सन् ।
> शिष्टं समानं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The word नेम (*nema*) is a synonym of "half". Two men fight, a sacrificer and a non-sacrificer; नेमस्मिन् (*nemasmin*) means in the sacrificer, one of the two. In him Soma is seen (ददृशे (*dadṛśe*)). That one of the two, the protector Indra, makes manifest with the vajra, the instrument of casting; the meaning is that he makes him unovercome by enemies. The other, in whom Soma is not seen, desires to fight against the sharp-horned (तिग्मशृंगं (*tigmaśṛṃgaṃ*), with sharp weapon) bull (वृषभं (*vṛṣabhaṃ*), one who showers arrows), who is favoured by Indra; being a traitor (द्रुहः (*druhaḥ*)) and bound, he remains in the thick darkness, in the midst of it. Or else it is to be explained thus: Soma is seen in the one who is a devotee of Indra; the other, not his devotee, Indra, protector of his own devotees, makes manifest with the vajra, that is, torments him with the vajra. That *nemaḥ*, a traitor, wishing to fight against Indra, the sharp-horned bull. The rest is the same.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- नेमस्मिन् (*nemasmin*)—among the two, the one who sacrifices and the one who does not, in the man who sacrifices
- अंतः (*aṃtaḥ*)—in the midst
- सोमः (*somaḥ*)—the Soma juice
- ददृशे (*dadṛśe*)—is seen
- तं नेमं (*taṃ nemaṃ*)—that man, one of the two
- गोपाः (*gopāḥ*)—Indra, the protector
- अस्था (*asthā*)—with the vajra weapon, the instrument of slaying
- आविः कृणोति (*āviḥ kṛṇoti*)—manifests; that is, makes him not defeated by enemies
- सः (*saḥ*)—the other man who does not sacrifice, that is, who does not obtain Soma juice
- तिग्मशृंगं (*tigmaśṛṃgaṃ*)—having a sharp weapon
- वृषभं (*vṛṣabhaṃ*)—with his opponent the sacrificer, who showers a rain of arrows in battle
- युयुत्सन् (*yuyutsan*)—when he desires to fight
- द्रुहः (*druhaḥ*)—he, the traitor
- बद्धः (*baddhaḥ*)—being bound with fetters
- बहुले अंतः (*bahule aṃtaḥ*)—in the midst of darkness
- तस्थौ (*tasthau*)—remains

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Of the two, the sacrificer and the non-sacrificer, in the man in whom Soma juice is seen, that is, the man who sacrifices, Indra helps him with his vajra weapon and makes him not defeated by enemies. But the man who does not sacrifice, when he fights with the sacrificer, who has a sharp weapon and showers a rain of arrows in battle, having no help of his own, is bound by the fetters of the enemies and remains in a prison enveloped in darkness; or, for lack of Indra's help, being helpless, he falls into the power of his enemies.

##### English translation
The Soma is seen in one of the two (combatants); the cowherd (Indra)manifests the other with his thunder-bolt: the latter warring against the sharp-horned bull, remains in great (darkness) bound by the victor.

#### Ṛk 11

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ॒दि॒त्यानां॒ वसू॑नां रु॒द्रिया॑णां दे॒वो दे॒वानां॒ न मि॑नामि॒  ॑
> धाम॑ ।

### Printed p. 160

> ते मा भद्राय शवसे ततक्षुरपराजितमस्तृतमषाळ्हं ॥ ११ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आदित्यानां । वसूनां । रुद्रियाणां । देवः। देवानां । न । मिनामि । धाम ॥  
> ते । मा । भद्राय । शवसे । ततक्षुः । अपराजितं । अस्तृतं । अषाळ्हं ॥ ११ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आदित्यानां वसूनां रुद्रियाणां रुद्राणां यद्वा रुद्रपुत्राणां मरुतां
> च देवानां धाम स्थानं देव इंद्रो न मिनाति । न हिनस्ति । त आदित्यादयो मा
> मां भद्राय शवसे ततक्षुः । अनुगृह्णंतु । अपराजितमस्तृतमहिंसिरमषाळ्ह
> मनभिभूतं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Of the Ādityas, the Vasus, the रुद्रियाणां (*rudriyāṇāṃ*)—the Rudras, or else the Maruts, sons of Rudra—of these gods the god Indra does not injure the abode (धाम (*dhāma*), place); he does not destroy it. May those Ādityas and the rest favour me (ततक्षुः (*tatakṣuḥ*) = अनुगृह्णंतु (*anugṛhṇaṃtu*)) for auspicious strength. अपराजितं (*aparājitaṃ*) is "undefeated"; अस्तृतं (*astṛtaṃ*) is "unharmed"; अषाळ्हं (*aṣāḷhaṃ*) is "not overcome".

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देवः (*devaḥ*)—Indra, the god in essence
- आदित्यानां (*ādityānāṃ*)—of the Ādityas, or of the gods who are sons of Aditi
- वसूनां (*vasūnāṃ*)—of the Vasus
- रुद्रियाणां (*rudriyāṇāṃ*)—of the Rudras, or of the Maruts, sons of Rudra
- देवानां (*devānāṃ*)—of the other gods
- धाम (*dhāma*)—the abode
- न मिनामि (*na mināmi*)—does not abandon
- ते (*te*)—the gods, the Ādityas and others
- मा (*mā*)—me
- भद्राय (*bhadrāya*)—for auspiciousness
- शवसे (*śavase*)—for strength and capacity
- ततक्षुः (*tatakṣuḥ*)—may they favour
- अपराजितं (*aparājitaṃ*)—so that I am not defeated by enemies
- अस्तृतं (*astṛtaṃ*)—so that I am not harmed by them
- अषाळ्हं (*aṣāḷhaṃ*)—so that I am not overcome; may they favour me

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May Indra, the god in essence, not abandon the abode of the Ādityas, Vasus, Rudras and the other gods. May those Ādityas and the other gods bring me auspiciousness, give me strength and capacity, and favour me so that I am not defeated by enemies, not harmed by them, and not overcome by anyone.

##### English translation
The divine (Indra) abandons not the abode of the deities, the Adityas, the Vasus, and the Rudriyas ; may they fashion me for auspicious vigour, undefeated, unharmed, unconquered.

### Printed p. 161
## Sūkta 49 (Maṇḍala 10)
**Introduction**
In this sūkta also Indra praises his own deeds of valour. Only some of them are described here.

**Description of the slaying of the Asura Vṛtra**
The Asura Vṛtra is of evil nature. His nature is known to no one. He has no good understanding at all. It is his nature always to lie down blocking the waters. It is described that Indra slew such a Vṛtra of base nature with the vajra weapon on the day of the full moon. On this matter the Bhāṣyakāra cites a passage of the Taittirīya Saṃhitā and shows that the slaying of Vṛtra by Indra on the day of the full moon is stated in it. Since all the seers have praised Indra's slaying of Vṛtra, it is necessary to state here a detailed account of that slaying. Therefore we give here the mantra portion of the Yajurveda that sets out that account, together with its meaning:

त्वष्टा हतपुत्रो वींद्रग्ं सोममाहरत्तस्मिन्निंद्र उपहवमैच्छत तं नोपाह्वयत पुत्रं मेऽवधीरिति स यज्ञवेशसं कृत्वा प्रासहा सोममपिबत्तस्य यदत्यशिष्यत तत्त्वष्टाहवनीयमुप प्रावर्तयत्स्वाहेंद्रशत्रुर्वर्धस्वेति यदवर्तयत्तद्वृत्रस्य वृत्रत्वं यदब्रवीत्स्वाहेंद्रशत्रुर्वधस्वेति तस्मादस्येंद्रः शत्रुरभवत्स संभवन्नग्नीषोमावभि समभवत्स इषुमात्र, मिषुमात्रं विष्टज्जवर्धत स इमान् लोकानवृणोद्यदिमान् लोकानवृणोत्तद्वृत्रस्य वृत्रत्वं तस्मादिंद्रोऽभिभेत् ॥ (*tvaṣṭā hataputro vīṃdragṃ somamāharattasminniṃdra upahavamaicchata taṃ nopāhvayata putraṃ me 'vadhīriti sa yajñaveśasaṃ kṛtvā prāsahā somamapibattasya yadatyaśiṣyata tattvaṣṭāhavanīyamupa prāvartayatsvāheṃdraśatrurvardhasveti yadavartayattadvṛtrasya vṛtratvaṃ yadabravītsvāheṃdraśatrurvadhasveti tasmādasyeṃdraḥ śatrurabhavatsa saṃbhavannagnīṣomāvabhi samabhavatsa iṣumātra, miṣumātraṃ viṣṭajjavardhata sa imān lokānavṛṇodyadimān lokānavṛṇottadvṛtrasya vṛtratvaṃ tasmādiṃdro 'bhibhet ‖*) (Tai. Saṃ. 2-5-2-1)

In this anuvāka the account of Indra's slaying of Vṛtra is stated. As a background to this story there is another episode. Although we have described it earlier, it is necessary to relate it briefly here as an introduction to what follows. In former times Tvaṣṭṛ Prajāpati had a son named Viśvarūpa. He was the priest of the gods. Yet since he was born of an Asura woman, he had a special partiality for the Asuras. That being so, Indra feared that this Viśvarūpa, though priest to us, being partial to the Asuras, might bide his time, help the Asuras, and plot so that the gods should have no kingdom at all. Therefore Indra slew him by another means. This very matter is here (त्वष्टा हतपुत्रो वींद्रग्ं (*tvaṣṭā hataputro vīṃdragṃ*))**NOTE**

### Printed p. 163
मीयमेकादशकपालं पूर्णमासे प्रायच्छत्तावब्रूतामभि संदष्टौ वै स्वो न शक्नुन ऐतुमिति स इंद्र आत्मनः शीतरूरानजनयत्तच्छीतरूरयोर्जन्मय एवग्ं शीतरूरयोर्जन्म वेद नैनग्ं शीतरूरौ हतस्ताभ्यामेनमभ्यन-यत्तस्माज्जंजभ्यमानादग्नीषोमौ निरक्रामतां ॥ अ (*mīyamekādaśakapālaṃ pūrṇamāse prāyacchattāvabrūtāmabhi saṃdaṣṭau vai svo na śaknuna aitumiti sa iṃdra ātmanaḥ śītarūrānajanayattacchītarūrayorjanmaya evagṃ śītarūrayorjanma veda nainagṃ śītarūrau hatastābhyāmenamabhyana-yattasmājjaṃjabhyamānādagnīṣomau nirakrāmatāṃ ‖ a*)

Thereafter, when Indra was ready to strike Vṛtra with his weapon, Agni and Soma stopped him, saying "Do not strike": "O Indra, do not strike Vṛtra now. We, the deities Agni and Soma, are dwelling in the mouth of this Vṛtra." Indra said, "O Agni and Soma, you are indeed ours. Therefore leave Vṛtra's body and come to me; afterwards I shall slay Vṛtra." Hearing this, the deities Agni and Soma said, "O Indra, if we do as you say, what favour will you do us? If you arrange for us also a share in the sacrifice, we shall do as you say." Indra agreed to this and arranged for them, in the full-moon sacrifice, an offering-share of the oblation called the eleven-potsherd **puroḍāśa**.**NOTE**
Then the deities Agni and Soma, although they had thus obtained the boon they desired, said to Indra, "O Indra, now we cannot come out of Vṛtra's mouth. We are caught between Vṛtra's teeth and held tightly. Unless he opens his mouth we cannot come out." Then Indra thought of a means for this. From his own body he created cold fever and the great heat that arises from it. This was the first time that cold fever afflicted people in the world. The man who knows this manner of origin of cold fever is not afflicted by this cold fever. Then Indra took the cold fevers he had created and made them enter the body of the Asura Vṛtra. Then Vṛtra, trembling with the cold and exhausted by the heat of the fever, opened his mouth wide in order to draw breath in weariness. At once the deities Agni and Soma came out of his mouth and came forth.

प्राणापानौ वा एनं तदजहितां प्राणो वै दक्षोऽपानः क्रतुस्तस्माज्जंजभ्यमानो ब्रूयान्मयि दक्षक्ररू इति प्राणापानावेवात्मन्धत्ते सर्वमायुरेति॥ (*prāṇāpānau vā enaṃ tadajahitāṃ prāṇo vai dakṣo 'pānaḥ kratustasmājjaṃjabhyamāno brūyānmayi dakṣakrarū iti prāṇāpānāvevātmandhatte sarvamāyureti‖*)

Thereafter the winds called prāṇa and apāna abandoned that Vṛtra; that is, they left his body. The word prāṇa has the meaning *dakṣa*, and the word apāna the meaning "kratu". Dakṣa is skill in work and activities, intelligence; kratu is bodily power for work, capacity. Both of these left that Vṛtra, who had his mouth open. Therefore, while performing a sacrificial rite, if one makes a bodily movement or yawns and opens the mouth, the mantra मयि द्रक्षकतू (*mayi drakṣakatū*)**NOTE**should be uttered. If one does so, prāṇa and apāna do not leave the sacrificer but stay firm, and long life also results.

### Printed p. 164

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> स देवता वृत्रान्निर्हूय वार्त्र्घ्नग्ं-हविः पूर्णमासे निरवपद् घ्नंति
> ना एनं पूर्णमास आमावास्यायां प्याययंति तस्माद्वार्त्र्घ्नी पूर्णमासेऽनूच्येते वृधन्वती अमावास्यायां ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Indra, having called the deities out from Vṛtra, prepared the Vārtraghna oblation on the full-moon day. The Brāhmaṇa says that on the full moon they strike him (Vṛtra), and on the new moon they make him swell. Therefore the two Vārtraghnī verses are recited on the full moon, and the Vṛdhanvatī verses on the new moon.

Afterwards Indra called Agni, Soma and the other deities out of the mouth of the demon Vṛtra, and on the full-moon day he prepared the oblation, consisting of the offering-materials and the portions of ghee, as the means of destroying Vṛtra. It is known to everyone in worldly life, too, that the enemy called darkness, which covers everything everywhere, is destroyed by the bright moonlight of the full moon. On the new-moon day, however, there is no moonlight, so the enemy, darkness (Vṛtra), spreads everywhere and grows. Therefore, for the slaying of Vṛtra, on the full-moon day, before the oblations called the Āgneya-Somīya ājyabhāgas,

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अग्निर्वृत्राणि जंघनद्दृविणस्युर्विपन्यया । समिद्धः शुक्र आहुतः ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

A Ṛk is quoted, with its reference (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-16-34).

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-16-34)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> त्वं सोमासि सत्पतिस्त्वं राजोत वृत्रहा । त्वं भद्रो असि क्रतुः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

A second Ṛk is quoted, with its reference (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-91-5).

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-91-5)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-91-5)

these Ṛks must be recited as puronuvākyā mantras. Since both Ṛks contain words indicating the slaying of Vṛtra, they are called the Vārtraghnī Ṛks. For the slaying of Vṛtra took place on the full-moon day itself. It is therefore fitting to recite on that day the Ṛks concerned with the slaying of Vṛtra and to make the offering. On the new-moon day darkness has grown; that is, Vṛtra, who pervades everything with darkness, flourishes. Therefore on that day the Ṛks containing words formed from the root वृधु-वृद्धौ (*vṛdhu-vṛddhau*), which denotes growth, are to be recited—

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अग्निः प्रश्नेन मन्मना शुंभमानस्तन्वं १ स्वां । कविर्विप्रेण नावृधे ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

A Ṛk is quoted, with its reference (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-44-12).

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-44-12)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सोम गीर्भिष्ट्वा वयं वर्धयामो वचोविदः । सुमृळीको न आ विश ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

A Ṛk is quoted, with its reference (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-91-11).

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-91-11)

These Ṛks, which contain words denoting growth, are called Vṛdhanvatī Ṛks. They are to be recited on the new-moon day, when darkness has grown.

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> तत्सग्स् स्थाप्य वार्त्रघ्नग्ं हविर्वज्रमादाय पुनरभ्यायत ते अब्रूतां
> द्यावापृथिनी मा प्रहारावयोर्वै श्रित इति ते अब्रूतां वरं वृणावहै
> नक्षत्रनिहिताहमसानीत्य सादब्रवीच्चित्रविहिताहमितीयं तस्मान्नक्षत्रनिहितासौ
> चित्रविहितेयं य विवं द्यावापृथिव्योर्वरं वेदैनं वरो गच्छति स अभ्यामेव प्रसूत इंद्रो वृत्रमहन् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Having set up the Vārtraghna oblation and taken the thunderbolt, Indra again advanced against Vṛtra. Heaven and Earth said to him: "Do not strike; he rests on us two." They then said: "Let us choose a boon." Heaven asked to be set with stars, and Earth said she would be variously formed. Whoever knows this boon of Heaven and Earth, the boon comes to him. Authorised by the two, Indra slew Vṛtra.

### Printed p. 165

Then Indra, having completed in full the ājyabhāga and the oblations that are the means of slaying Vṛtra, took the thunderbolt in his hand and rushed against Vṛtra to destroy him. Heaven and Earth (earth and atmosphere) then stopped Indra: "O Indra, do not kill Vṛtra. He pervades the whole region from the earth up to heaven. He lives in dependence on us, and we cannot give him up." When Indra did not agree, Heaven and Earth spoke to him again: "O Indra, if we are to give room for the slaying of Vṛtra, you must grant the boon we ask." Indra said, "So be it." Then the atmosphere (heaven) asked as its boon that it be adorned with stars. The earth asked that it be filled with many kinds of wonderful things: human beings, animals, trees, mountains, rivers, seas and so on. Indra gave the boon accordingly. The man who knows this manner in which Heaven and Earth obtained their boons obtains the desired objects he wishes for and is happy. Afterwards Indra, having obtained the leave of Heaven and Earth, struck Vṛtra with the thunderbolt and slew him.

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ते देवा वृत्रग्ं हत्वाग्नीषोमावब्रुवन् हव्यं नो वहतमिति तावब्रूतामप तेजसौ वै त्र्यौ वृत्रे वै त्ययोस्तेज इति तेऽब्रुवन् क इदमच्छैतीति गौरित्यब्रुवन्गौर्वाव सर्वस्य मित्रमिति साब्रवीद्वरं वृणै मय्येव सतोभयेन भुनजाध्वा इति तद्गौराहरत्तस्माद्दवि सतोभयेन भुंजत एतद्वा
> अग्नेस्तेजो यद्धृतमेतत्सोमस्य यत्पयो य एवमग्नीषोमयो

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

After slaying Vṛtra the gods asked Agni and Soma to carry the oblation for them. The two answered that their vigour had gone away into Vṛtra. The gods asked who would go for it; the cow was chosen, being the friend of all. She said she would choose a boon: that people should enjoy food through her. The cow brought it, and so people still eat with her. This, the ghee, is Agni's vigour; this, the milk, is Soma's. He who knows the vigour of Agni and Soma becomes, as it were, vigorous.

After the slaying of Vṛtra, all the gods who were near Indra addressed Agni and Soma: "O Agni and Soma, you have now come back from the mouth of Vṛtra. From now on, carry the oblations that are offered for us in sacrifices and the like, and present them to us." Agni and Soma answered the gods: "O gods, we were for a long time in the body of Vṛtra, and through his biting and the like we have become without vigour; that is, our strength has dwindled. All our strength, our vigour, has stayed behind in Vṛtra." The gods then deliberated among themselves how to bring back the vigour that Agni and Soma had left in Vṛtra's body. Since the cow does no harm to anyone and behaves in a friendly way toward all, they decided to send her and have the vigour brought from Vṛtra. They then called the cow and told her to bring the vigour that was in Vṛtra. She said: "So be it; but the vigour I bring must always remain in me in the form of milk and ghee, and people must obtain these from me and carry out their meals and the like. If you grant this boon I will bring it." The gods said, "So be it," and gave the boon accordingly. Having received the boon, the cow brought that vigour. As a result of this granting of the boon, people in the world even now keep obtaining nourishing food such as milk and ghee from cows and take their meals with it.

### Printed p. 166

Here the ghee, or धृत (*dhṛta*), is said to be Agni's vigour. For that very reason, in sacrifices and the like, people offer oblations into the fire with धृत (*dhṛta*), that is ghee, and make the fire blaze up. Milk is said to be Soma's vigour. For Soma is liquid like milk, and to heighten the taste of the Soma juice milk is mixed with it. This is called गदाशिर (*gadāśira*). By mixing milk in this way the vigour of Soma, or its essence (its taste), increases. The man who knows this greatness of the vigour of Agni and of Soma becomes full of vigour.

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ब्रह्मवादिनो वदंति किंदेवत्यं पौर्णमासमिति प्राजापत्यमिति ब्रूयात्तेनेंद्रं ज्येष्यं पुत्रं निरवासाययदिति तस्माज्जैष्ठ्यं पुत्रं धनेन
> निरवासयंति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The expounders of the Veda ask what the deity of the full-moon sacrifice is. One should answer: Prajāpati. By it he settled Indra, his eldest son, in his inheritance; therefore people settle the eldest son with wealth. (Tai. Saṃ. 2-5-2-1 to 7.)

(Tai. Saṃ. 2-5-2-1 to 7)

The expounders of the Veda ask who the chief deity of this sacrifice called Paurṇamāsya is. The answer to be given is that Prajāpati is the deity presiding over the sacrifice. But Prajāpati has no share of the oblation in this sacrifice; so how is Prajāpati the deity of this sacrifice? The answer: in the beginning Prajāpati created all the sacrifices. This is stated in the Vedic sentence—

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> प्रजापतिर्यज्ञानसृजताग्निहोत्रं चाग्निष्टोमं च पौर्णमासीं
> चोक्थ्यं चामावास्यां जातिरात्रं च ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Prajāpati created the sacrifices: the Agnihotra and the Agniṣṭoma, the full-moon sacrifice, the Ukthya, the new-moon sacrifice, and the Atirātra. (Tai. Saṃ. 1-5-9-1.)

(Tai. Saṃ. 1-5-9-1)

It is said here that Prajāpati created all the sacrifices: the Agnihotra, the Agniṣṭoma, the Paurṇamāsī (the sacrifice performed on the full-moon day), the Ukthya, the Amāvāsyā (the sacrifice performed on the new-moon day), the Atirātra and the rest. All the sacrifices created by Prajāpati became his own property. Therefore, as is the custom in worldly life, that is, as one gives one's property to one's eldest son, Prajāpati handed over the chief deityship of these sacrifices to Indra, his eldest son. This is stated in the very sentence of the Taittirīya Saṃhitā: तेनेंद्रं निरवासाययत्तेनेंद्रः परमां काष्ठामगच्छत् (*teneṃdraṃ niravāsāyayatteneṃdraḥ paramāṃ kāṣṭhāmagacchat*). Therefore all the oblations offered to Indra, the son, in the Paurṇamāsya sacrifice are to be regarded as offered to Prajāpati, the father. For this very reason it is customary to say that this sacrifice, the Paurṇamāsya, is Prājāpatya, that is, related to Prajāpati. In accordance with this order, even now in worldly life it is customary for a father to give all his property, wealth and the rest, to his eldest son.

### Printed p. 167

**The story of the goddess Prāsahā**

The gods said to Indra: "O Indra, we honoured you and offered you in the sacrifice all the choice things you wished. You alone have taken all the choice things. Give us some of them too." Indra said: "That cannot be. I alone desired all these choice things, and I obtained them all. What reason is there to give them to you?" The gods again said, "O Indra, do not refuse our request like this; give us a little share too." When they prayed thus, Indra looked at them with a gracious eye. He said nothing more.

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ते देवा अब्रुवन्नियं ना इंद्रस्य प्रिया जाया पावाता प्रासहा नामास्यामेवेच्छामहा इति तथेति तस्यामैच्छंत सैनानब्रवीत्पातर्वः प्रतिवक्तास्मीति तस्मात्त्रयः पत्याविच्छंते तस्मादु स्त्रनुरात्रं पत्याविच्छते तां प्रातरुपायन्सैतदेव प्रत्यपह्यत ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The gods said: "This is Indra's dear wife, Vāvātā, of the middle rank, named Prāsahā; let us seek her out." They sought her, and she said she would answer them in the morning. Hence three kinds of women seek their husband; a woman seeks her husband at night. They approached her in the morning, and she answered with this very verse. (Ai. Brā. 12-22.)

(Ai. Brā. 12-22)

Since Indra said nothing, the gods did not understand his mind. They therefore spoke among themselves: "This woman who is before us now is Indra's beloved wife. She is of the middle rank. Her name is Vāvātā. Kings have three kinds of wives. Among them it is customary to call the wife of the highest rank Mahiṣī, the wife of the middle rank Vāvātā, and the wife of the lowest rank Parivṛtti. For that reason, in the duties to be performed by the women in the Aśvamedha sacrifice, the Mahiṣī is designated by the word भूः (*bhūḥ*), the wife called Vāvātā by the word भुवः (*bhuvaḥ*), and the wife called Parivṛtti by the word सुवः (*suvaḥ*). Of these three, the wife of the middle rank, called Vāvātā, bears the name Prāsahā. For this wife, knowing her husband's wishes and acting accordingly, is able to do every task by force, and so she is very dear to the king. Therefore, understanding that it was fitting to learn the mind of Indra the king through her, they laid their matter before her. Hearing it, Vāvātā said: "O gods, now is not the proper time to tell Indra of your matter and learn his mind. In the world it is the custom for women to tell their husbands any important matter at night, when they are in private. When they are in private there are no obstructions or disturbances of any kind, so the man listens sympathetically to what the woman says and gives a fitting answer. Therefore tonight, when I am in private with Indra, I shall bring up your matters and learn Indra's mind. So if you come tomorrow morning, I shall tell you Indra's answer." The gods said, "So be it," and came the next morning at dawn to ask. Vāvātā then spoke in reply this sentence in the form of a mantra—

### Printed p. 168

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यद्वावान पुगुतमं पुराषाळा वृत्रहेंद्रो नामान्यप्राः ।
> अचेति प्रासहस्पतिस्तुविष्मान् यदीमुश्मसि कर्तसे करत्तक् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

A Ṛk is quoted, with its reference (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-74-6).

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-74-6)

Among the men of old, Indra slew Vṛtra, was victorious in many other battles as well, and won fame throughout the world. Therefore Indra bears the name Prāsahaspati. प्र (*pra*) means "exceedingly", सहः (*sahaḥ*) "of strength", पतिः (*patiḥ*) "lord": the name, "the exceedingly mighty one", is a name that fits its meaning. Having earned such a name by the great deed of slaying Vṛtra, he has made known throughout the world that honoured names such as the Māhendra graha and the midday pressing are very dear to him. This Indra knows what the gods desire, and he is very mighty: this is the meaning of the three pādas of this Ṛk. This is what Vāvātā told the gods. **NOTE**

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इंद्रो वै प्रासहस्पतिस्तुविष्मान् । यदीमुश्मसि कर्तवे करत्तदिति यदेवैतदवोचामाकरत्तदित्येवैनांस्तदब्रवीत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"Indra is Prāsahaspati, the mighty; 'whatever we wish he will do': what we have said, he will do." So she told them.

Having heard the answer Vāvātā gave through the three pādas of the mantra as above, the gods spoke among themselves by the fourth pāda—

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यदीमुश्मसि कर्तवे करत्तत् ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The fourth pāda of the Ṛk is quoted.

The meaning of the fourth pāda is: "If we gods too have a share in the sacrifice, and if Indra will arrange that sacrificial share for us, then he will not leave our wish unfulfilled." Or, if this sentence of the fourth pāda is taken to be Vāvātā's own reply, it means: "O gods, Indra will fulfil, or will accomplish, the thing you have thought out and prayed Indra for." This is stated plainly here—

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ते देवा अब्रुवन्यप्यस्या इहास्तु यानोऽस्मिन्न वैकमविददिति तथेति तस्या
> अप्यत्राकुर्वन् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The gods said: "Let her too have a part here; she has served us in this matter." "So be it," they said, and they gave her a part in it.

Hearing Vāvātā's reply, the gods said: "This Vāvātā has done a great service to us gods, so we must do a service in return. We do not know whether this Vāvātā has any connection with the niṣkevalya śastra mantra. Whatever the case, let us arrange a fitting connection for this Vāvātā in the niṣkevalya śastra mantra." Having thought so, they did accordingly.

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> तस्मादेषात्रापि शस्यते यद्वानान्पुरुत्वमं पुराषाळिति ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Therefore this verse too is recited there: यद्वानान्पुरुत्वमं पुराषाळिति (*yadvānānpurutvamaṃ purāṣāḷiti*).

Because the gods thus arranged for Vāvātā a connection, in the niṣkevalya śastra mantra, with this Ṛk (यद्वावान (*yadvāvāna*), Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-24.6), this Ṛk is called a dhāyyā mantra in the niṣkevalya śastra mantra.

### Printed p. 169

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सेना वा इंद्रस्य प्रिया जाया नावाता प्रासहा नाम को नाम प्रजापतिः
> श्वशुरस्तद्यास्य कामे सेना जयेत्तस्या अर्धात्तिष्ठंस्तृणमुभयुतः परिच्छिद्येतरां सेनामभ्यस्यॆत्प्रासहे कस्त्वा पश्यतीति तद्यथैवादः स्नुषा श्वशुराल्लज्जमाना निलीयमान्यैत्येवमेव सा सेना भज्यमाना निलीयमानैति
> यत्रैवं विद्वांस्तृणमुभयतः परिच्छिद्येतरां सेनामभ्यस्यति प्रासहे कस्त्वा
> पश्यतीति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Vāvātā, Indra's dear wife named Prāsahā, is the army; Ka, that is Prajāpati, is her father-in-law. One who desires his army to win should stand on its side, cut a blade of grass at both ends, and throw it at the other army, saying प्रासहे कस्त्वा पश्यतीति (*prāsahe kastvā paśyatīti*). As a daughter-in-law shrinks away from her father-in-law out of shame, so that army, being broken, shrinks away. The knower who does this casts the grass at the other army with those words.

The praise of Vāvātā, Indra's wife, who spoke this dhāyyā mantra, is given here in somewhat more detail. It was said before that Vāvātā was Indra's dear wife and that her name was Prāsahā. In worldly usage this Vāvātā or Prāsahā is said to exist in the form of the army made ready for battle, because Indra's wife is the deity presiding over the army. In the Veda too it is said: इंद्राणी वै सेनाय देवता (*iṃdrāṇī vai senāya devatā*). It has been explained before that Prajāpati is what the word क (*ka*) denotes, since she says को नाम (*ko nāma*), "I am who". That Prajāpati is the father-in-law of Indra's wife. As to Indra being Prajāpati's son, the Veda itself says: प्रजापतिरिंद्रमसृजतानुजावरं देवानां (*prajāpatiriṃdramasṛjatānujāvaraṃ devānāṃ*). This being so, in worldly practice a man eager for battle, if he wishes his army to win, must do one thing. When the two armies stand facing each other ready to fight, that man must go forward, passing beyond his army, that is, a little ahead of the middle of the army, stand on the ground, take a blade of grass, cut off its root and its tip, and throw the middle part toward the enemy army as one throws an arrow. While throwing it he must throw the blade of grass with the mantra प्रासहे कस्त्या पश्यति (*prāsahe kastyā paśyati*): "O wife of Indra named Prāsahā, Prajāpati, who is denoted by the word Ka and is your father-in-law, is looking at you with his eyes." If the blade of grass is thrown thus with this mantra, defeat comes to the enemy army. This is made clear by an illustration. In worldly life, in the house of a respectable householder faithful to the customs of his forefathers, when a young daughter-in-law sees her father-in-law she adjusts the end of her sari out of shyness, draws in her hands and other limbs, and slips quietly into the inner part of the house. In the same way, through the throwing of the blade of grass with the mantra, the enemy army suffers defeat and retreats, crossing mountains, forests and rivers, and flees to its own country. Having broken off this matter here as incidental, he now takes up the context at hand—

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> तानिंद्र उवाचापि वोऽत्मास्त्विति ते देवा ८ ब्रुवन्विराड्याज्यास्तु निष्केवल्यस्य या त्रयस्त्रिंशदक्षरा त्रयस्त्रिंशद्वै देवा अष्टौ वसन एकादश रुद्रा
> द्वादशादित्याः प्रजापतिश्च वषट्कारश्च देवता अक्षरभाजः करोत्यक्षरमक्षरमेवतद्देवता अनुप्रपिबंति देवपात्रेणैव तद्देवतास्तृप्यंति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Indra spoke to them: "Let there be a share for you also." The gods said: "Let the virāj be the ājyā of the niṣkevalya, the one of thirty-three syllables. The gods are thirty-three: eight Vasus, eleven Rudras, twelve Ādityas, Prajāpati and the vaṣaṭkāra. He makes the deities sharers by syllable; the deities drink up syllable after syllable, and the deities are satisfied by the divine vessel itself."

### Printed p. 170

On hearing Vāvātā's reply (answer), the gods went to Indra's presence. Indra said to them: "O gods, as you desire, a share of the sacrifice has been given to you in the niṣkevalya-śastra of the sacrifice." Hearing this, the gods said: "We, the gods, are thirty-three. How? Eight Vasus, eleven Rudras and twelve Ādityas, with Prajāpati as one and the vaṣaṭkāra as one: thirty-three in all." Since there are thirty-three gods, they prayed Indra to provide the soma-drinking by means of a yājyā mantra in the Virāṭ metre, of thirty-three syllables, namely:

पिबा सोममिंद्र मंदतु त्वा यं ते सुषाव हर्यश्वाद्रिः । सोतुर्बाहुभ्यां सुयतो नार्वा ॥ (*pibā somamiṃdra maṃdatu tvā yaṃ te suṣāva haryaśvādriḥ | soturbāhubhyāṃ suyato nārvā ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-22-1)

This yājyā mantra ought to have thirty-three syllables, but it has only twenty-nine. Even so, by the method of combining syllables, it is to be reckoned as having thirty-three syllables although some are lacking. In this way the thirty-three syllables of the yājyā mantra, each syllable being like one god, will satisfy the thirty-three gods. Next he explains the harm that results if the yājyā offering is made with a mantra of another metre, leaving aside this procedure.

यं कामयेतानायतवान् स्यादित्यविराजास्य यजेद्गायत्रा ना त्रिष्टुभा वान्मेन वा छंदसा वषट्कुर्यादनायतनवंतमेवैनं तत्करोति (*yaṃ kāmayetānāyatavān syādityavirājāsya yajedgāyatrā nā triṣṭubhā vānmena vā chaṃdasā vaṣaṭkuryādanāyatanavaṃtamevainaṃ tatkaroti*)
(Ai. Brā. 12-22)

If the hotṛ desires that the sacrificing yajamāna's house and other possessions be destroyed, then in the niṣkevalya-śastra he should perform the homa while reciting, not the yājyā mantra in the Virāṭ metre, but mantras of Gāyatrī, Triṣṭubh or another metre. If he does so, the yajamāna's dwellings, house and the rest, will be destroyed. Therefore, if there is a wish that the yajamāna's household wealth be lasting, the hotṛ should perform the yājyā homa with the Ṛk in the Virāṭ metre, पिबा सोममिंद्र (*pibā somamiṃdra*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-22-1), and no other. If he does so, the yajamāna will have abundance of house and dwelling-places.

**The reason Indra came to be called Marutvān**

The permanent companionship of Indra and the Maruts, the relation of king and subjects between them, the offering of oblations in the principal sacrificial acts to Indra together with the Maruts, and the help the Maruts gave Indra when he fought with Vṛtra

### Printed p. 171

are praised in many places in the Saṃhitās and Brāhmaṇas. In the Śatapatha-brāhmaṇa an earlier story is told that shows how Indra stayed together with the Maruts, apart from the other gods.

अतिथ्येन है देवा इष्ट्वा । तान्त्वमदविंदत्ते चतुर्धाव्यद्रवन्नन्योन्यस्य श्रिया तिष्ठमाना अग्निर्वसुभिः सोमो रुद्रैर्वरुण आदित्यैरिंद्रो मरुद्भिर्भृहस्पतिर्विश्वैर्देवैः ॥ (*atithyena hai devā iṣṭvā | tāntvamadaviṃdatte caturdhāvyadravannanyonyasya śriyā tiṣṭhamānā agnirvasubhiḥ somo rudrairvaruṇa ādityairiṃdro marudbhirbhṛhaspatirviśvairdevaiḥ ‖*)
(Śa. Brā. 3-4-2-1)

Formerly, when the gods performed the rite of hospitality, discord arose among them; unwilling to join one another, they formed four groups and separated. Agni joined the Vasus, Soma the Rudras, Varuṇa the Ādityas, Indra the Maruts, and Bṛhaspati the Viśve Devas, each forming a separate group. In this story the close relation that Indra developed with the Maruts, and kept with them, is told.

Moreover, in many places it is said that Indra is the sovereign and the Maruts are the subjects. For example, in the same Śatapatha-brāhmaṇa:

क्षत्रं ना इंद्रो विशो मरुतो विशा वै क्षत्रियो बलवान् भवति ॥ (*kṣatraṃ nā iṃdro viśo maruto viśā vai kṣatriyo balavān bhavati ‖*)
(Śa. Brā. 4-3-3-6)

Indra is the king, possessed of kṣatra-splendour; the Maruts are his subjects. It is indicated that a king becomes powerful only through the support of his subjects, and that Indra's prowess grew through the help of the Maruts. Because of this close relation and companionship, the practice of offering oblations to Indra and the Maruts together, addressing them jointly, is found in the Śrutis. And with Ṛks such as

स नो मरुद्भिर्वृषभ श्रवो धा उग्र उग्रेभिः स्थविरः सहोदाः ॥ (*sa no marudbhirvṛṣabha śravo dhā ugra ugrebhiḥ sthaviraḥ sahodāḥ ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-171-5)

there is joint invocation of Indra and the Maruts and an offering of oblation to them. Having described the companionship in this way, the Bṛhaddevatā says:

मरुद्भिः सह यत्रेंद्रो मरुत्वांस्तत्र सोऽभवत् (*marudbhiḥ saha yatreṃdro marutvāṃstatra so 'bhavat*)
(Bṛ. De. 4-56)

By sentences such as this the epithet that denotes the relation of these deities is explained. But in one sūkta, as a result of a dispute between Indra and the Maruts, their mutual enmity and Indra's anger are indicated. The dispute between them that is the root of this anger is stated as follows:

क्व १ स्यावो मरुतः स्वधासीद्यन्मामेकं समाधत्ताहिहत्ये । अहं ह्यु १ ग्रस्तविषस्तुविष्मान्मिश्वस्य शत्रोरनमं वधस्मैः ॥ (*kva 1 syāvo marutaḥ svadhāsīdyanmāmekaṃ samādhattāhihatye | ahaṃ hyu 1 grastaviṣastuviṣmānmiśvasya śatroranamaṃ vadhasmaiḥ ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-165-6)

### Printed p. 172

"O Maruts, it is false to say that I slew Ahi (Vṛtra) with your help. Where indeed was that power in you? Since my own power is extraordinary, it helped me at the time of the slaying of Vṛtra without needing the help of any other power. I alone am terrible, of wondrous strength and endowed with greatness." So Indra, looking on the Maruts with contempt, displays his anger. Here that anger is indicated. It is well known that Indra received the help of the Maruts at the time of killing the asura Vṛtra. Even so, when the Maruts mentioned this and asked that, in remembrance of that help, they too be given a share in the oblation that belongs to Indra, Indra declares that no help whatever came from the Maruts and that he slew all his enemies by his own power. The authors of the Nirukta, telling this same matter, say:

अगस्त्य इंद्राय हविर्निरुप्य मरुद्भः संप्रतिश्वांचकार । स इंद्र एत्य परिदेवयांचक्रे ॥ (*agastya iṃdrāya havirnirupya marudbhaḥ saṃpratiśvāṃcakāra | sa iṃdra etya paridevayāṃcakre ‖*)
(Ni. 1-5)

Having set out the circumstance that the sage Agastya prepared an oblation for Indra, and as soon as he wished to offer a share of it to the Maruts too, Indra objected to it, the authors of the Nirukta cite the following Ṛk, which expresses this matter:

न नूनमस्ति नो त्वः कस्तद्वेद यदद्भुतं । अन्यस्य चित्तमभि संचरेण्यमुताधीतं विनश्यति ॥ (*na nūnamasti no tvaḥ kastadveda yadadbhutaṃ | anyasya cittamabhi saṃcareṇyamutādhītaṃ vinaśyati ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-170-1)

Here the prayer is addressed to the Maruts, so that the force of that anger may abate and not harm him, and so that the share of the oblation that rightly belongs to the Maruts may reach them. After such words of conciliation, Indra's friendship grows as before, and he himself gives his share of the oblation to the Maruts. The seer in the eighth Maṇḍala praises this same matter, saying:

वृत्रस्य त्वा श्वसथादीषमाणा विश्वे देवा अजहुर्ये सखायः । मरुद्धिरिंद्र सख्यं ते अत्त्वथेमा विश्वाः पृतना जयासि ॥ (*vṛtrasya tvā śvasathādīṣamāṇā viśve devā ajahurye sakhāyaḥ | maruddhiriṃdra sakhyaṃ te attvathemā viśvāḥ pṛtanā jayāsi ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-96-7)

"O Indra, your friendship with the Maruts is well known. Give them too a share in your oblation, and with their help conquer all your enemies": so the prayer runs.

### Printed p. 173

**Indra's slaying of the demon Namuci**

After giving a brief account of this Namuci, we shall explain how Indra killed him.

The name Namuci occurs nine times in the Ṛgveda, and also in the Vājasaneyi-saṃhitā, the Taittirīya-brāhmaṇa (Tai. Brā. 1-7-1-6, 7), the Śatapatha-brāhmaṇa (Śa. Brā. 12-7-1-10) and other places. He is spoken of as being of asura nature, and in the Ṛgveda as a dāsa (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-30-7, 8). In one place (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-57-7) he is described as māyin. The Ṛgveda says that when Indra defeated Namuci he had the friendship or support of Namī and Sāyka (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-53-7; 6-20-6). **NOTE** Like other asuras Namuci was slain by Indra (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-14-5; 7-19-5), or was struck down and destroyed (1-53-7). Indra killed the asura Vṛtra-Namuci and destroyed a hundred fortresses of the asuras (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-19-5). He does not cut off Namuci's head as he cut off Vṛtra's head; he kills him by crushing (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-30-8; 6-20-6), or by twisting his head (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-30-7), or he twists (wrings) his head off with the foam of water (the sea) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-14-3). In some places it is also said that he cut off the head (Tai. Brā. 1-7-1-6, 7). In one place (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-131-4, 5) Indra sat at Namuci's side and drank liquor; then Sarasvatī healed him with the help of the Aśvin deities. All these are the accounts given in the Ṛgveda.

Now we shall explain how, in the Taittirīya-brāhmaṇa of the Yajurveda, Indra destroyed Namuci. नमुचिः । इंद्रेण सह युद्धं न मुंचतीति नमुचिः (*namuciḥ | iṃdreṇa saha yuddhaṃ na muṃcatīti namuciḥ*): some commentators have derived the form of the word Namuci thus: since he does not give up fighting with Indra, he is called Namuci. As this asura was of great strength, it was very hard for Indra to fight and conquer him. Therefore Indra had to kill him by a stratagem. In the Taittirīya-brāhmaṇa there is an upākhyāna on this matter.

इंद्रो वृत्रग्ं हत्वा । असुरान् पराभाव्य । नमुचिमासुरं नालभत । तग्ं शच्यागृह्णात् । तौ समलभेताम् । सोऽस्मादभिशुनतरोऽभवत् । सोऽब्रवीत् । संधाग्ं संदथावहै । अथ त्वावस्रक्षामि । न मा शुष्केण नार्द्रेण हनः । नॆ दिवा न नक्तमिति । स एतमपां फेनमसिंचत् । न वा एष तुष्टो नार्द्रो व्युष्टासीत् । अनुदितः सूर्यः । न वा एतद्दिवा न नक्तं । तस्यैतस्मिन् लोके । अपां फेनेन शिर उदवर्तयत् । तदेन मन्ववर्तत । मित्रद्रुगिति । स एतानपामार्गानजनयत् । तानजुहोत् । तैर्वै स रक्षाग् स्यपाहत । यदपमार्गहोमो भवति । रक्षसामपहत्यै ॥ (*iṃdro vṛtragṃ hatvā | asurān parābhāvya | namucimāsuraṃ nālabhata | tagṃ śacyāgṛhṇāt | tau samalabhetām | so 'smādabhiśunataro 'bhavat | so 'bravīt | saṃdhāgṃ saṃdathāvahai | atha tvāvasrakṣāmi | na mā śuṣkeṇa nārdreṇa hanaḥ | nè divā na naktamiti | sa etamapāṃ phenamasiṃcat | na vā eṣa tuṣṭo nārdro vyuṣṭāsīt | anuditaḥ sūryaḥ | na vā etaddivā na naktaṃ | tasyaitasmin loke | apāṃ phenena śira udavartayat | tadena manvavartata | mitradrugiti | sa etānapāmārgānajanayat | tānajuhot | tairvai sa rakṣāg syapāhata | yadapamārgahomo bhavati | rakṣasāmapahatyai ‖*)
(Tai. Brā. 1-7-1-6, 7)

### Printed p. 174

After slaying the asura Vṛtra, Indra slew other asuras as well. But he could not conquer the asura named Namuci. This one was very strong; no kind of weapon made him yield. So Indra, wishing to conquer him by the strength of his own body, began a wrestling fight with him. Yet the more they fought, the more Namuci's strength grew. Indra could not free himself from his grip. Then Indra asked Namuci to release him, and the asura said to Indra: "O Indra, if you are to be released, you must do as I say. Let us now make a compact (agreement) with each other. You may again wish to fight me by some device and kill me. In that case, the weapon you use to kill me must be neither wet nor dry; you must not kill me by day, and you must not kill me at night either. If you agree to these conditions, I shall release you." Indra agreed, "So be it," freed himself from his grip, and began to consider how to conquer him. After some time Indra thought of a means. As he was roaming on the seashore, the foam of the sea fell under his eye. This foam is neither wet nor dry. "Therefore I shall make this my weapon and kill Namuci. When night has passed and the dawn has begun, before the sun rises, it is quite light. That time is not night, since it is not dark; and since the sun has not risen, it is not day. If I fight Namuci at such a time and kill him with a weapon made of the foam of water, I shall not have broken my word." Having resolved thus, he fought Namuci accordingly and cut off his head with that foam. Even so the asura did not die, but pursued Indra to kill him. "O Indra, betrayer of a friend, stand, stand! You promised me before that you would not kill me, and now you do such a treacherous deed. Even now I shall not leave you without killing you," he said. Then Indra, afraid, created a special tree called apāmārga and with its sticks made a homa in the fire for the increase of virility. By this Indra's strength grew, and he destroyed that Namuci. Therefore those who wish to be delivered from the affliction of rākṣasas should perform a homa in the fire with apāmārga sticks for the increase of virility. By this homa their strength grows and they are able to conquer the rākṣasas: such is the upākhyāna. The name of this Namuci occurs in the Ṛgveda in:

नम्मा यदिंद्र सख्या परावति निबर्हयो नमुचिं नाम मायिनं ॥ (*nammā yadiṃdra sakhyā parāvati nibarhayo namuciṃ nāma māyinaṃ ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-53-7)

यः पिपुं नमुचिं यो रुधिकां तस्मा इंद्रायांधसो जुहोत ॥ (*yaḥ pipuṃ namuciṃ yo rudhikāṃ tasmā iṃdrāyāṃdhaso juhota ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-14-5)

### Printed p. 175

निवेशने शततमाविवेषीरहङ्ङ वृत्रं नमुचिमुताहन् (*niveśane śatatamāviveṣīrahaṅṅa vṛtraṃ namucimutāhan*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-19-5)

त्वं जघंथ नमुचिं मखस्युं दासं कृष्णान ऋषये विमायं ॥ (*tvaṃ jaghaṃtha namuciṃ makhasyuṃ dāsaṃ kṛṣṇāna ṛṣaye vimāyaṃ ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-23-7)

अत्रा दासस्य नमुचेः शिरो यपवर्तयो मनवे गातुमिच्छन् ॥ (*atrā dāsasya namuceḥ śiro yapavartayo manave gātumicchan ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-30-7)

युजं हि मामकृथा आदिदिंद्र शिरो दासस्य नमुचेर्मथायन् ॥ (*yujaṃ hi māmakṛthā ādidiṃdra śiro dāsasya namucermathāyan ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-30-8)

प्र श्येनो न मदिरमंशुमस्मै शिरो दासस्य नमुचेर्मथायन् ॥ (*pra śyeno na madiramaṃśumasmai śiro dāsasya namucermathāyan ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-20-6)

अपां फेनेन नमुचेः शिर इंद्रोदवर्तयः (*apāṃ phenena namuceḥ śira iṃdrodavartayaḥ*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-14-13)

युवं सुराममश्विना नमुचावासुरे सचा (*yuvaṃ surāmamaśvinā namucāvāsure sacā*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-131-4)

It is in these Ṛks; it is nowhere else.

**The reason Indra came to be called Yajiṣī**

Yajiṣī: for this word Yāska gives the following explanation:

ऋजीषी सोमो यत्सोमस्य पूयमानस्यातिरिच्यते तदृजीषमपार्जितं भवति तेनर्जिषी सोमः । अथाप्यैंद्रो निगमो भवति ऋजीषी वज्री इति ॥ (*ṛjīṣī somo yatsomasya pūyamānasyātiricyate tadṛjīṣamapārjitaṃ bhavati tenarjiṣī somaḥ | athāpyaiṃdro nigamo bhavati ṛjīṣī vajrī iti ‖*)
(Ni. 5-12)

After the soma plant has been crushed with stones and the soma juice drawn off, the juiceless, husk-like substance that remains is called ṛjīṣa. This ṛjīṣa is thrown away as useless. On some occasions such ṛjīṣa is soaked in water and a little more juice is pressed and drawn from it. Such soma juice is called ṛjīṣaḥ somaḥ. At the third pressing, flour and other āśira substances are mixed with such ṛjīṣa-soma and offered to Indra. Because Indra drinks this ṛjīṣa-soma, it has become customary to call Indra by the very word ṛjīṣī. Besides, it is well known that Indra's horses, called Hari, eat the husks of these soma plants called ṛjīṣa. On this matter Yāska, by way of example, cites the Śruti sentence बद्धांते हरीधाना उप ऋजीषं जिघ्रतां (*baddhāṃte harīdhānā upa ṛjīṣaṃ jighratāṃ*). The meaning of the sentence above is: "O Indra, let your horses eat grain and smell this ṛjīṣa."

### Printed p. 176
What is the Ṛjīṣa? Why does it have this name? What is its use in the sacrifice? These and similar matters are examined here closely, as stated in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa and the Taittirīya Saṃhitā.

(Ai. Brā. 13-25)

In former times King Soma (the soma plant; it is customary to call the soma plant "King Soma" out of respect) was in heaven. It was not in this world. Knowing this, the gods and the seers considered how this Soma might be brought here. Having deliberated, they concluded that the metres had the power to bring Soma from heaven to earth. They addressed the metres: "O metres, for our sake, that is, for use in our sacrifice, bring to earth the Soma that is in heaven." The metres accepted the prayer, took the forms of certain well-known birds and flew up to heaven. First the Jagatī metre took bird form and flew toward heaven. But after going a short distance it tired, could not reach heaven, and turned back. Then the Triṣṭubh metre said that it would bring Soma and leapt up into the atmosphere. But after going a little beyond half the way it grew weary and turned back. The gods saw that Jagatī and Triṣṭubh had come back unable to bring Soma, so they went to the Gāyatrī metre and prayed: "O Gāyatrī, bring us Soma from heaven." Gāyatrī agreed, took the form of a falcon, flew to heaven, frightened Sthāna, Bhrāja and the other guardians of Soma there, seized Soma and carried it to earth. Because she brought the soma plant, Gāyatrī gained the foremost place among the metres.

### Printed p. 177
(Ai. Brā. 13-26)

When bringing the soma plants the falcon carried them bound with both its feet and its beak. The Soma (or soma plant) that the bird brought holding it with its right foot became the morning pressing. Gāyatrī made it, the morning pressing, her own abode. Therefore among all the pressings the morning pressing is held by the sacrificers to be the best. In that morning rite the application of mantras and the like is also special. Hence the man who knows this well is regarded as the best and chief among his equals. When Gāyatrī brought Soma holding it with the left foot, the left foot not having as much strength as the right, the plant kept slipping a little from the foot from time to time. For this reason the midday pressing did not become equal to the morning pressing in strength and power. Seeing this, the gods deliberated and established the Triṣṭubh metre, and Indra, the best of the gods, for the metres used in the midday pressing. Through the company of Indra, the best of the gods, and of Triṣṭubh, dear to him, the midday pressing gained a place equal to the morning pressing in strength, power and excellence. The man who knows this equality, strength and excellence of the morning and midday pressings obtains prosperity in wealth and the like, and well-being, and is happy. Then the portion of Soma that Gāyatrī, in falcon form, bit off with her beak and brought became known as the third pressing.

(Ai. Brā. 13-27)

While Gāyatrī (the falcon) was coming, biting the soma plant with her beak, she drank the sap in the plant little by little. Therefore, since the plants brought in the beak had little sap and not much, this third pressing did not become equal to the morning and midday pressings in strength, power and the like. The gods saw this, deliberated, and found that the suitable means lay in cattle. From cattle come milk, curds, and the oblation materials such as ghee (ājya) obtained from them. If milk is mixed with the soma juice, which is scanty in the plants, that juice becomes strong. By offering oblations with ghee and with parts of the animals the pressing rite becomes complete.

### Printed p. 178
Therefore those who know the sacrificial rite add milk and the other āśira substances to the juice of the Ṛjīṣa used in the third pressing (the husks of the soma plant left after the juice has been taken in the morning and midday pressings are called Ṛjīṣa), and make oblations with the ghee obtained from cattle and with the heart and other limbs of cattle; knowing that by this the third pressing becomes equal to the morning and midday pressings in strength, power and the like, they act accordingly. The same matter is stated in another śākhā as follows.

(Tai. Saṃ. 6-1-6-4)

Thus the same idea is stated in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā. Whoever knows this matter, that the three pressings, morning, midday and third, have gained an equal place in strength, power and excellence, is endowed with every prosperity and is happy.

**How Indra and Vāyu obtained the first drink of Soma**

In the Ṛk beginning अग्रं पिबा मधूनां (*agraṃ pibā madhūnāṃ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-46-1) it is said that Vāyu, rather than Indra and the other gods, is first entitled to drink Soma. In support of this, the Ṛk

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-92-1)

is cited, and it indicates that the seer there prays that Vāyu should drink Soma first. How Vāyu obtained the foremost place among the gods in drinking Soma is the subject of an old story in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa. We set it out here.

### Printed p. 179
(Ai. Brā. 9-25)

In former times all the gods wished to drink Soma together, and a dispute arose among them as to who should drink Soma first; each began to argue that he should drink first. Since the matter could not be settled in any way, they all conferred and came to an agreement: "Let us all arrange a contest. Whoever wins in it shall be entitled to drink Soma first." All agreed, and the contest was fixed thus: those who ran from one place to another, a distant one, and first reached a rock or a tree there, were to be regarded as winners. All agreed that the winner should be victorious among them and drink Soma first, and all the gods began to run at the same time. Of all the running gods Vāyu reached the appointed place ahead of everyone. Indra came right behind Vāyu and was second. After Indra, Mitra and Varuṇa, and after them the Aśvin gods, ran in and reached the appointed place. Therefore among these gods Vāyu ran ahead of all and was the victor.

While Vāyu, and Indra behind him, were running, this conversation took place between them. Indra, behind him, saw Vāyu running swiftly and resolved: "This Vāyu will run ahead of all and be victor. Somehow I must run with him and win." Using all his strength he ran close to Vāyu. Yet there was no sign of leaving Vāyu behind and going ahead, so Indra said to Vāyu: "O Vāyu, we two are ahead of everyone. So let us regard ourselves both as winners and share the first drink of Soma between us, half for me, half for you. Thus let us both cooperate and gain the victory." Hearing this Vāyu said: "O Indra, I am ahead of you, if only a little. So I alone shall be the winner. Everything in the first drink of Soma must come to me. I cannot give you half." Then Indra said: "Very well, I do not want half. Take two of three parts, and give me only one part." Vāyu did not agree to this: "In any case I shall win. The whole share will be mine; I do not wish to give you even one part in three." Then Indra again prayed to Vāyu: "I do not want even a third; give me still less, that is, one part in four; that will do. Agree at least to that." Vāyu agreed and said it should be so.

### Printed p. 180
Therefore in the Aindravāyava graha (that is, when Indra and Vāyu drink Soma together) it was arranged that Indra should have one part and Vāyu three.

Thus Indra and Vāyu, cooperating, won the contest and first reached the appointed place. Mitra and Varuṇa came and arrived behind them; the Aśvin gods came behind them. Therefore, in whatever place they gained in the contest, in that order they became entitled to drink Soma: first Indra and Vāyu, then Mitra and Varuṇa, and after them the Aśvin gods. In this way the practice came into use of two gods drinking Soma together. So in the Aindravāyava graha Indra has only one part in four in the drinking of Soma.

तदेतद्दृषिः पश्यन्नभ्यनूनाच नियुत्वा इंद्रसारथिरिति । (*tadetaddṛṣiḥ paśyannabhyanūnāca niyutvā iṃdrasārathiriti |*)

The seer, knowing by divine vision that Indra has one part in four in the Aindravāyava graha, expresses this in the next Ṛk (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-46-2), which begins *śatenā no abhiṣṭibhiḥ*, by the words नियुत्वान् इंद्रसारथिः (*niyutvān iṃdrasārathiḥ*). Here, as the Nirukta statement नियुतो वायोः (*niyuto vāyoḥ*) (Ni. 2-28) says, the horses called Niyut are well known as the vehicle of the god Vāyu. Therefore the word *niyutvat* indicates Vāyu. In the Ṛk above the word नियुत्वान् (*niyutvān*) denotes Indra accompanied by Vāyu. Mantras such as अधा वायुं नियुतः सश्चतस्वाः (*adhā vāyuṃ niyutaḥ saścatasvāḥ*) (Tai. Brā. 2-8-1-1) support this. The meaning of इंद्रसारथिः (*iṃdrasārathiḥ*) is "Indra, the charioteer of Vāyu". Since Indra stands in the place of charioteer to Vāyu, this indicates that Indra has only a fourth part in the first drink of Soma.

### Printed p. 181

The matter stated above is made clear by an example from worldly practice. Warriors in battle sit in chariots, are victorious in battle with the help of their charioteers, and seize the wealth and the like of the enemies. When they have seized it, they give one part of that wealth, that is, one part in four, to the charioteers who helped them. This custom is current in worldly practice. In the same way, in this contest arranged by the gods, Indra was Vāyu's charioteer, and since Vāyu was victorious, a fourth part of the first Soma drink obtained in the contest became Indra's share. The main meaning is that Indra gained his fourth part as Vāyu's charioteer.

**The slaying of the asura Varāha by Indra**

There is a story that there was an asura named Varāha and that Indra killed him. Viṣṇu also has a part in it. We set out here the account stated in the Caraka Brāhmaṇa on this matter.

The main sense of this is as follows. Viṣṇu is the sacrifice. That Viṣṇu, in the form of the sacrifice, once left the gods and went away and stayed in a hidden place. The other gods did not learn of this. Only Indra learned. When Indra went to the place where Viṣṇu was, Viṣṇu asked Indra who he was. In reply Indra said: "I am the destroyer of fortresses and asuras. Who are you?" Viṣṇu said: "I am the one who carries off from fortresses the wealth and the like belonging to the asuras. Who are you? What is your work?" he asked again. Then Viṣṇu said: "If you are, as you say, the destroyer of the fortresses and of the wealth of the asuras, then the asura named Varāha, who has carried off the wealth and the like and hidden it in himself, lives in a fortress firmly built of stones, at the end of twenty-one forts from here.

### Printed p. 182
He has a great deal of the Asuras' wealth. So kill those Asuras," he said. Hearing this, Indra destroyed those cities of the Asuras, pierced the heart of the Asura named Varāha, and killed him. Then Viṣṇu brought away all the wealth that had been in the Asura. This is the main point of the story. The commentator says that this matter is stated in the Ṛk quoted above, and also in the Ṛk

अस्येदु मातुः सवनेषु सद्यो महः पितुं पपिनाञ्चार्वन्ना । मुषायद्विष्णुः पचतं सहीयान्विध्यद्वराहं तिरो अद्रिमस्ता ॥ (*asyedu mātuḥ savaneṣu sadyo mahaḥ pituṃ papināñcārvannā | muṣāyadviṣṇuḥ pacataṃ sahīyānvidhyadvarāhaṃ tiro adrimastā ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 16-17) **NOTE**

and he then explains the sentences of the present Ṛk and of the example cited, by fitting them to the story told above.

अस्येदु मातुः इत्यत्र विष्णुना हे इंद्र त्वं दुर्गाणां हंतेत्यात्मानं कथयसि तर्हि वाममुषं वराहमसुरं जहीत्युक्तार्थो विध्यद्वराहं इति पादेन प्रतिपादितः ॥ (*asyedu mātuḥ ityatra viṣṇunā he iṃdra tvaṃ durgāṇāṃ haṃtetyātmānaṃ kathayasi tarhi vāmamuṣaṃ varāhamasuraṃ jahītyuktārtho vidhyadvarāhaṃ iti pādena pratipāditaḥ ‖*)

The meaning of Viṣṇu's words in the story above is: "O Indra, you say of yourself that you are the destroyer of strongholds and of the Asuras. Then kill Varāha, who has stolen wealth and hidden it in himself." This meaning is stated in the last pāda, विध्यद्वराहं तिरो अद्रिमस्ता (*vidhyadvarāhaṃ tiro adrimastā*), of the Ṛk अस्येदु मातुः (*asyedu mātuḥ*) cited above as the example.

इंद्रेण च विष्णो त्वं दुर्गादाहर्तेति ब्रूषे मया पुराणि जितान्यसुरश्च घातितस्तस्य वामं वस्वानयेत्युक्तो विष्णुमूर्तिस्तस्य वराहासुरस्य धनं मुमोष । सोऽर्थः मुषायद्विष्णुः पचतं सहीयान् इति पादेन सूचितः ॥ (*iṃdreṇa ca viṣṇo tvaṃ durgādāharteti brūṣe mayā purāṇi jitānyasuraśca ghātitastasya vāmaṃ vasvānayetyukto viṣṇumūrtistasya varāhāsurasya dhanaṃ mumoṣa | so 'rthaḥ muṣāyadviṣṇuḥ pacataṃ sahīyān iti pādena sūcitaḥ ‖*)

And Indra said to Viṣṇu: "O Viṣṇu, you have declared yourself to be one who carries off the wealth of the Asuras from their strongholds. I have now conquered many enemy cities and killed the Asura. Go and bring the wealth that is in that Asura." Viṣṇu agreed and carried off the wealth that was with that Asura Varāha. This is made clear by the pāda of the cited Ṛk, मुषायद्विष्णुः । पचतं सहीयान् (*muṣāyadviṣṇuḥ | pacataṃ sahīyān*).

स किं पुनर्मुषितवानिति तदत्रोच्यते । विश्वेत्ता इति । हे इंद्र त्वेषितस्त्वया प्रेरितो विष्णुर्यज्ञरूपी त्वेषितस्त्वं दुर्गादाहर्ता किल तर्हि त्वं तस्य धनान्याहरेति त्वया प्रेरितः सन्नुरुक्रमो भूत्वा विश्वेत्ता यानि च तत्र स्थितानि तानि सर्वण्याभरत् । अभरत् । कानि तानीति । शतं महिषानपरि- मितान् प्रशस्तान् पदार्थान् तेषां वाहनरूपान् महिषान्वा क्षीरपाकमोदनं च पक्वमात्रमेवादनं चाभरत् ॥ (*sa kiṃ punarmuṣitavāniti tadatrocyate | viśvettā iti | he iṃdra tveṣitastvayā prerito viṣṇuryajñarūpī tveṣitastvaṃ durgādāhartā kila tarhi tvaṃ tasya dhanānyāhareti tvayā preritaḥ sannurukramo bhūtvā viśvettā yāni ca tatra sthitāni tāni sarvaṇyābharat | abharat | kāni tānīti | śataṃ mahiṣānapari- mitān praśastān padārthān teṣāṃ vāhanarūpān mahiṣānvā kṣīrapākamodanaṃ ca pakvamātramevādanaṃ cābharat ‖*)

### Printed p. 183
It was said that Viṣṇu again brought away many other things as well. The detail of this is stated clearly in the present Ṛk विश्वेत्ता (*viśvettā*). O Indra, you said to Viṣṇu, who is of the form of sacrifice: "O Viṣṇu, you have declared yourself to be one who brings the wealth and other things of the Asuras from their strongholds. Then bring the wealth and other things that are with this Asura Varāha." As soon as he was thus urged by you (by Indra), Viṣṇu agreed, "So be it," went swiftly to the Asura's city, and brought away all the wealth and other things that had been in the Asura, that is, whatever things he had been told to bring. And what were the things Viṣṇu brought? The best and countless excellent things, wealth and the like; the buffaloes and other animals that carry them; and food and other fare cooked with milk and the like. He brought away everything as Indra had said. This detail is clearly indicated in the present Ṛk.

विध्यद्वराहमित्यत्रोक्तोऽर्थश्च चरमपादेनोच्यते । इंद्रस्तु वराहारं स्वीकृतासुरसर्वस्वं वराहरूपिणं वैमुषमेमुषनामानमथ वैमुषं धनानामाशकं वराहमसुगं हृदयेऽविध्यदिति शेषः ॥ (*vidhyadvarāhamityatrokto 'rthaśca caramapādenocyate | iṃdrastu varāhāraṃ svīkṛtāsurasarvasvaṃ varāharūpiṇaṃ vaimuṣamemuṣanāmānamatha vaimuṣaṃ dhanānāmāśakaṃ varāhamasugaṃ hṛdaye 'vidhyaditi śeṣaḥ ‖*)

Now the meaning of the last pāda of the present Ṛk, विध्यद्वराहमिंद्र एमुषं (*vidhyadvarāhamiṃdra emuṣaṃ*), is explained. This Asura called Varāha was of the form of a boar (varāha) and had stolen a great deal of wealth from many people and kept it. It is therefore customary to call him by the name एमुषं (*emuṣaṃ*), that is, धनानामामोषकं (*dhanānāmāmoṣakaṃ*), "one who steals wealth". The sense developed in the last pāda is that Indra, by his power and strength, pierced the chest of this Asura Eṣumuṣa, who was of the form of a boar, and killed him.

The explanation of this matter is given in the same way in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā of the Kṛṣṇa Yajurveda.

यज्ञो देवेभ्यो निलायुत विष्णू रूपं कृत्वा स पृथिवीं प्राविशत्तं देवा हस्तान्त्सग्ं रभ्यैच्छन्तमिंद्र उपर्युपर्यत्यक्रामत्सोऽब्रवीत्कोमायमुपर्युपर्यत्यक्रमादित्यहं दुर्गॆ हंतेत्यथ कस्यमित्यहं दुर्गादाहर्तेति सोऽब्रवीद्दुर्गे वै हंतावोचथावराहोऽयं नाममोषः सप्तानां गिरीणां परस्ताद्वित्तं वेद्यमसुराणां बिभर्ति तं जहि यदि दुर्गॆ हंतासीति स दर्भपुंजीलमुदृह्य सप्तगिरीन् भित्वा तमहन्सोऽब्रविद्दुर्गाद्वा आहर्ता वोचथा एतमाहरेति तमेभ्यो यज्ञ एव यज्ञमाहरद्यत्तद्वित्तं वेद्यमसुराणामविंदंत तदेकं वेद्यै वेदित्वं ॥ (*yajño devebhyo nilāyuta viṣṇū rūpaṃ kṛtvā sa pṛthivīṃ prāviśattaṃ devā hastāntsagṃ rabhyaicchantamiṃdra uparyuparyatyakrāmatso 'bravītkomāyamuparyuparyatyakramādityahaṃ durgè haṃtetyatha kasyamityahaṃ durgādāharteti so 'bravīddurge vai haṃtāvocathāvarāho 'yaṃ nāmamoṣaḥ saptānāṃ girīṇāṃ parastādvittaṃ vedyamasurāṇāṃ bibharti taṃ jahi yadi durgè haṃtāsīti sa darbhapuṃjīlamudṛhya saptagirīn bhitvā tamahanso 'braviddurgādvā āhartā vocathā etamāhareti tamebhyo yajña eva yajñamāharadyattadvittaṃ vedyamasurāṇāmaviṃdaṃta tadekaṃ vedyai veditvaṃ ‖*)

### Printed p. 184
(Tai. Saṃ. 6-2-4-2, 3)

To explain how the sacrificial altar came to be called वेदि (*vedi*), a story is told in the Yajurveda.

In ancient times, for some reason, the sacrifice took the form of Viṣṇu, left the gods and went away. Then that Viṣṇu, in the form of the sacrifice, entered the earth and hid himself so that no one knew. The gods, not seeing him, searched everywhere, holding one another's hands. Yet they did not find the place where Viṣṇu was. But when Indra passed over the spot where Viṣṇu was hidden, Viṣṇu below asked, "Who is that? Who is it that passes over this place where I am?" Hearing this, Indra said, "I am the hero who destroys the strongholds of the Asuras. But who are you?" Viṣṇu replied, "I am the one who brings the wealth and other things that are there after the strongholds of the Asuras have been destroyed." And he said to Indra: "O man, if you are the destroyer of enemy cities as you claim, then beyond seven mountains from here lives an Asura called Varāha. He has stolen the wealth of the Asuras and keeps it within his strong fortress. If you have the power you claim to destroy the strongholds of the Asuras, strike that Asura Varāha and destroy him. Only then will your power be known." Hearing this, Indra took a bunch of darbha grass that lay nearby, made it his weapon, split the seven unbreakable mountains, and struck down that Asura Varāha. Then he said to Viṣṇu: "O man, as you said, I have split the seven mountains and killed the Asuras there. My power and strength have thus become plain. Now, as you said before, bring the wealth and other things from the destroyed strongholds of the Asuras; then your power will be shown." Viṣṇu agreed, "So be it," and, in the very form of the sacrifice, took away the wealth and other things that were with that Asura and brought them to the gods. Because the gods thus obtained the Asuras' wealth through the sacrifice, the place where the sacrifice is performed came to be called वेदि (*vedi*). This is the main point of the story.

**Indra's slaying of one named Viśvarūpa**

Indra's slaying of one named Viśvarūpa is told at the beginning of the fifth prapāṭhaka in the second kāṇḍa of the Taittirīya Saṃhitā. In the Ṛgveda too, on this matter, in the Ṛk

भूरीदिंद्र उदिनक्षंतमोजोऽवाभिनत्सत्पतिर्मन्यमानं । त्वाष्ट्रस्य चिद्विश्वरूपस्य गोनामाचक्राणस्त्रीणि शीर्षा परा वर्क् ॥ (*bhūrīdiṃdra udinakṣaṃtamojo 'vābhinatsatpatirmanyamānaṃ | tvāṣṭrasya cidviśvarūpasya gonāmācakrāṇastrīṇi śīrṣā parā vark ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-8-9)

### Printed p. 185
it is said that Indra cut off the three heads of Viśvarūpa, the son of Tvaṣṭṛ. In the 7th Ṛk of the same sūkta it is said that Indra did this deed for Trita, and that Trita helped Indra in it.

अस्य त्रितः क्रतुना वव्रे अंतरिच्छन् धीतिं पितुरेवैः परस्य । सचस्यमानः पित्रोरुपस्थे जामि ब्रुवाण आयुधानि वेति । स पित्राण्यायुधानि विद्वानिंद्रॆषित आप्त्यो अभ्ययुध्यत् । त्रिशीर्षाणं सप्तरश्मिं जघन्वान् त्वाष्ट्रस्यचिन्निः सस्यजे त्रितो गाः ॥ (*asya tritaḥ kratunā vavre aṃtaricchan dhītiṃ piturevaiḥ parasya | sacasyamānaḥ pitrorupasthe jāmi bruvāṇa āyudhāni veti | sa pitrāṇyāyudhāni vidvāniṃdrèṣita āptyo abhyayudhyat | triśīrṣāṇaṃ saptaraśmiṃ jaghanvān tvāṣṭrasyacinniḥ sasyaje trito gāḥ ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-8-7, 8)

In the commentary on these Ṛks Sāyaṇa also tells a story. Indra asked for Trita's help in killing Viśvarūpa, the son of Tvaṣṭṛ, who had three heads. Trita, to increase his own strength, asked as a boon that he be given a share of the offering in sacrifices. Indra agreed and gave him water for washing the hands and a share in the sacrifice. Then Trita, equipped with the weapons that belonged to his father and urged on by Indra, fought Viśvarūpa, the son of Tvaṣṭṛ, struck him hard with his weapons and made him faint. After that Indra cut off the three heads of that Viśvarūpa. This is what the above Ṛks say. The same matter is told at length in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā (Tai. Saṃ. 2-5-1-1). That too is explained here with its meaning:

विश्वरूपो वै त्वाष्ट्रः पुरोहितो देवानामासीत् स्वस्रियोऽसुराणां तस्य त्रीणि शीर्षाण्यासन्सोमपानग्ं सुरापानमन्नादनग्ं स प्रत्यक्षं देवेभ्यो भागमवदत् परोक्षमसुरेभ्यः ॥ (*viśvarūpo vai tvāṣṭraḥ purohito devānāmāsīt svasriyo 'surāṇāṃ tasya trīṇi śīrṣāṇyāsansomapānagṃ surāpānamannādanagṃ sa pratyakṣaṃ devebhyo bhāgamavadat parokṣamasurebhyaḥ ‖*)

In former times Viśvarūpa, the son of Tvaṣṭṛ, was the priest of the gods. Because he was the son of the Asuras' sister, he also felt attachment to the Asuras. This Viśvarūpa had three heads. With one of the three he drank Soma, with another he drank surā (liquor), and with the third he ate food and other fare. Meanwhile this Viśvarūpa declared openly, before everyone, that he would secure the share of the offering for the gods in the sacrifice. But secretly, within, without anyone knowing, he was helping the Asuras.

सर्वस्मै वै प्रत्यक्षं भागं वदंति यस्मा एव परोक्षं वदंति तस्य भाग उदितस्तस्मादिंद्रोऽबिभेदीदृजै राष्ट्रं विपर्यावर्तयतीति तस्य वज्रमादाय शीर्षाण्यच्चिनत् ॥ (*sarvasmai vai pratyakṣaṃ bhāgaṃ vadaṃti yasmā eva parokṣaṃ vadaṃti tasya bhāga uditastasmādiṃdro 'bibhedīdṛjai rāṣṭraṃ viparyāvartayatīti tasya vajramādāya śīrṣāṇyaccinat ‖*)

### Printed p. 186
People promise everyone openly, before all, that they will give a share. But those who fulfil the promise they have given are very rare. If a promise to give a share is made secretly (in private), in such a way as to inspire trust, they will certainly keep that word. Therefore Indra, seeing Viśvarūpa's secret dealing, was afraid. Fearing that this man would betray him and put the kingdom into the hands of others (the Asuras), he took his thunderbolt and cut off all three heads of Viśvarūpa.

यत्सोमपानमासीत्स कपिंजलोऽभवद्यत्सुरापानग्ं स कलविंको यदन्नादनग्ं स तित्तिरिस्तस्यांजलिना ब्रह्महत्यामुपागृह्णात्ताग्ं संवत्सरमुबिभस्तं भूतान्यभ्यक्रोशन् ब्रह्महन्निति ॥ (*yatsomapānamāsītsa kapiṃjalo 'bhavadyatsurāpānagṃ sa kalaviṃko yadannādanagṃ sa tittiristasyāṃjalinā brahmahatyāmupāgṛhṇāttāgṃ saṃvatsaramubibhastaṃ bhūtānyabhyakrośan brahmahanniti ‖*)

Of the three heads that Indra cut off, the head that had drunk Soma became a bird called kapiñjala (hazecock). The second head, which had drunk surā, became a kalaviṅka, that is, a sparrow. The third head, which had eaten food, became a bird called tittiri (partridge). Then, since Indra had killed Viśvarūpa, who was a Brāhmaṇa, the guilt of brahmin-slaying fell on him, and he had to take it up with his own hands. Accordingly he bore the guilt of brahmin-slaying for a full year. All the creatures, seeing him (Indra), cried out, "O Indra, you are a killer of a Brāhmaṇa."

स पृथिवी मुपासीददस्यै ब्रह्महत्यायै तृतीयं प्रतिगृहाणेति साब्रवीद्वरं वृणै खातात्पराभविष्यंती मन्ये ततो मा पराभूवमिति पुरा ते संवत्सरादसिरोहादित्यब्रवीत्तस्मात्पुरा संवत्सरात्पृथिव्यै खातमपिरोहति वारेवृतग्न् ह्यस्यै तृतीयं ब्रह्महत्यायै प्रत्यगृह्णात्तत्स्वकृतमिरिणमभवत्तस्मादाहिताग्निः श्रद्धादेवः स्वकृत इरिणे नावस्येद्ब्रह्महत्यायै हैष वर्णः ॥ (*sa pṛthivī mupāsīdadasyai brahmahatyāyai tṛtīyaṃ pratigṛhāṇeti sābravīdvaraṃ vṛṇai khātātparābhaviṣyaṃtī manye tato mā parābhūvamiti purā te saṃvatsarādasirohādityabravīttasmātpurā saṃvatsarātpṛthivyai khātamapirohati vārevṛtagn hyasyai tṛtīyaṃ brahmahatyāyai pratyagṛhṇāttatsvakṛtamiriṇamabhavattasmādāhitāgniḥ śraddhādevaḥ svakṛta iriṇe nāvasyedbrahmahatyāyai haiṣa varṇaḥ ‖*)

Seeing this, Indra (ashamed) went to the Earth to be rid of the guilt of brahmin-slaying and said, "O Earth, take one third of this brahmin-slaying that has come upon me." The Earth replied, "Then I will ask a boon. People dig the earth and make pits, and I am dishonoured by this. Give me a boon that this may not be so." Indra said, "So be it: let a pit thus dug close up of itself within a year, without any effort, and let the earth become level as before," and gave the boon. Therefore a pit or the like dug in the earth closes up of itself before a year has passed.

### Printed p. 187
This is the boon the Earth asked for. Having obtained such a boon, the Earth took one third of Indra's guilt of brahmin-slaying. From that time it became natural to the Earth. Therefore a man who has set up the sacred fires or performed sacrifices and who has faith and devotion towards the gods must not choose a part of the earth that has been dug into a pit as the sacrificial altar for a sacrifice. For such a dug pit is the mark (varṇa) of the brahmin-slaying that the Earth took upon herself.

स वनस्पतीनुपासीददस्यै ब्रह्महत्यायै तृतीयं पतिगृह्णितेति तेऽब्रुवन्वरं वृणामहै वृक्षात्परा भविष्यंतो मन्यामहे ततो मा पराभूमेत्याव्रश्चनाद्वो भूयाग्‌ंस उत्तिष्ठानित्यब्रवीत्तस्मादाव्रश्चनाद्वृष्णाणां भूयाग्ंस उत्तिष्ठंति नारेवृतग्स् हैषां तृतीयं ब्रह्महत्यायै प्रत्यगृह्णन्स् निर्यासोऽभवत्तस्मान्निर्यासस्य नाश्यं ब्रह्महत्यायै ह्येषवर्णोऽथो खलु य एव लोहितो यो वाव्रश्चनान्निर्येषति तस्यनाश्यं काममन्यस्य ॥ (*sa vanaspatīnupāsīdadasyai brahmahatyāyai tṛtīyaṃ patigṛhṇiteti te 'bruvanvaraṃ vṛṇāmahai vṛkṣātparā bhaviṣyaṃto manyāmahe tato mā parābhūmetyāvraścanādvo bhūyāgṃsa uttiṣṭhānityabravīttasmādāvraścanādvṛṣṇāṇāṃ bhūyāgṃsa uttiṣṭhaṃti nārevṛtags haiṣāṃ tṛtīyaṃ brahmahatyāyai pratyagṛhṇans niryāso 'bhavattasmānniryāsasya nāśyaṃ brahmahatyāyai hyeṣavarṇo 'tho khalu ya eva lohito yo vāvraścanānniryeṣati tasyanāśyaṃ kāmamanyasya ‖*)

Then Indra went near the trees and said, "O trees, take one third of my guilt of brahmin-slaying." The trees replied, "Then we shall desire a boon. People cut the branches and so on of trees, and we are overcome by it. Give us a boon that this may not be so." Indra said, "Very well: when they cut, let more shoots sprout all around the cut place," and gave the boon. Therefore, as the trees asked, when branches are cut, more shoots sprout at that place. Then the trees, as they had said, accepted one third of Indra's brahmin-slaying. The sap that comes out when a tree is cut is the mark of this brahmin-slaying. Therefore no one should drink the sap (liquor) that flows out when a tree is cut. For it is the colour of brahmin-slaying; or, if the sap that flows from the cutting of the tree is red, it should not be drunk; if it is not so, it may be drunk if one wishes.

स स्त्रीषग्ंसादमुपासीददस्यै ब्रह्मत्यायै तृतीयं प्रतिगृह्णीतेति ता अब्रुवन्वरं मृणामहा ऋत्वियात् प्रजां विंदामहै काममाविजनितोः संभवामेति तस्मादृत्वियात् स्त्रियः प्रजां विंदंते काममा विजनितोः संभवंति वारेवृतग्गं हासां तृतीयं ब्रह्महत्यायै प्रत्यगृह्णन्ता मलवद्वासा अभवत् ॥ (*sa strīṣagṃsādamupāsīdadasyai brahmatyāyai tṛtīyaṃ pratigṛhṇīteti tā abruvanvaraṃ mṛṇāmahā ṛtviyāt prajāṃ viṃdāmahai kāmamāvijanitoḥ saṃbhavāmeti tasmādṛtviyāt striyaḥ prajāṃ viṃdaṃte kāmamā vijanitoḥ saṃbhavaṃti vārevṛtaggaṃ hāsāṃ tṛtīyaṃ brahmahatyāyai pratyagṛhṇantā malavadvāsā abhavat ‖*)

Then Indra went to the company of women and said to them, "O women, take the remaining third part of my guilt of brahmin-slaying." The women replied, "Then we

### Printed p. 188
they asked for one boon: "After we have become impure (after the ritual bath following the period), children should be born to us, and until delivery (childbirth) we should obtain satisfaction of desire through intercourse wherever we wish." Indra said, "So be it." Therefore women obtain offspring after their period, and until delivery they have intercourse whenever they wish, for this is the boon they asked for. Then those women took upon themselves one third of Indra's guilt of Brahmin-slaying. The menstrual discharge that flows when women are in their period is the mark of this Brahmin-slaying. During the time the discharge flows (when they are in their period) women are impure (unclean).

तस्मान्मलवद्वाससा न संवदेत न सहासीत नास्या अन्नमद्याद्ब्रह्महत्यायै हैषा वर्णं प्रतिमुच्यास्तेऽथो खल्वाहुरभ्यंजनं वाव स्त्रिया अन्नमभ्यंजनमेव न प्रतिगृह्यं काममन्यदिति ॥ (*tasmānmalavadvāsasā na saṃvadeta na sahāsīta nāsyā annamadyādbrahmahatyāyai haiṣā varṇaṃ pratimucyāste 'tho khalvāhurabhyaṃjanaṃ vāva striyā annamabhyaṃjanameva na pratigṛhyaṃ kāmamanyaditi ‖*)

Therefore one should not speak with a woman in her period. One should not eat food given or touched by her. For she is discharging the unclean menstrual flow, which is the mark of the guilt of Brahmin-slaying. During the time of the period one should not apply oil and the like, or else no one should accept food and the like from her (food she has touched) during that time.

यां मलववद्वाससग्ं संभवंति यस्ततो जायते सोऽभिशस्तो यामरण्ये तस्यै स्तेनो यां पराचीं तस्यै ह्रीतमुख्यपगल्भो या स्नाति तस्या अप्पु मारुको याभ्यंक्ते तस्यै दुश्चर्मा या प्रलिखते तस्यॆ खलतिरपमारी यांक्ते तस्यॆ काणो या दतो धावते तस्यै श्यावदन् या नखानि निकृंतते तस्यै कुनखी या कृणत्ति तस्यै क्लीबो या रज्जुग्‌ंसृजति तस्या उद्बंधुको या पर्णॆन पिबति तस्या उन्मादुको या खर्वॆण पिबति तस्यै खर्वस्तिस्रो रात्रिर्वृतं चरेदंजलिना वा पिबेदखर्वॆण ना पात्रेण प्रजाय्कॆ गोपीथाय ॥ (*yāṃ malavavadvāsasagṃ saṃbhavaṃti yastato jāyate so 'bhiśasto yāmaraṇye tasyai steno yāṃ parācīṃ tasyai hrītamukhyapagalbho yā snāti tasyā appu māruko yābhyaṃkte tasyai duścarmā yā pralikhate tasyè khalatirapamārī yāṃkte tasyè kāṇo yā dato dhāvate tasyai śyāvadan yā nakhāni nikṛṃtate tasyai kunakhī yā kṛṇatti tasyai klībo yā rajjugṃsṛjati tasyā udbaṃdhuko yā parṇèna pibati tasyā unmāduko yā kharvèṇa pibati tasyai kharvastisro rātrirvṛtaṃ caredaṃjalinā vā pibedakharvèṇa nā pātreṇa prajāykè gopīthāya ‖*) (Tai. Saṃ. 2-5-1-1 to 7)

If one has intercourse with a woman in her period (while she is menstruating), the son born of it will be cursed. If a woman in her period has intercourse in a forest, the son born to her will be a thief. If she has intercourse with her face covered or turned away, the son born will be a bashful man who from shame cannot speak properly with anyone. The son of a woman who bathes during her period will die by drowning in water. The son of a woman who anoints herself with oil will be afflicted with a skin disease (leprosy, white patches). The son born to a woman who combs her hair will be bald, without hair on his head, and powerless. The son born to a woman who applies collyrium to her eyes will be blind. The son born to a woman who cleans her teeth at that time

### Printed p. 189
will have foul teeth. The son born to a woman who cuts her nails will have ugly, ill-formed nails. The son born to a woman who plaits (mats and the like) at that time will be impotent. The son born to a woman who twists rope will die by suicide. A woman who drinks water from leaves (lotus leaves and the like) will have a son who is deficient in limbs or deformed in limb. Therefore, while women are in their period, they should observe the vow very strictly until three nights have passed. For the obtaining of good offspring, women should at that time drink water only from the cupped palms or from a good, unbroken vessel.

In the matters stated above are told Indra's slaying of Viśvarūpa, the guilt of Brahmin-slaying that came to Indra from that slaying, the acts Indra performed to remove that guilt, and, as a result, the rule that women should observe restraint while in their period. In the present context only the account of Viśvarūpa is what is to be taken, yet other matters connected with it and belonging to the same anuvāka are also mentioned here. For in the Purāṇas and elsewhere it is said that the guilt of Brahmin-slaying came to Indra through the slaying of Vṛtra and that Indra made the efforts described above to remove it; so this explanation became necessary in order to show the difference between the details of the Veda and those of the Purāṇas and the like.

**Indra Vaikuṇṭha**

About the seer Saptagu, the seer of the forty-seventh sūkta beginning जगृभ्मा ते (*jagṛbhmā te*), and about Indra Vaikuṇṭha, the seer of the following sūktas अहं भुवं (*ahaṃ bhuvaṃ*) (48) and अहं दां (*ahaṃ dāṃ*) (49), the work called Bṛhaddevatā says as follows:

प्राजापत्यासुरी त्वासीद्वि कुंठा नाम नामतः । सेच्छंतींद्रसमं पुत्रं तेपेऽथ सुमहत्तपः ॥ सा प्रजापतितः कामांल्लेभेऽथ विविधान्वरान् । तस्यां चेंद्रः स्वयं जज्ञॆ जिघांसुर्दैत्यदानवान् ॥ (*prājāpatyāsurī tvāsīdvi kuṃṭhā nāma nāmataḥ | secchaṃtīṃdrasamaṃ putraṃ tepe 'tha sumahattapaḥ ‖ sā prajāpatitaḥ kāmāṃllebhe 'tha vividhānvarān | tasyāṃ ceṃdraḥ svayaṃ jajñè jighāṃsurdaityadānavān ‖*)

Formerly there was an Asura woman, a daughter of Prajāpati. Her name was Vikuṇṭhā. At one time a desire arose in her that a son equal to Indra in valour, prowess and the like should be born in her. So she performed very fearsome austerity and obtained from Prajāpati the boons she wanted. Indra, wishing to destroy the Daityas and Dānavas, was born in that very woman Vikuṇṭhā as her son.
