एकदा दानवैः सार्धं समरे समसज्यत । जघान तेषां नवतीर्नव सप्त च सप्तकान् ॥ (*ekadā dānavaiḥ sārdhaṃ samare samasajyata | jaghāna teṣāṃ navatīrnava sapta ca saptakān ‖*)

### Printed p. 190
भित्वा स्वबाहुनीर्येण हैमरौप्यायसीः पुरीः । हत्वा सर्वान्यथास्थानं पृथिव्यादिव्यवस्थितान् ॥ पृथिव्यां कालकेयांश्च पौलोमांश्चैव धन्विनः । तांश्च व्युत्सादयामास प्रह्लादतनयान्दिवि ॥ (*bhitvā svabāhunīryeṇa haimaraupyāyasīḥ purīḥ | hatvā sarvānyathāsthānaṃ pṛthivyādivyavasthitān ‖ pṛthivyāṃ kālakeyāṃśca paulomāṃścaiva dhanvinaḥ | tāṃśca vyutsādayāmāsa prahlādatanayāndivi ‖*)

On one occasion, while Indra was fighting the Dānavas, he destroyed ninety-nine and seventy-seven of their chiefs. They lived on the earth, in the atmosphere and in heaven, having built strong forts of gold, silver and iron. By his own strength Indra destroyed all of these, and destroyed the Kālakeyas on the earth, the Pulomas' line, the Asuras of Prahlāda's line, and others.

राज्यं प्राप्य स दैत्येषु स्वेन वीर्येण दर्पितः । देवान्बाधितुमारेभे मोहितोऽसुरमायया ॥ बाध्यमानास्तु तेनापि असुरेणामिंतौजसा । उपाधावन्नृषिश्रेष्ठं तत्प्रबोधाय सप्तगुं ॥ ऋषिस्तु सप्तगुर्नाम तस्यासीत्सुप्रियः सखा । स चैनमभितुष्टाव जगृभ्येति करे स्पृशन् ॥ (*rājyaṃ prāpya sa daityeṣu svena vīryeṇa darpitaḥ | devānbādhitumārebhe mohito 'suramāyayā ‖ bādhyamānāstu tenāpi asureṇāmiṃtaujasā | upādhāvannṛṣiśreṣṭhaṃ tatprabodhāya saptaguṃ ‖ ṛṣistu saptagurnāma tasyāsītsupriyaḥ sakhā | sa cainamabhituṣṭāva jagṛbhyeti kare spṛśan ‖*)

Having thus destroyed the Daityas, Indra, the son of Vikuṇṭhā, because he was born of an Asura woman, was under the sway of delusion; having won sovereignty by his own might and strength, he forgot himself, became arrogant, and began to oppress the gods. While Indra, in Daitya form, swollen with his boundless prowess, was oppressing the gods, they could not bear his oppression and came to the seer Saptagu and took refuge with him. That seer was Indra's dearest friend. Then the seer Saptagu took Indra's hand in his own and praised Indra with the sūkta जगृभ्मा ते (*jagṛbhmā te*) (sūkta 47).

ततः स बुध्वा चात्मानं सप्तगुस्तुतिहर्षितः । आत्मानमेव तुष्टाव अहं भुवमिति त्रिभिः ॥ कीर्तयन् स्वानि कर्माणि यानि स्म कृतवान्पुरा । यथाकरोच्च वैदेहं व्यंसं सोमपतिं नृपं ॥ वसिष्ठशापादभवद्वैदोहो नृपतिः पुरा । इंद्रप्रसादादेजे च सत्त्रैः सारस्वतादिभिः ॥ (*tataḥ sa budhvā cātmānaṃ saptagustutiharṣitaḥ | ātmānameva tuṣṭāva ahaṃ bhuvamiti tribhiḥ ‖ kīrtayan svāni karmāṇi yāni sma kṛtavānpurā | yathākarocca vaidehaṃ vyaṃsaṃ somapatiṃ nṛpaṃ ‖ vasiṣṭhaśāpādabhavadvaidoho nṛpatiḥ purā | iṃdraprasādādeje ca sattraiḥ sārasvatādibhiḥ ‖*)

### Printed p. 191
प्रभूतां शक्तिमत्तां च शत्रूणामप्यपाक्रियां । नृषु सर्वेषु चैश्वर्यं प्रभुत्वं भुवनेषु च ॥ (*prabhūtāṃ śaktimattāṃ ca śatrūṇāmapyapākriyāṃ | nṛṣu sarveṣu caiśvaryaṃ prabhutvaṃ bhuvaneṣu ca ‖*)

Indra Vaikuṇṭha, praised by the seer Saptagu, recalled his former existence and became aware of who he was. Then that Vaikuṇṭha Indra glorified himself for the many deeds of valour he had done before, in the three sūktas (48–50) beginning अहं भुवं (*ahaṃ bhuvaṃ*). It is also stated how the king of Videha, Vyaṃsa, was freed from a curse by Vasiṣṭha, and how that king, by Indra's grace, performed a sattra sacrifice on the bank of the river Sarasvatī, conquered his enemies and established universal sovereignty.

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अहं दामित्येकादशर्चं सप्तमं सूक्तं । वैकुंठ एवर्षिदेवता च ।
> द्वितीयैकादश्यौ त्रिष्टुभौ । शिष्टा जगत्यः । अनुक्रांतं च । अहं दामंत्योपाद्ये
> चेति ॥ द्वितीयै रात्रिपर्याये मैत्रावरुणस्येदं सूक्तं । सूत्रितं च। अहं दां
> पाता सुतमिंद्रो अस्तु सोमं । आ-६-४ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta अहं दां (*ahaṃ dāṃ*) has eleven ṛks and is the seventh sūkta. Vaikuṇṭha is the seer, and also the deity. The second and the eleventh ṛks are in Triṣṭubh; the rest are in Jagatī. The Anukramaṇī says: अहं दामंत्योपाद्ये च (*ahaṃ dāmaṃtyopādye ca*). In the second night-service this sūkta belongs to the Maitrāvaruṇa. It is also prescribed in the sūtra: अहं दां पाता सुतमिंद्रो अस्तु सोमं (*ahaṃ dāṃ pātā sutamiṃdro astu somaṃ*) (Ā. 6-4).

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta अहं दां (*ahaṃ dāṃ*) is the seventh sūkta in the fourth anuvāka. It has eleven ṛks. Indra Vaikuṇṭha himself is the seer and also the deity. The second and eleventh ṛks of this sūkta are in the Triṣṭup metre; the remaining ṛks are in the Jagatī metre. In the Anukramaṇī it is said: अहं दामंत्योपाद्ये च (*ahaṃ dāmaṃtyopādye ca*). The application of this sūkta is for the śastra mantras recited by the priest called Maitrāvaruṇa in the second night-service; this is set forth in Āśvalāyana's Śrauta Sūtra in the sūtra अहं दां पाता सुतमिंद्रो अस्तु सोमं (*ahaṃ dāṃ pātā sutamiṃdro astu somaṃ*) (Ā. 6-4).

- Sūkta—48
- Maṇḍala—10; Anuvāka—4; Sūkta—49
- Aṣṭaka—8; Adhyāya—1; Varga—7, 8
- Number of ṛks in the sūkta—11
- Ṛṣi—Indra Vaikuṇṭha
- Devatā—Indra Vaikuṇṭha
- Chandas—1, 3–10 Jagatī; 2, 11 Triṣṭup

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒हं दां॑ गृण॒ते पूर्व्यं॒ वस्व॒हं ब्रह्म॑ कृणवं॒ मह्यं॒ वर्ध॑नं।

### Printed p. 192

> अहं भुवं यजमानस्य चोदितायज्वनः साक्षि विश्मस्मिन्भरे ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अहं । दां । गृणते । पूर्व्यं । वसु । अहं । ब्रह्म । कृणवं । मह्यं । वर्धनं ।  
> अहं । भुवं । यजमानस्य । चोदिता । आयज्जनः । साक्षि । विश्वस्मिन् । भरे ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अहमिंद्रो गृणते स्तुवते पूर्व्यं मुख्यं वसु धनं दां । अदां ।
> अहमेव ब्रह्म परिवृढं कर्म स्तुतिलक्षणं मह्यं वर्धनं कृणवं । करोमि ।
> स्तोतुः संबंधि स्तोत्रं तस्मै धनं प्रयच्छन् महम्येव वर्धनं करोमीत्यर्थः ।
> तथाहं याजमानस्य मामुद्दिश्य यष्टुर्धनस्य चोदिता प्रेरको भुवं । भवामि ।
> अयज्जनोऽयष्टुः । अयष्टारमित्यर्थः । तं विश्वस्मिन्भरे सर्वस्मिन्नपि संग्रामे
> साक्षि । अभिभवामि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

I, Indra, give to the one who praises me the foremost wealth. I myself make the sacred prayer (the action marked by praise), the speaker's hymn, a source of increase for myself: giving wealth to him, I make myself grow. Likewise I become the impeller of the wealth of the sacrificer who sacrifices to me. As for the one who does not sacrifice, I overcome him in every battle.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I, Indra
- गृणते (*gṛṇate*)—to the man who praises me
- पूर्व्यं (*pūrvyaṃ*)—abundant
- वसु (*vasu*)—wealth
- दां (*dāṃ*)—I give
- अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I
- ब्रह्म (*brahma*)—the sacrificial and other rite that praises me
- मह्यं वर्धगः (*mahyaṃ vardhagaḥ*)—so as to bring me increase
- कृणवं (*kṛṇavaṃ*)—I make
- अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I
- यजमानस्य (*yajamānasya*)—of the wealth of the sacrificer who performs a sacrifice addressed to me
- चोदिता (*coditā*)—as impeller
- भुवं (*bhuvaṃ*)—I have become
- आयज्जनः (*āyajjanaḥ*)—to one who does not worship me
- विश्वस्मिन् भरे (*viśvasmin bhare*)—in all battles
- साक्षि (*sākṣi*)—I bring defeat

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

I, Indra, give very excellent or abundant wealth to the man who praises me. I make the sacrificial and other rite that praises me bring me increase, that is, be praiseworthy. I impel the wealth of the sacrificer who sacrifices to me so that it increases. To the man who does not worship me I bring defeat in all battles.

##### English translation
I give excellent treasure to him who praise me, I render sacred prayer (a source) of greatness to myself, I am the instigator of the

### Printed p. 193
worshipper : I am the discomfiter in every battle of him who does not worship

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> मां धु॒रिंद्रं॒ नाम॑ दे॒वता॑ दि॒वश्च॒ ग्मश्चा॒पां च॑ जं॒तवः॑ ।  
> अ॒हं हरी॒ वृष॑णा॒ विव्र॑ता र॒घू अ॒हं वज्रं॒शव॑से धृ॒ष्ट्वा द॑दे॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> मां । धुः । इंद्रं । नाम। देवता । दिवः । च । गृः । च । अपां । च । जंतवः ॥  
> अहं । हरी इति । वृषणा । विव्रता । रघू इति । अहं । वज्रं । शवसे । धृष्णु । आ ।  
> ददे ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इंद्रं नामंद्रनामानं मा देवता देवानां मध्ये दिवो द्युलोकस्य ।
> ग्मा पृथिव्यापोऽंतरिक्षं । एतेषु लोकेषु जंतवो जाता मा मामेव धुः ।
> दधुः । धारयंति । हविर्भिः स्तुतिभिश्च । अहं यज्ञगमनाय संग्रामगमनाय वा
> हरी हरितवर्णौ वृषणा पुंस्तोपेत् विव्रता विविधकर्माण् रघू लघू शीघ्रगती
> अश्वा नियोजयामि रथे । तथाहं धृष्णु धर्षकं वज्रमायुधं शवसे बलाया
> ददे । स्वीकरोमि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Among the gods, the creatures born in heaven, on earth and in the waters (the atmosphere), all hold me alone, the one named Indra, upholding me with oblations and praises. For going to the sacrifice or to battle, I yoke to my chariot two horses: green-hued, virile, of manifold works, swift. Likewise I take up the daring vajra, my weapon, for strength.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्रं नाम (*iṃdraṃ nāma*)—by the name Indra
- मां (*māṃ*)—me
- देवता (*devatā*)—among the gods, whoever
- दिवः च (*divaḥ ca*)—dwellers in heaven
- गृः च (*gṛḥ ca*)—and those on earth
- अपां (*apāṃ*)—and those who move in the atmosphere
- जंतवः (*jaṃtavaḥ*)—who have been born, all of them
- मां (*māṃ*)—me alone
- धुः (*dhuḥ*)—hold and nourish with oblations and the like and with praises
- अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I, when I go to the sacrifice or to battle
- हरी (*harī*)—green-hued
- वृषणा (*vṛṣaṇā*)—youthful
- विव्रता (*vivratā*)—famed for manifold works
- रघू (*raghū*)—swift-moving: my two horses I yoke to the chariot; likewise
- अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I
- धृष्णु (*dhṛṣṇu*)—daring
- वज्रं (*vajraṃ*)—the vajra weapon
- शवसे (*śavase*)—for the exercise of strength in battle
- आ ददे (*ā dade*)—I take up

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Among the gods, whoever have been born in the world of heaven, and likewise whatever gods there are on earth and in the atmosphere, all of them hold me with oblations and the like and with praises

### Printed p. 194
(The Bhāvārtha of the previous Ṛk continues:) They worship me with oblations and hymns and nourish me. I yoke to my chariot my two horses, which are green-hued, youthful, famed for their many deeds, and swift; and I take up my thunderbolt to conquer the enemies in battle by force.

##### English translation
Those born in heaven, in earth, aad in the firmament, support me, whose name is Indra amongst the deities (by their offerings). I (harness to my car) two bay steeds vigorous, many functioned, rapid : I wield for my strength the fearful thunder-bolt.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒हमत्कं॑ क॒वये॑ शिश्नथं॒ हथै॑र॒हं कुत्स॑मावमा॒भिरू॒तिभिः॑ ।  
> अ॒हं शुष्ण॑स्य॒ श्नथि॑ता॒ वध॑र्यमं॒ न यो र॒र आर्यं॒ नाम॒  ॑
> दस्यवे ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अहं । अत्कं । कवये । शिश्नथं । हथैः । अहं । कुत्सं । आवं । अभिः । ऊतिभिः ॥  
> अहं । शुष्णस्य । शृथिता । वधः । यमं । न । यः । ररे । आर्यं । नाम । दस्यवे ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अहमिंद्रोऽत्कमाच्छादकं शत्रुपुत्रं कवय उशनस ऋषये तस्य सुखार्थं
> हथैर्बहुप्रकारैः प्रहारैस्तत्साधनैरायुधैर्वा शिश्नथं । ताडितवानस्मि । श्नथतिर्वधकर्मा । किंचाहं कुत्समेतन्नामकमृषिमाभिरूतिभिर्वधादिरूपैः । कुत्साय शुष्णमशुषं नि बर्हीः । ऋ. ४-१६-१२ । इत्यादि मंत्रांतरं । रक्षणैरावं । अरक्षं ।
> पूर्वमेव कृतान्यपि रक्षणानीदानीमिव कृतानीत्यनुवदति । कुत्सार्थं शुष्णस्यैतन्नामकस्यासुरस्य श्नथिता हिंसिता । तस्य हननायेत्यर्थः । वधर्वज्रं यमं ।
> प्रहरणाय नियमितवानस्मि । अपि चाहमार्यमार्यसंबंधि । आर्याणां देयमित्यर्थः । तादृशं नाम । उदकनामैतत् । नामकमुदकं दस्यवे शत्रव उपक्षपयित्रे न ररे । न दत्तवानस्मि । यद्वा । आर्यं पूज्यमित्यसाधारणं नाम दस्यवे
> न दत्तवानस्मि । अत्रेंद्रस्यात्मस्तुत् कुत्सरक्षणशुष्णवधादिकं आवः कुत्समिंद्र

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

I, Indra, struck अत्कं (*atkaṃ*), an enemy's son, a "covering" one, for the happiness of the seer Kavi (Uśanas), with many kinds of blows, or with weapons that are the means of them; श्नथति (*śnathati*) is a root meaning killing. Moreover I protected the seer named Kutsa with protections consisting of slaying and the like; another mantra, Ṛ. 4-16-12, says कुत्साय शुष्णमशुषं नि बर्हीः (*kutsāya śuṣṇamaśuṣaṃ ni barhīḥ*). Although those protections were made long ago, they are told as if done just now. For Kutsa's sake I am the smiter of the asura named Śuṣṇa, that is, for his killing; I have held ready the thunderbolt for striking. Further, I did not give to the enemy, the destroyer, the name (water) that belongs to the Āryas, that is, what is to be given to Āryas; नाम (*nāma*) is a word for water. Or else: I did not give to the Dasyu the unshared name आर्यं (*āryaṃ*), "worshipful". Here Indra praises himself; the protection of Kutsa, the slaying of Śuṣṇa and so on are elaborated in many places.

### Printed p. 195

> यस्मिञ्चाकन् । ऋ-१-३३ १४ । शुष्णं पिपुं कुयवं वृत्रमिंद्र । ऋ-१-१०३-८ ।
> इत्यादिषु बहुधा प्रपंचितं । अतोऽत्र ग्रंथविस्तरभयान्न लिख्यते ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The protection of Kutsa and the slaying of Śuṣṇa are elaborated in many ways in Ṛ. 1-33-14 **NOTE** and 1-103-8 and the like; hence it is not written here, for fear of prolixity.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I (Indra)
- अत्कं (*atkaṃ*)—Atka, the enemy's son, who besieged in battle
- कवये (*kavaye*)—for protecting the dwelling of the seer named Uśanas
- हथैः (*hathaiḥ*)—with various weapons or blows, the means of killing
- शिश्नथं (*śiśnathaṃ*)—I have struck or slain
- अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I
- आभिः ऊतिभिः (*ābhiḥ ūtibhiḥ*)—with these protections consisting of destroying enemies
- कुत्सं (*kutsaṃ*)—the seer named Kutsa
- आवं (*āvaṃ*)—I have protected
- अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I
- शुष्णस्य (*śuṣṇasya*)—of the asura named Śuṣṇa
- श्नथिता (*śnathitā*)—I am the destroyer
- अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I
- वधः (*vadhaḥ*)—the thunderbolt
- यमं (*yamaṃ*)—I have held
- आर्यं (*āryaṃ*)—belonging to the Āryas
- नाम (*nāma*)—the rain-water
- दस्यवे (*dasyave*)—to enemy folk
- न ररे (*na rare*)—I do not give

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

I, Indra, struck with many kinds of weapons the enemy Atka who had besieged the dwelling of the seer Uśanas, in order to protect it; with my enemy-destroying protections I protected the seer Kutsa. We have slain the asura Śuṣṇa. I hold the thunderbolt in my hand for killing enemies. The rain-water that I graciously give to the Āryas I do not give to the Dasyu folk, the enemies.

##### English translation
I smote Atka with many weapons for the defence of the sage : with those protections I preserved Kutsa; I am the player of Shushna : I grasped the thunderbolt, I who have not given the water of the Aryas to the Dasyu.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

कवये (*kavaye*)—Although the word kavi is used here, the matter referred to is that of his son, the seer Uśanas. The name of this seer is read in the Ṛgveda in the Ṛks 1-50-10; 1-83-5; 1-121-12; 4-16-2; 4-26-1; 6-20-11; 8-23-17; 9-87-3; 9-97-7; 10-40-7; 1-130-9; 5-31-8; 5-34-2; 8-7-26; 10-22-6. Besides, in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā: अग्निर्देवानां दूत आसीदुशना काव्योऽसुराणां तौ प्रजापतिं प्रश्नमैताग्ं स प्रजापतिरग्निं दूतं वृणीमह इत्यभिपर्यावर्तत ततो देवा अभवन्सरासुराः ॥ (*agnirdevānāṃ dūta āsīduśanā kāvyo 'surāṇāṃ tau prajāpatiṃ praśnamaitāgṃ sa prajāpatiragniṃ dūtaṃ vṛṇīmaha ityabhiparyāvartata tato devā abhavansarāsurāḥ ‖*)

### Printed p. 196
In the past Agni was the messenger of the gods, carrying to the deities addressed the oblations and the like offered by sacrificers at sacrifices. In the same way Uśanas, son of Kavi, was the messenger of the asuras. That being so, Agni and Uśanas both went to Prajāpati and asked which of the two of them was capable of bearing the office of messenger; Prajāpati said, अग्निं दूतं वृणीमहे (*agniṃ dūtaṃ vṛṇīmahe*), "we choose Agni as messenger". Therefore the gods, pleased by Agni's carrying of the oblations, became victorious in all battles, and the asuras were defeated.
This seer is mentioned in Atharvaveda 4-29-6; Pañcaviṃśa Brāhmaṇa 7-5-20, 14-12-5; Jaiminīya Upaniṣad Brāhmaṇa 2-7-2-6; Sāṅkhyāyana Śrauta Sūtra 14-27-1, and other places.
अत्कं (*atkaṃ*)—This word is read only in Ṛgveda Saṃhitā 10-49-3 and 10-99-9. Sāyaṇa explains it as one named Atka, an enemy's son. The Western scholars Roth, Grassmann and Ludwig support this. But Zimmer has expressed the opinion that atka is the metal armour used by warriors in battle and the like, and Pischel that it is an axe.
कुत्सं (*kutsaṃ*)—About Kutsa, Sāyaṇa narrates a story in his commentary on Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-16-10:
रुरुनामकः कश्चिद्राजर्षिः । तस्य पुत्रः कुत्साख्यो राजर्षिरासीत् । स च कदाचित् शत्रुभिः सह युयुत्सुः संग्रामे स्वयमशक्तः सन् शत्रूणां हननार्थमिंद्रस्य आह्वानं चकार । स चेंद्रः कुत्सस्य गृहमागत्य तस्य शत्रून् जघान । तदनंतरमतिप्रीत्या तयोः सख्यमभवत् । संख्य्यानंतरमिंद्र एनमपि स्वकीयं गृहं प्रापयामास । तस्य शची इंद्रं प्राप्तु मागता सती त्वा समानरूपौ दृष्ट्वा अयमिंद्रोऽयं कुत्स इति विवेकाभावेन संशयं चकार । अनयाख्यायिकया प्रतीयमानोऽर्थोऽत्र प्रतिपाद्यते ॥ (*rurunāmakaḥ kaścidrājarṣiḥ | tasya putraḥ kutsākhyo rājarṣirāsīt | sa ca kadācit śatrubhiḥ saha yuyutsuḥ saṃgrāme svayamaśaktaḥ san śatrūṇāṃ hananārthamiṃdrasya āhvānaṃ cakāra | sa ceṃdraḥ kutsasya gṛhamāgatya tasya śatrūn jaghāna | tadanaṃtaramatiprītyā tayoḥ sakhyamabhavat | saṃkhyyānaṃtaramiṃdra enamapi svakīyaṃ gṛhaṃ prāpayāmāsa | tasya śacī iṃdraṃ prāptu māgatā satī tvā samānarūpau dṛṣṭvā ayamiṃdro 'yaṃ kutsa iti vivekābhāvena saṃśayaṃ cakāra | anayākhyāyikayā pratīyamāno 'rtho 'tra pratipādyate ‖*) (Sā. Bhā. 4-16-10)
Formerly there was a royal sage named Ruru. He had a son named Kutsa. At one time he had to fight enemies. Since the enemies were very strong and he alone could not face and conquer them, he sought Indra's help and invoked him. Then Indra came to Kutsa's house and slew his enemies. From this friendship arose between Kutsa and Indra.

### Printed p. 197
Afterwards Indra took Kutsa to his own house. At the door of the house Indra's wife Śacī came to welcome Indra, and seeing two men of the same form as Indra, could not tell which was Indra and which Kutsa, and expressed doubt. This matter is what is indicated in this Ṛk.
Sāyaṇa's explanation shows that two meanings can be given to the word kutsa: first, कुत्सं कर्तनसाधनं (*kutsaṃ kartanasādhanaṃ*), an epithet of the thunderbolt, the instrument of cutting; second, कुत्सं कुत्सनामानमृषिं (*kutsaṃ kutsanāmānamṛṣiṃ*), the seer named Kutsa. If taken as the seer, Indra is praised as having helped Kutsa by slaying the asura Śuṣṇa. In the present Ṛk this is the explanation.
The descriptions in the Ṛksaṃhitā show that the friendship between Kutsa and Indra is very close. The epithet ārjuneya is applied to Kutsa in many places. The word ārjuneya may mean son of the woman Arjunī; or, अर्जुन इतींद्रस्य नाम तस्य पुत्रः (*arjuna itīṃdrasya nāma tasya putraḥ*): Arjuna is a name of Indra, and since Kutsa is his son, the epithet ārjuneya is natural: thus the meaning can be given another way. To support that Indra has the name Arjuna, Sāyaṇa in some places cites the Vājasaneya Saṃhitā mantra एतद्वा इंद्रस्य गुह्यं नाम यदर्जुनः (*etadvā iṃdrasya guhyaṃ nāma yadarjunaḥ*). But in the Ṛks
अहं कुत्समार्जुनेयं न्यृंजेऽहं कविरुशना पश्यता मा । (*ahaṃ kutsamārjuneyaṃ nyṛṃje 'haṃ kaviruśanā paśyatā mā |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-26-1)
दासं यच्छुष्णं कुयवं न्यस्मा अरंधय आर्जुनेयाय शिक्षन् । (*dāsaṃ yacchuṣṇaṃ kuyavaṃ nyasmā araṃdhaya ārjuneyāya śikṣan |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-19-2)
वहत्कुत्समार्जुनेयं शतक्रतुः त्वरद्दंधर्वमस्तृतं । (*vahatkutsamārjuneyaṃ śatakratuḥ tvaraddaṃdharvamastṛtaṃ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-1-11)
Sāyaṇa too explains every ārjuneya there as अर्जुन्याः पुत्रं कुत्सं (*arjunyāḥ putraṃ kutsaṃ*), Kutsa, son of the woman Arjunī. Since other śrutis say that the words Arjuna and Arjunī relate to Indra, the two explanations above may be said to give one meaning. For example:
फल्गुनीष्वग्नी आदधीत । एता ना इंद्रनक्षत्रं यत्फलु न्नोऽप्यस्य प्रतिनाम्योऽप्यर्जुनो ह वै नामेंद्रो यदस्य गुह्यं नामार्जन्यो वै (*phalgunīṣvagnī ādadhīta | etā nā iṃdranakṣatraṃ yatphalu nno 'pyasya pratināmyo 'pyarjuno ha vai nāmeṃdro yadasya guhyaṃ nāmārjanyo vai*)

### Printed p. 198
नामैतास्ता एतत्परोक्षमाचक्षते फल्गुन्य इति को ह्येतस्यार्हति गुह्यं नाम ॥ (*nāmaitāstā etatparokṣamācakṣate phalgunya iti ko hyetasyārhati guhyaṃ nāma ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 2-1-2-11)
इंद्रो भवति यच्च क्षत्रियो यदु च यजमानस्तस्मादाहारिष्टोऽर्जुन इति ॥ ( (*iṃdro bhavati yacca kṣatriyo yadu ca yajamānastasmādāhāriṣṭo 'rjuna iti ‖ (*) (Śa. Brā. 5-4-3-7)
The Phalgunīs are Indra's constellations. There is agreement in the name too: Indra has the name Arjuna, and the Phalgunīs have the name Arjunīs, as the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa and other places say; hence Kutsa may be called in two ways, son of Indra and son of Arjunī.
In many places of the Ṛksaṃhitā the relation of Indra and Kutsa is shown: Kutsa conquers his enemies only with Indra's help.
त्वं शुष्णं वृजने पक्ष आणौ यूने कुत्साय द्युमते सचाहन् । (*tvaṃ śuṣṇaṃ vṛjane pakṣa āṇau yūne kutsāya dyumate sacāhan |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-63-3)
कुत्साय यत्र पुरुहूत वन्वञ्छुष्णमनंतैः परियासि वधैः । (*kutsāya yatra puruhūta vanvañchuṣṇamanaṃtaiḥ pariyāsi vadhaiḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-121-9)
In these Ṛks, and likewise in many other places, it is described that Indra slew Śuṣṇa to help Kutsa. And
अहं पितेव वेतसूरभिष्टये तुग्रं कुत्साय स्मदिभं च रंधयं । (*ahaṃ piteva vetasūrabhiṣṭaye tugraṃ kutsāya smadibhaṃ ca raṃdhayaṃ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-49-4)
Indra himself declares: for Kutsa I defeated all enemies such as Vetasu, Tugra and Smadibha and helped my friend. But
तस्मै कुत्समतिथिग्वमायुं महे राज्ञे यूने अरंधनायः । (*tasmai kutsamatithigvamāyuṃ mahe rājñe yūne araṃdhanāyaḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-53-10)
य आयुं कुत्समतिथिग्वमर्दयो नावृधानो दिवेदिने । (*ya āyuṃ kutsamatithigvamardayo nāvṛdhāno divedine |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-53-10)
In these and other Ṛks it is also described that Indra defeated Kutsa and others. In some Ṛks it is said that Indra slew the asura Śuṣṇa to help Kutsa and scattered and put to flight in battle his soldiers, the asuras called Paṇis, in Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-174-5, 1-175-4 and other Ṛks.

### Printed p. 199
शुष्णं (*śuṣṇaṃ*)—His name is read about 40 times in the Ṛgveda. He is Kutsa's chief enemy. For Kutsa's sake or together with Kutsa, Indra uproots Śuṣṇa (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-16-12, 5-29-9 etc.). He has horns (1-33-12); he has eggs (6-10-10, 11), that is, young born of eggs (10-12-11); from this Śuṣṇa may be called a serpent. Six times he has the epithet अशुष (*aśuṣa*) (swallowing). He has strong forts (1-51-11, 4-30-13); these are moving forts (8-1-28). Indra destroys Śuṣṇa's forts and releases the waters (1-51-11); Indra uproots Śuṣṇa's line and wins the heavenly water (8-40-10). Four times Śuṣṇa has the epithet कुयुव (*kuyuva*) (producer of bad grain). The result of the battle of Indra and Śuṣṇa may be not only the release of waters but the winning of cows or of the sun (8-85-17). When fighting Indra, Śuṣṇa moves about in darkness. From the passages above Śuṣṇa may be called a cause of drought. The word śuṣṇa is derived from the root शुष् (*śuṣ*), meaning to hiss or to dry up; hence this too supports his being a cause of drought.
दस्युं (*dasyuṃ*)—उपक्षपयितारं (*upakṣapayitāraṃ*). Yāska comments on this word: दस्युदर्स्यतेः क्षयार्थादुपदस्यंत्यस्मिन् रसाः उपदासयति कर्माणि (*dasyudarsyateḥ kṣayārthādupadasyaṃtyasmin rasāḥ upadāsayati karmāṇi*) (Ni. 7-23). The word dasyu is derived from the root दसु (*dasu*), meaning to injure or destroy, and is used in the sense that waters are dried up and rites are destroyed. Sāyaṇa gives the meaning "destroyer".
The word dasyu is in many contexts used together with the word अव्रतं (*avrataṃ*). For अव्रतं (*avrataṃ*) the glosses अकर्माणं (*akarmāṇaṃ*) and अननुष्ठायिनं (*ananuṣṭhāyinaṃ*) are given: let Indra destroy the one who does not perform sacrificial rites and destroys everything. There is also a description that Indra should destroy even him who, though fit to perform sacrifice, remains indifferent and does not sacrifice.
Whatever meaning the word deva shows, expansive, illuminating and giving (Ni. 7-15), the word dasyu indicates the opposite of all that: a meaning of decay, darkness and violence. Vṛtra, Śambara and all such asuras belong to this group of dasyus. Since their deeds are to hold back the water so that it does not flow or fall, and to cover the light, Indra is prayed to destroy them and make the water flow and the light spread, and is called by the epithet दस्युहा (*dasyuhā*).

### Printed p. 200
The word दस्यु (*dasyu*) is used in many places as a synonym of rākṣasas and the like, but in some places the word is also used of human beings who are without rites. For example—

त्वं ह नु त्यददमायो दस्यू रेकः कृष्टीरवनोरार्याय । (*tvaṃ ha nu tyadadamāyo dasyū rekaḥ kṛṣṭīravanorāryāya |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-18-3)

In Ṛks of this kind दस्यून् (*dasyūn*) is explained as कर्महीनान् जनान् (*karmahīnān janān*), "people devoid of rites". In this very sense the word दस्यु (*dasyu*) is customarily said to mean the earlier inhabitants of this country who did not perform sacrifices and the like.

कुवित्सस्य प्र हि व्रजं गोमंतं दस्युहा गमत् ॥ (*kuvitsasya pra hi vrajaṃ gomaṃtaṃ dasyuhā gamat ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-45-24)

In many such Ṛks it is told that Indra slays the Dasyus and protects the world. Some hold that the two words Ārya and Dasyu are conventional terms denoting two different groups of people. Historians have discussed the difference between the two groups: Āryas are cultured people who perform sacrifices and the like and worship the gods; Dasyus are uncultured, do not perform sacrifices but obstruct them, have no devotion to the gods, are hostile to the Āryas, and are the dark-skinned earlier inhabitants of this country.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒हं पि॒तेव॑ वेत॒सूर॒भिष्ट॑ये॒ तुग्रं॒ कुत्सा॑य॒ स्मदि॑भं च  
> रंधयं ।  
> अ॒हं भु॑वं॒ यज॑मानस्य रा॒जनि॒ प्र यद्भरे॒ तुज॑ये॒ न प्रि॒याधृषे॑ ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अहं । पिताइव । वेतसून् । अभिष्टये । तुग्रं । कुत्साय । स्मत् इभं । च ।  
> रंधयं ।  
> अहं । भुवं । यजमानस्य । राजनि । प्र । यत् । भरे । तुजये । न । प्रिया ।  
> आधृषे ॥ ४ ॥

### Printed p. 201

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अहमिंद्रः पितेव पिता पुत्रायेव स यथा तस्मै निर्वाहार्थमभिमतं
> प्रदेशं प्रसाढयति तद्वद्वेतसूनेतन्नामकाञ्जनपदानभिष्टयेऽभीच्छते कुत्साय
> महर्षये तुग्रं स्मदिभं च रंधयं। वशमनयं । अहं यजमानस्य राजनि राजनार्थं भुवं । अभवं । समर्थ इति शेषः । यद्यस्मात्तुजये न पुत्रायेव पिता
> तस्मै यजमानायादृषे शत्रुणां धर्षणाय प्रिया प्रियाणि प्र भरे प्रभरामि
> तस्माद्यजमानस्य राजनि भुवमिति संबंधः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Indra says: like a father who sets up for his son the place he wants so that he can manage, so I desired (for Kutsa) the Vetasu peoples, so named; for the great seer Kutsa I brought Tugra and Smadibha under control (रंधयं (*raṃdhayaṃ*) from the root रध्यति (*radhyati*) meaning to bring under control). I have become capable, in the matter of governing, for the sacrificer, because, as a father to a son, I bring pleasing things to that sacrificer for the assailing of his enemies; therefore I have become the ruler of the sacrificer.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I
- पितेव (*piteva*)—as a father desires his son's welfare
- वेतसून् (*vetasūn*)—the people of the nation called Vetasu
- अभिष्टये (*abhiṣṭaye*)—who desires
- कुत्साय (*kutsāya*)—to the sage Kutsa
- तुग्रं (*tugraṃ*)—Tugra
- स्मदिभं च (*smadibhaṃ ca*)—and Smadibha
- रंधयं (*raṃdhayaṃ*)—I subdued
- अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I
- यजमानस्य (*yajamānasya*)—of the sacrificer who performs the sacrifice
- राजनि (*rājani*)—as a lord who desires his welfare
- भुवं (*bhuvaṃ*)—I have become
- यत् (*yat*)—because
- तुजये न (*tujaye na*)—as a father acts toward a son
- आधृषे (*ādhṛṣe*)—for the subduing of enemies
- प्रिया (*priyā*)—pleasing things
- प्र भरे (*pra bhare*)—I provide

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

As a father desires his son's welfare, in order to fulfil the wish of the sage Kutsa I subdued to Kutsa the people called Vetasu, Tugra and Smadibha. I am always the well-wisher and lord of the sacrificer who performs sacrifice, because, as a father behaves lovingly toward his son, I provide him pleasing things for the subduing of enemies.

##### English translation
Like a father (for a son), I brought under subjection to Kutsa, at his desire, the Vetasus and Tugra and Smadibha : I have been in place of a sovereign to the worshipper when I bring (to him) as to a son acceptable (gifts) for assailing (his enemies)

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

वेतसून् (*vetasūn*)—The word वेतसु (*vetasu*) is used in the singular in some places. For example—
स वेतसुं दशमायं दशोणिं तूतुजिमिंद्रः स्वभिष्टिसुम्नः । (*sa vetasuṃ daśamāyaṃ daśoṇiṃ tūtujimiṃdraḥ svabhiṣṭisumnaḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-20-8)

### Printed p. 202
For the word वेतसुं (*vetasuṃ*) in this Ṛk Sāyaṇa explains वेतसुं वेतसुनामानमसुरं (*vetasuṃ vetasunāmānamasuraṃ*), the Asura named Vetasu. In the present Ṛk वेतसून् (*vetasūn*) means the followers of the Asura Vetasu, or the people of that name. Indra's defeat of this Vetasu is indicated in Ṛks such as
धृतस्य धारा अभि चाकशीमि हिरण्ययो वेतसो मध्य आसां । (*dhṛtasya dhārā abhi cākaśīmi hiraṇyayo vetaso madhya āsāṃ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-85-5)
त्वं तुग्रं वेतसवे सचाहन् त्वं तुजिं गृणंतमिंद्र तूतोः । (*tvaṃ tugraṃ vetasave sacāhan tvaṃ tujiṃ gṛṇaṃtamiṃdra tūtoḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-26-4)
**NOTE**
Tugra denotes an Asura of that name. Elsewhere Tugra is said to be a famous king whose son Bhujyu, when sinking in the sea, was saved by the Aśvins. Hence the king Tugra is one person and the Asura Tugra another; there is no room for doubt that they are different persons.

स्मधिभ (*smadhibha*)—In the present Ṛk only his name is mentioned. Since he is named in the group of Asuras, he must be an Asura. Apart from Indra's defeating him, no more facts about him are known.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒हं रं॑धयं॒ मृग॑यं श्रु॒तर्व॑णे॒ यन्माजि॑हीत व॒युना॑  ॒॒
> च॒नानु॒षक् ।  
> अ॒हं वे॒शं न॒म्रमा॒यवे॑करम॒हं सन्या॑य॒ पड्ग॑भिमरंधयं ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अहं । रंधयं । मृगयं । श्रुतर्वणे । यत् । मा । अजिहीत । वयुना । चन ।  
> आनुषक् ।  
> अहं । वेशं । नम्रं । आयवे । अकरं । अहं । सन्याय । पट्गृभिं । अरंधयं ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अहमिंद्रो मृगयमेतन्नामकमसुरं त्रुतर्वण एतन्नामकाय महर्षये रंधयं । रध्यतिर्वशगमने । वशमनयं । यद्यस्माच्छुतर्वा मा माम-

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

I, Indra, brought the Asura named Mṛgaya under the control of the great seer named Śrutarvan (रध्यति (*radhyati*), root meaning to come under control), because Śrutarvan approached me.

### Printed p. 203

> जिहीत ॥ ओहाज् गतौ॥ आगच्छत् ।तदेवोच्यते। वयुना प्रज्ञानेन स्तोत्रेणानुषग_
> नुषक्तोऽभूत् । चनेति पूरणः । अहं वेशमेतन्नामानं नम्रं प्रह्वं करं ।
> अकार्ष० ॥ करोतेर्लुजति कृमृद्यरुहिभ्यश्चंदसीति चेरज् । अडभावश्छांदसः ॥
> अहं सव्यायैतन्नामकाय पड्गृभिमेतन्नामकमरंधयं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

(continuing) He approached with knowledge, with praise, and became devoted (चन (*cana*) is a filler). I made Veśa, so named, humble. The aorist अकार्ष० (*akārṣa0*) is explained through the root करोते (*karote*) with the affix चेरज् (*ceraj*) in the Veda, with Vedic absence of the augment. I brought Paḍgṛbhi, so named, under the control of Savya, so named.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I, Indra
- मृगयं (*mṛgayaṃ*)—the Asura Mṛgaya
- श्रुतर्वणे (*śrutarvaṇe*)—to the sage Śrutarvan
- रंधयं (*raṃdhayaṃ*)—I subdued
- यत् (*yat*)—because that Śrutarvan
- मा (*mā*)—me
- अजिहीत (*ajihīta*)—approached, and
- वयुना (*vayunā*)—with praises and the like
- आनुषक् चन (*ānuṣak cana*)—having praised, became devoted to my worship
- अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I
- वेशं (*veśaṃ*)—Veśa
- नम्रं (*namraṃ*)—humble
- करं (*karaṃ*)—I made
- अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I
- षड्गृभिं (*ṣaḍgṛbhiṃ*)—Ṣaḍgṛbhi
- सव्याय (*savyāya*)—of Savya
- अरंधयं (*araṃdhayaṃ*)—I subdued

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

I subdued Mṛgaya to the seer Śrutarvan, who approached me and worshipped with praises and the like; likewise I made Veśa humble, and I subdued Ṣaḍgṛbhi to Savya.

##### English translation
I have brought Mrigaya under subjection to Shrutarvan ; when he came to me, (he was) earnestly engaged in supplication. I humiliated Vesha for Ayu; I subjugated Padgribhi for Savya.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Śrutarvan and Savya are seers; Mṛgaya, Veśa and Ṣaḍgṛbhi are Asuras. Not many facts about them are known.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒हं स यो नव॑वास्त्वं बृ॒हद्र॑थं॒ सं वृ॒त्रेव॒ दासं॑ वृत्र॒हा  ॑
> रुजं ।  
> यद्व॒र्धयं॑तं प्र॒थयं॑तमानु॒षग्दू॒रे पा॒रे रज॑सो रोच॒  ॑
> नाकरं ॥ ६ ॥
